\eta\circ g\mod\mathbb{Z}
is a constant on
\Omega
and thus
\eta\circ g^{\prime}\mod\mathbb{Z}
is a constant on
\iota^{-1}(\Omega)
.

Let
i
be the largest integer
1\leq i\leq s
such that
\eta|_{G_{i}}
is nontrivial.
Since
\eta\circ g^{\prime}\mod\mathbb{Z}
is a constant on
\iota^{-1}(\Omega)
, we have that

\eta|_{G_{i}}(\Delta_{h_{i}}\dots\Delta_{h_{1}}g^{\prime}(n))=\eta(\Delta_{h_{i}}\dots\Delta_{h_{1}}g^{\prime}(n))=\Delta_{h_{i}}\dots\Delta_{h_{1}}\eta\circ g^{\prime}(n)

is a constant mod
\mathbb{Z}
for all
(n,h_{1},\dots,h_{i})\in\Box_{p,i}(\iota^{-1}(\Omega))
.
Similar to the proof of Lemma 3.7 of
[
9
]
,
\eta|_{G_{i}}
is an
i
-th type-III horizontal character of complexity
O_{C,s}(C^{\prime}\delta^{-K})
. So
g^{\prime}
is not
(O_{C,s}(C^{\prime}\delta^{-K}),\Omega,p)
-irrational and thus
g
is not
(O_{C,s}(C^{\prime}\delta^{-K}),\Omega)
-irrational. We arrive at a contradiction if
O_{C,s}(C^{\prime}\delta^{-K})\leq N
, i.e. if
\delta\geq C^{\prime\prime}N^{-1/K}
for some
C^{\prime\prime}>0
depending only on
C,C^{\prime},K
and
s
.
∎

We now state and prove the factorization property regarding the irrationality polynomials sequences.

Proposition 5.6

(Weak factorization property, the rationality version)
.

Let
d\in\mathbb{N}_{+},s\in\mathbb{N}
with
d\geq s^{2}+s+3
,
C,N>0
,
p
be a prime,

M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a non-degenerate quadratic form,

G/\Gamma
be an
s
-step
\mathbb{N}
-filtered nilmanifold of complexity at most
C
, equipped with a
C
-rational Mal’cev basis
\mathcal{X}
, and
g\in\text{poly}_{p}(V(M)\to G_{\mathbb{N}}|\Gamma)
. If
p\gg_{C,d,N}1
,
then either
g
is
(N,V(M))
-irrational or
there exists a factorization

g(n)=\epsilon g^{\prime}(n)\gamma(n)\text{ for all }n\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d},

where
\epsilon\in G
is of complexity at most 1,

g^{\prime}\in\text{poly}_{p}(V(M)\to G^{\prime}_{\mathbb{N}}|\Gamma^{\prime})

for some sub-nilmanifold
G^{\prime}/\Gamma^{\prime}
of
G/\Gamma
whose Mal’cev basis is
O_{C,d,N}(1)
-rational relative to
\mathcal{X}
and whose total dimension is strictly smaller than that of
G/\Gamma
, and
\gamma\in\text{poly}(V(M)\to G_{\mathbb{N}}|\Gamma)
.

Proof.

The proof uses ideas from Lemma 2.9 of
[
11
]
and the work
[
2
]
. However, in our case, we need some extra effort to obtain the desired periodicity requirements for the sequences
g^{\prime}
and
\gamma
.

There is nothing to prove when
s=0
, and so we assume that
s\geq 1
.
Let
\tilde{M}\colon\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to\mathbb{Z}/p
be a regular lifting of
M
.
We may write
g=\tilde{g}\circ\tau
for some
\tilde{g}\in\text{poly}_{p}(\iota^{-1}(V(M))\to G_{\mathbb{N}}|\Gamma)=\text{poly}_{p}(V_{p}(\tilde{M})\to G_{\mathbb{N}}|\Gamma)
(recall the definition of
V_{p}(\tilde{M})
in Section
1.3
). Suppose that
g
is not
(N,V(M))
-irrational.
Then there exist
1\leq i\leq s
and a nontrivial
i
-th type-II horizontal character
\eta_{i}\colon G_{i}\to\mathbb{R}
of complexity at most
N
such that

\eta_{i}(\Delta_{h_{i}}\dots\Delta_{h_{1}}\tilde{g}(n))\mod\mathbb{Z}

is a constant for all
(n,h_{1},\dots,h_{i})\in\Box_{p,i}(\iota^{-1}(V(M)))=\Box_{p,i}(V_{p}(\tilde{M}))
.
We may use the type-I Taylor expansion to write

\tilde{g}(n)=\prod_{j\in\mathbb{N}^{d},|j|\leq s}g_{j}^{\binom{n}{j}}

for some
g_{j}\in G_{|j|}
.
Denote

g_{-}(n):=\prod_{j\in\mathbb{N}^{d},|j|\leq i-1}g_{j}^{\binom{n}{j}}
,
g_{+}(n):=\prod_{j\in\mathbb{N}^{d},i+1\leq|j|\leq s}g_{j}^{\binom{n}{j}}
and

f(n):=\prod_{j\in\mathbb{N}^{d},|j|=i}g_{j}^{\binom{n}{j}}
.
Then

(5.3)

\tilde{g}=g_{-}fg_{+}.

By Proposition
5.2
(i), we have that

\eta_{i}(\Delta_{h_{i}}\dots\Delta_{h_{1}}\tilde{g}(n))\equiv\Delta_{h_{i}}\dots\Delta_{h_{1}}(\eta_{i}\circ f(n))\mod\mathbb{Z}

is a constant for all
(n,h_{1},\dots,h_{i})\in\Box_{p,i}(V_{p}(\tilde{M}))
. Note that
\eta_{i}\circ f
is a polynomial from
\mathbb{Z}^{d}
to
\mathbb{R}
of degree
i
.

For any
j=(j_{1},\dots,j_{d})\in\mathbb{N}^{d}
with
|j|=i
, pick
1\leq d_{1},\dots,d_{i}\leq d
so that
j_{\ell}
of
d_{1},\dots,d_{i}
equals to
\ell
for all
1\leq\ell\leq d
.
Then for any
n\in V_{p}(\tilde{M})
, we have that
(n,pe_{d_{1}},\dots,pe_{d_{i}})\in\Box_{p,i}(V_{p}(\tilde{M}))
. Since
\tilde{g}\in\text{poly}_{p}(V_{p}(\tilde{M})\to G_{\mathbb{N}}|\Gamma)
, we have
\Delta_{pe_{d_{i}}}\dots\Delta_{pe_{d_{1}}}\tilde{g}(n)\in\Gamma
and so

\begin{split}0\equiv\eta_{i}(\Delta_{pe_{d_{i}}}\dots\Delta_{pe_{d_{1}}}\tilde{g}(n))\equiv\Delta_{pe_{d_{i}}}\dots\Delta_{pe_{d_{1}}}(\eta_{i}\circ f(n))=p^{i}\eta_{i}(g_{j})\mod\mathbb{Z}.\end{split}

Therefore,
\eta_{i}\circ f
takes values in
\mathbb{Z}/p^{s}
.
Since
d\geq s^{2}+s+3
, by Proposition
B.13
, we may write

\eta_{i}\circ f=\frac{1}{q}P_{1}+P_{2}

for some
q\in\mathbb{N},p\nmid q
, some
P_{1}\in\text{poly}(V_{p}(\tilde{M})\to\mathbb{R}|\mathbb{Z})
and some polynomial
P_{2}
of degree at most
i-1
.

Let
\psi\colon G_{i}/G_{i+1}\to\mathbb{R}^{k}
be the natural isomorphism induced by the Mal’cev basis of
G/\Gamma
for some
k\in\mathbb{N}
, and write
\eta_{i}(g):=(m_{1},\dots,m_{k})\cdot\psi(g)
for some
m_{1},\dots,m_{k}\in\mathbb{Z}
with
|m_{1}|+\dots+|m_{k}|\leq N
for all
g\in G_{i}
. Let
c
be the greatest common divisor of
m_{1},\dots,m_{k}
. Since
\eta_{i}
is nontrivial and is of complexity at most
N
, we have that
0<c\leq O_{C,N,s}(1)
. In particular,
c<p
.
We may then pick
u=(u_{1},\dots,u_{k})\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}
such that
(m_{1},\dots,m_{k})\cdot(u_{1},\dots,u_{k})=c
. Let
h_{1},\dots,h_{k}\in G_{i}\cap\Gamma
be such that
\psi(h_{t}\mod G_{i+1})=e_{t}
for all
1\leq t\leq k
.
Denote

\gamma_{0}(n):=\prod_{t=1}^{k}h_{t}^{c^{\ast}u_{t}P_{1}(n)},

where
c^{\ast}
is an integer such that
c^{\ast}cq\equiv 1\mod p^{s}\mathbb{Z}
(which exists since
0<c<p
). Since
P_{1}(n)\in\mathbb{Z}
if
n\in V_{p}(\tilde{M})
, we have that
\gamma_{0}\in\text{poly}(V_{p}(\tilde{M})\to G_{\mathbb{N}}|\Gamma)
. Moreover, for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
, we have

(5.4)

\eta_{i}\circ f-\eta_{i}\circ\gamma_{0}=\frac{1-c^{\ast}cq}{q}P_{1}+P_{2}.

Claim.

For any
i
-th type-III horizontal character
\xi_{i}
, and
j\in\mathbb{N}^{d}
with
|j|=i
, we have that
\xi_{i}(g_{j})\in\mathbb{Z}/p^{s}
.

Indeed, denote
F_{\xi_{i}}(h_{1},\dots,h_{i}):=\xi_{i}(\Delta_{h_{i}}\dots\Delta_{h_{1}}g^{\prime}(n))
. Note that
F_{\xi_{i}}(h_{1},\dots,h_{i})
is independent of the choice of
n
. It is not hard to compute that

(5.5)

F_{\xi_{i}}(h_{1},\dots,h_{i}):=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}^{d},|j|=i}\xi_{i}(g_{j})\sum_{(t_{1},\dots,t_{i})\in J(j)}h_{1,t_{1}}\dots h_{i,t_{i}},

where we write
h_{i^{\prime}}=(h_{i^{\prime},1},\dots,h_{i^{\prime},d})
for
1\leq i^{\prime}\leq i
and
J(j)
is the set of tuples
(t_{1},\dots,t_{i})\in\{1,\dots,d\}^{i}
such that the number of
t_{k}
which equals to
r
is
j_{r}
for
1\leq r\leq d
.
Since
g^{\prime}\in\text{poly}_{p}(V_{p}(\tilde{M})\to G_{\mathbb{N}}|\Gamma)
,
for all
n\in V_{p}(\tilde{M}),
and
h_{1},\dots,h_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
, we have that
\Delta_{ph_{i}}\dots\Delta_{ph_{1}}g^{\prime}(n)\in\Gamma
, which implies that
F_{\xi_{i}}(h_{1},\dots,h_{i})\in\mathbb{Z}/p^{s}
.
By (
5.5
), this implies that

\xi_{i}(g_{j})\in\mathbb{Z}/p^{s}
. This completes the proof of the claim.

By the claim,
\psi(g_{j}^{p^{s}}\mod G_{i+1})\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}
and so we have that

p^{s}\eta_{i}(g_{j})=\eta_{i}(g_{j}^{p^{s}})\in\eta_{i}(G_{i}\cap\Gamma)=c\mathbb{Z}
. Since
1-c^{\ast}cq\in p\mathbb{Z}
, we have
(1-c^{\ast}cq)\eta_{i}(g_{j})\in c\mathbb{Z}
.
Then for all
j\in\mathbb{N}^{d}
with
|j|=i
, there exists
\gamma_{j}\in G_{i}\cap\Gamma
such that
\eta_{i}(\gamma_{j})=(1-c^{\ast}cq)\eta_{i}(g_{j})
.

Note that the leading terms of
\frac{1-c^{\ast}cq}{q}P_{1}
is
\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}^{d},|j|=i}(1-c^{\ast}cq)\eta_{i}(g_{j})\frac{n^{j}}{j!}
.
Writing

\gamma^{\prime}(n):=\prod_{j\in\mathbb{N}^{d},|j|=i}\gamma_{j}^{\binom{n}{j}},

we have that
\gamma^{\prime}
takes values in
\Gamma
and that

\eta_{i}\circ\gamma^{\prime}(n)
has the same leading terms as
\frac{1-c^{\ast}cq}{q}P_{1}(n)
.
So it follows from (
5.4
) that

\eta_{i}\circ f-\eta_{i}\circ\gamma_{0}-\eta_{i}\circ\gamma^{\prime}

is a polynomial of degree at most
i-1
.

Since
\eta_{i}
is nontrivial,
there exists
f^{\prime}\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to G_{\mathbb{N}})
of the form

f^{\prime}(n)=\prod_{j\in\mathbb{N}^{d},|j|\leq i-1}u_{j}^{\binom{n}{j}}

for some
u_{j}\in G_{i}
such that

\eta_{i}\circ f-\eta_{i}\circ\gamma_{0}-\eta_{i}\circ\gamma^{\prime}=\eta_{i}\circ f^{\prime}.

Let
\gamma:=\gamma_{0}\gamma^{\prime}
, and
g^{\prime\prime}:={f^{\prime}}^{-1}f\gamma^{-1}
. We have that
\gamma\in\text{poly}(V_{p}(\tilde{M})\to G_{\mathbb{N}}|\Gamma)
, that
g^{\prime\prime}
takes values in
G_{i}
, and that

\eta_{i}\circ g^{\prime\prime}=0
.
By (
5.3
), we may write

\tilde{g}=g_{-}fg_{+}=g_{-}f^{\prime}g^{\prime\prime}\gamma g_{+},

where
g_{-}(n):=\prod_{j\in\mathbb{N}^{d},|j|\leq i-1}g_{j}^{\binom{n}{j}}
and
g_{+}(n):=\prod_{j\in\mathbb{N}^{d},i+1\leq|j|\leq s}g_{j}^{\binom{n}{j}}
.
By the Baker-Campbell-Hausdorff formula, we may write

\tilde{g}=g_{-}f^{\prime}g^{\prime\prime}g^{\prime}_{+}\gamma

for some
g^{\prime}_{+}
which can be written in the form

g^{\prime}_{+}(n)=\prod_{j\in\mathbb{N}^{d},|j|\leq s}h_{j}^{\binom{n}{j}}

for some
h_{j}\in G_{\max\{|j|,i+1\}}
.

Let
G^{\prime}/\Gamma^{\prime}
be the sub-nilmanifold of
G/\Gamma
given by
\Gamma^{\prime}=\Gamma\cap G^{\prime}
,
G^{\prime}_{j}=G_{j}
for all
j\neq i
and
G^{\prime}_{i}=\ker(\eta_{i})
. Similar to the proof of Lemma 2.9 of
[
9
]
,
G^{\prime}_{\mathbb{N}}
is indeed an
\mathbb{N}
-filtration.
9
9

9

Unlike in the proof Lemma 2.9 of
[
11
]
, our
G^{\prime}_{\mathbb{N}}
is an
\mathbb{N}
-filtration even if
i=1
, since in this paper we allow
G^{\prime}_{0}\neq G^{\prime}_{1}
in the
\mathbb{N}
-filtration
G^{\prime}_{\mathbb{N}}
.
Moreover, the Mal’cev basis of
G^{\prime}/\Gamma^{\prime}
is
O_{C,d,N}(1)
-rational relative to
\mathcal{X}
, and the total dimension of
G^{\prime}/\Gamma^{\prime}
is strictly smaller than that of
G/\Gamma
.

For any
h\in G
, we may write

h=\{h\}[h]

such that
\psi(\{h\})\in[0,1)^{m}
and
[h]\in\Gamma
, where
\psi\colon G\to\mathbb{R}^{m}
is the Mal’cev coordinate map.
Let
\epsilon=\{\tilde{g}(\mathbb{0})\}
,
\tilde{\gamma}=[\tilde{g}(\mathbb{0})]\gamma
and

g^{\prime}=\{\tilde{g}(\mathbb{0})\}^{-1}\tilde{g}\tilde{\gamma}^{-1}=\{\tilde{g}(\mathbb{0})\}^{-1}\tilde{g}\gamma^{-1}[\tilde{g}(\mathbb{0})]^{-1}=\{\tilde{g}(\mathbb{0})\}^{-1}g_{-}f^{\prime}g^{\prime\prime}g^{\prime}_{+}[\tilde{g}(\mathbb{0})]^{-1}.

Then
\tilde{g}=\epsilon g^{\prime}\tilde{\gamma}
and
\tilde{\gamma}\in\text{poly}(V_{p}(\tilde{M})\to G_{\mathbb{N}}|\Gamma)
.
By the constructions, it is not hard to see that

g^{\prime\prime},g^{\prime}_{+},g_{-},f^{\prime}\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to G^{\prime}_{\mathbb{N}})
. By Corollary B.4 of
[
12
]
, we have that
g^{\prime}\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to G^{\prime}_{\mathbb{N}})
. Since

\tilde{g}\in\text{poly}_{p}(V_{p}(\tilde{M})\to G_{\mathbb{N}}|\Gamma)
and
\tilde{\gamma}\in\text{poly}(V_{p}(\tilde{M})\to G_{\mathbb{N}}|\Gamma)
, by Lemma
A.14
, it is not hard to see have that
g^{\prime}
belongs
\text{poly}_{p}(V_{p}(\tilde{M})\to G_{\mathbb{N}}|\Gamma)
and thus belongs
\text{poly}_{p}(V_{p}(\tilde{M})\to G^{\prime}_{\mathbb{N}}|\Gamma^{\prime})
. Since
\iota^{-1}(V(M))=V_{p}(\tilde{M})
, we are done by composing
\tau
on both sides.
∎

By repeatedly using Proposition
5.6
, we get the strong factorization property:

Theorem 5.7

(Strong factorization property, the rationality version)
.

Let
d\in\mathbb{N}_{+},s\in\mathbb{N}
with
d\geq s^{2}+s+3
,
C>0
,
\mathcal{F}\colon\mathbb{R}_{+}\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
be a growth function,
p\gg_{C,d,\mathcal{F}}1
be a prime,
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a non-degenerate quadratic form,

G/\Gamma
be an
s
-step
\mathbb{N}
-filtered nilmanifold of complexity at most
C
, equipped with a
C
-rational Mal’cev basis
\mathcal{X}
, and
g\in\text{poly}_{p}(V(M)\to G_{\mathbb{N}}|\Gamma)
.
Then there exists
C\leq C^{\prime}\leq O_{C,d,\mathcal{F}}(1)

and a factorization

g(n)=\epsilon g^{\prime}(n)\gamma(n)\text{ for all }n\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}

where
\epsilon\in G
if of complexity
O_{C^{\prime}}(1)
,

g^{\prime}\in\text{poly}_{p}(V(M)\to G^{\prime}_{\mathbb{N}}|\Gamma^{\prime})
is
(\mathcal{F}(C^{\prime}),V(M))
-irrational
for some sub-nilmanifold
G^{\prime}/\Gamma^{\prime}
of
G/\Gamma
whose Mal’cev basis is
C^{\prime}
-rational relative to
\mathcal{X}
, and
\gamma\in\text{poly}(V(M)\to G_{\mathbb{N}}|\Gamma)
.

Theorem
5.7
can be derived by repeatedly using Proposition
5.6
. The method is almost identical to the proof of Theorem 11.2 in
[
20
]
. So we omit the proof.

We conclude this section with a property stating that every partially periodic nilsequence on
V(M)
can be represent as a sufficiently irrational one.

Proposition 5.8

(Irrational representation property)
.

Let
d\in\mathbb{N}_{+},s\in\mathbb{N}
with
d\geq s^{2}+s+3
,
C>0
,
\mathcal{F}\colon\mathbb{R}_{+}\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
be a growth function,
p\gg_{C,d,\mathcal{F}}1
be a prime, and
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a non-degenerate quadratic form. Then
there exists
C\leq C^{\prime}\leq O_{C,d,\mathcal{F}}(1)
such that for any function
f\colon V(M)\to\mathbb{C}
of the form

f(n)=F(g(n)\Gamma)\text{ for all }n\in V(M),

where
G/\Gamma
is an
\mathbb{N}
-filtered nilmanifold of degree at most
s
and complexity at most
C
,
g\in\text{poly}_{p}(V(M)\to G_{\mathbb{N}}|\Gamma)
, and
F\in\text{Lip}(G/\Gamma\to\mathbb{C})
with Lipschitz norm at most
C
, we may rewrite
f
as

f(n)=F^{\prime}(g^{\prime}(n)\Gamma^{\prime})\text{ for all }n\in V(M)

for some
\mathbb{N}
-filtered nilmanifold
G^{\prime}/\Gamma^{\prime}
of degree at most
s
and complexity at most
O_{C,C^{\prime}}(1)
, some
(\mathcal{F}(C^{\prime}),V(M))
-irrational
g^{\prime}\in\text{poly}_{p}(V(M)\to G^{\prime}_{\mathbb{N}}|\Gamma^{\prime})
, and some
F^{\prime}\in\text{Lip}(G^{\prime}/\Gamma^{\prime}\to\mathbb{C})
with Lipschitz norm at most
O_{C,C^{\prime}}(1)
.

Proof.

By Theorem
5.7
, there exists
C\leq C^{\prime}\leq O_{C,d,\mathcal{F}}(1)

and a factorization

g(n)=\epsilon g^{\prime}(n)\gamma(n)\text{ for all }n\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}

where
\epsilon\in G
is of complexity
O_{C^{\prime}}(1)
,

g^{\prime}\in\text{poly}_{p}(V(M)\to G^{\prime}_{\mathbb{N}}|\Gamma^{\prime})

is
(\mathcal{F}(C^{\prime}),V(M))
-rational
in some sub-nilmanifold
G^{\prime}/\Gamma^{\prime}
of
G/\Gamma
whose Mal’cev basis is
C^{\prime}
-rational relative to
\mathcal{X}
, and
\gamma\in\text{poly}(V(M)\to G_{\mathbb{N}}|\Gamma)
.
Then
G^{\prime}/\Gamma^{\prime}
is of complexity
O_{C,C^{\prime}}(1)
.
Writing
F^{\prime}=F(\epsilon\cdot)
, we have that
F^{\prime}\colon G^{\prime}/\Gamma^{\prime}\to\mathbb{C}
is a Lipschitz function with Lipschitz norm at most
O_{C,C^{\prime}}(1)
. Moreover, for all
n\in V(M)
, we have that

f(n)=F(\epsilon g^{\prime}(n)\gamma(n)\Gamma)=F^{\prime}(g^{\prime}(n)\Gamma).

∎

Remark 5.9
.

We caution the readers that were unable to extend Proposition
5.8
to
p
-periodic nilsequences, i.e. even if
g
belongs to
\text{poly}_{p}(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to G_{\mathbb{N}}|\Gamma)
, we could not ensure that

g^{\prime}
belongs to
\text{poly}_{p}(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to G^{\prime}_{\mathbb{N}}|\Gamma^{\prime})
in the construction.
This is because our factorization theorem (Theorem
5.7
) applies only for partially
p
-periodic nilsequences (see also Remark 11.3 in
[
20
]
).

5.3.
Structure theorems for spherical Gowers norms

For any
s\in\mathbb{N}
and
C>0
,
it is not hard to see that the sum and product of two
p
-periodic nilsequences of degrees at most
s
and complexities at most
C
is still a
p
-periodic nilsequence of degree at most
s
and complexity at most
O(C)
(see
[
22
]
for definitions). So
combining the spherical Gowers inverse theorem (Theorem 1.2 of
[
22
]
) with an argument similar to Section 2 of the work of Green and Tao
[
9
]
(see also the discussion before Theorem 3.4 of
[
2
]
), we have the following structure theorem (whose proof is omitted):

Theorem 5.10

(Structure theorem for spherical Gowers norms in
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
)
.

For every
s\in\mathbb{N}
, every
d\geq(2s+14)(15s+438)
, every
\epsilon>0
, and every growth function
\mathcal{F}\colon\mathbb{R}_{+}\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
, there exist
C:=C(d,\epsilon,\mathcal{F})>0
and
p_{0}:=p_{0}(d,\epsilon,\mathcal{F})\in\mathbb{N}

such that for every prime
p\geq p_{0}
, every non-degenerate quadratic form
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
, and every
f\colon V(M)\to\mathbb{C}
taking values in
[0,1]
, there exist a decomposition

f=f_{\text{str}}+f_{\text{uni}}+f_{\text{err}}

such that the followings hold:

•

f_{\text{str}}
and
f_{\text{str}}+f_{\text{err}}
take values in
[0,1]
;

•

we may write
f_{\text{str}}
as

f_{\text{str}}(n)=F(g(n)\Gamma)\text{ for all }n\in V(M)

for some
\mathbb{N}
-filtered nilmanifold
G/\Gamma
of degree at most
s
and complexity at most
C
, some
g\in\text{poly}_{p}(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to G_{\mathbb{N}}|\Gamma)
, and some
F\in\text{Lip}(G/\Gamma\to\mathbb{C})
of Lipschitz norm at most
C
;

•

\|f_{\text{uni}}\|_{U^{s+1}(V(M))}\leq 1/\mathcal{F}(C)
;

•

\|f_{\text{err}}\|_{L^{2}(V(M))}\leq\epsilon
.

We may also impose additional irrationality restrictions to the
f_{\text{str}}
part in Theorem
5.10
, and have the following variation of the structure theorem:

Theorem 5.11

(Another structure theorem for spherical Gowers norms in
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
)
.

For every
s\in\mathbb{N}
, every
d\geq(2s+14)(15s+438)
, every

\epsilon>0
, and every growth function
\mathcal{F}\colon\mathbb{R}_{+}\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
, there exist
C:=C(d,\epsilon,\mathcal{F})>0
and
p_{0}:=p_{0}(d,\epsilon,\mathcal{F})\in\mathbb{N}

such that for every prime
p\geq p_{0}
, every non-degenerate quadratic form
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
, and every
f\colon V(M)\to\mathbb{C}
taking values in
[0,1]
, there exist a decomposition

f=f_{\text{str}}+f_{\text{uni}}+f_{\text{err}}

such that the followings hold:

•

f_{\text{str}}
and
f_{\text{str}}+f_{\text{err}}
take values in
[0,1]
;

•

we may write
f_{\text{str}}
as

f_{\text{str}}(n)=F(g(n)\Gamma)\text{ for all }n\in V(M)

for some
\mathbb{N}
-filtered nilmanifold
G/\Gamma
of degree at most
s
and complexity at most
C
, some
(\mathcal{F}(C),V(M))
-irrational
g\in\text{poly}_{p}(V(M)\to G_{\mathbb{N}}|\Gamma)
, and some
F\in\text{Lip}(G/\Gamma\to\mathbb{C})
of Lipschitz norm at most
C
;

•

\|f_{\text{uni}}\|_{U^{s+1}(V(M))}\leq 1/\mathcal{F}(C)
;

•

\|f_{\text{err}}\|_{L^{2}(V(M))}\leq\epsilon
.

Since
\text{poly}_{p}(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to G_{\mathbb{N}}|\Gamma)\subseteq\text{poly}_{p}(V(M)\to G_{\mathbb{N}}|\Gamma)
,
Theorem
5.11
can be proved by first applying Theorem
5.10
for some growth function
\mathcal{F}_{1}
, and then applying Proposition
5.8
for some growth function
\mathcal{F}_{2}
, with
\mathcal{F}_{1}
growing much faster than
\mathcal{F}_{2}
and with
\mathcal{F}_{2}
growing much faster than
\mathcal{F}
. We omit the proof.

Remark 5.12
.

Again we caution the readers that unlike in Theorem
5.10
where
f_{\text{str}}
is
p
-periodic, we can only require
f_{\text{str}}
to be partially
p
-periodic on
V(M)
in Theorem
5.11
. This is because we are only able to obtain the factorization theorem (Theorem
5.7
) for partially
p
-periodic nilsequences, which in turn is insufficient to extend Proposition
5.8
to
p
-periodic nilsequences.

6.
Equisitribution properties for polynomial sequences on product spaces

With the help of
Theorems
4.2
and
5.11
, one can reduce the study of (
4.1
) to the special case when all the
f_{i}
are nilsequences. Therefore, in order to obtain the positivity of (
4.1
), in this section we provide a joint equisitribution property for polynomial sequences on the product space.

6.1.
Parametrization for
\Omega
and its relative self-products

From now on it is convenient to work in the
\mathbb{Z}^{d}
-setting instead of the
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
one.
Recall that for any generating set

I
of
X
, we have generating constants
b_{I,i,j}\in\mathbb{F}_{p},i\in I,0\leq j\leq k+s-1
and generating maps
L_{I,j}\colon(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k+1}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d},0\leq j\leq k+s-1
.
For all
i\in I,0\leq j\leq k+s-1
, let

\tilde{b}_{I,i,j}:=\tau(b_{I,i,j})\in\mathbb{Z}
, and denote

\tilde{b}_{I,i}:=(\tilde{b}_{I,i,0},\dots,\tilde{b}_{I,i,k+s-1})\in\mathbb{Z}^{k+s}
.
Let

\tilde{L}_{I,j}\colon(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{k+1}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
be the linear map given by

\tilde{L}_{I,j}(\mathbb{x}):=\sum_{i\in I}\tilde{b}_{I,i,j}x_{i}\text{ for all }\mathbb{x}=(x_{0},\dots,x_{k})\in(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{k+1},

and
\tilde{\mathbb{L}}_{I}\colon(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{k+1}\to(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{k+s}
be the linear map given by

\tilde{\mathbb{L}}_{I}(\mathbb{x}):=(\tilde{L}_{I,0}(\mathbb{x}),\dots,\tilde{L}_{I,k+s-1}(\mathbb{x}))\text{ for all }\mathbb{x}\in(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{k+1}.

It is not hard to see that
L_{I,j}=\iota\circ\tilde{L}_{I,j}\circ\tau
. In other words,
\frac{1}{p}\tilde{L}_{I,j}
is the regular lifting of
L_{I,j}
.

Let
C_{0}
be the complexity of
X
.
If
p\gg_{C_{0},k,s}1
, then the normalization properties of
b_{I,i,j}
and
L_{I,j}
imply the following
normalization properties
of
\tilde{b}_{I,i,j}
and
\tilde{L}_{I,j}
:

\tilde{b}_{I,i,j}=\delta_{i,j}\text{ for all $i,j\in I$ and}\sum_{i\in I}\tilde{b}_{I,i,j}=1\text{ for all $j\in J$};

\tilde{L}_{I,j}((x_{i})_{i\in I})=x_{j}\text{ for all $j\in I$ and }\tilde{L}_{I,j}(x,\dots,x)=x\text{ for all $j\in J$}.

For
t\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
, we say that
V\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{t}
is a
rational subspace
of
\mathbb{R}^{t}
if there exist
\mathbb{R}
-linearly independent vectors
u_{1},\dots,u_{r}\in\mathbb{Z}^{t}
for some
r\in\mathbb{N}
such that
V
is the
\mathbb{R}
-span of
u_{1},\dots,u_{r}
.
For
i\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
, let
V^{[i]}
denote the linear subspace of
\mathbb{R}^{t}
spanned by
u_{1}\ast\ldots\ast u_{i},u_{1},\dots,u_{i}\in V
, where
\ast
is the coordinate-wise product.

Let
V_{X}:=\tilde{\mathbb{L}}_{I}((\mathbb{R}^{d})^{k+1})
. We summarize some basic properties for
V_{X}
.

Lemma 6.1
.

Assume that the complexity of
X
is at most
C_{0}
and
p\gg_{C_{0},k,s}1
.

(1)

The set
V_{X}
is a well defined rational subspace of
\mathbb{R}^{k+s}
, i.e. it is independent of the choice of the generating set
I
.

(2)

We have that
V_{X}=\text{span}_{\mathbb{R}}\{\tilde{b}_{I,i},i\in I\}
for any generating set
I
of
X
.

(3)

The vectors
(1,\dots,1)
belongs to
V_{X}
. Therefore, we have the
flag property

V_{X}^{[1]}\leq V_{X}^{[2]}\leq\dots.

(4)

The complexities of all of
V_{X}^{[i]},1\leq i\leq s-1
(recall Section
1.3
for definitions) are at most
O_{C_{0},k,s}(1)
.

Proof.

We start with Part (i). It is clear that
V_{X}
is a rational subspace of
\mathbb{R}^{k+s}
. Let
I,I^{\prime}
be generating sets of
X
with generating constants
b_{I,i,j}
and
b_{I^{\prime},i^{\prime},j}
. Recall from (
2.3
)
that

\begin{split}b_{I^{\prime},i^{\prime},j}=\sum_{i\in I}b_{I,i,j}b_{I^{\prime},i^{\prime},i}\end{split}

for all
j\in J
and
i^{\prime}\in I
. Since
p\gg_{C_{0},k,s}1
and all of
b_{I,i,j}
and
b_{I^{\prime},i,j}
are of complexity at most
C_{0}
, we have that

\begin{split}\tilde{b}_{I^{\prime},i^{\prime},j}=\sum_{i\in I}\tilde{b}_{I,i,j}\tilde{b}_{I^{\prime},i^{\prime},i}\end{split}

for all
j\in J
and
i^{\prime}\in I
.
For all
\mathbb{x}=(x_{i^{\prime}})_{i^{\prime}\in I^{\prime}}\in(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{k+1}
. Let
\mathbb{y}:=(\tilde{L}_{I^{\prime},i}(\mathbb{x}))_{i\in I}
. Then for all
j\in J
,

\begin{split}&\quad\tilde{L}_{I^{\prime},j}(\mathbb{x})=\sum_{i^{\prime}\in I^{\prime}}\tilde{b}_{I^{\prime},i^{\prime},j}x_{i^{\prime}}=\sum_{i^{\prime}\in I^{\prime}}\Bigl(\sum_{i\in I}\tilde{b}_{I,i,j}\tilde{b}_{I^{\prime},i^{\prime},i}\Bigr)x_{i^{\prime}}\\
&=\sum_{i\in I}\tilde{b}_{I,i,j}\Bigl(\sum_{i^{\prime}\in I^{\prime}}\tilde{b}_{I^{\prime},i^{\prime},i}x_{i^{\prime}}\Bigr)=\sum_{i\in I}\tilde{b}_{I,i,j}\tilde{L}_{I^{\prime},i}(\mathbb{x})=\tilde{L}_{I,j}(\mathbb{y}).\end{split}

This means that
\tilde{\mathbb{L}}_{I^{\prime}}((\mathbb{R}^{d})^{k+1})\subseteq\tilde{\mathbb{L}}_{I}((\mathbb{R}^{d})^{k+1})
. Similarly,
\tilde{\mathbb{L}}_{I}((\mathbb{R}^{d})^{k+1})\subseteq\tilde{\mathbb{L}}_{I^{\prime}}((\mathbb{R}^{d})^{k+1})
. Thus
\tilde{\mathbb{L}}_{I^{\prime}}((\mathbb{R}^{d})^{k+1})=\tilde{\mathbb{L}}_{I}((\mathbb{R}^{d})^{k+1})
. This completes the proof of Part (i).

Part (ii) follows from the knowledge of linear algebra.
Part (iii) follows from the fact that
(1,\dots,1)=\tilde{b}_{I,0}+\dots+\tilde{b}_{I,k}\in V_{X}
, which follows from the normalization property of
\tilde{b}_{I,i,j}
. Part (iv) is straightforward.
∎

For technical reasons, in additional to (
4.1
), in later sections, we also need to study recurrence results of the form

(6.1)

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}_{(x_{0},\dots,x_{k+s-1})\in\Omega}f_{i}(x_{i})\Bigl|\prod_{0\leq j\leq k+s-1,j\neq i}f_{j}(x_{j})\Bigr|^{2}.\end{split}

This motivates us to study the relative self-products of the set
\Omega
.

Let
B
be a set,
J
be an (ordered) finite index set,
j\in J
,
x_{j}\in B
and

\mathbb{x}^{\prime}=(x_{j^{\prime}})_{j^{\prime}\in J\backslash\{j\}}\in B^{|J|-1}
, write

(x_{j}\downharpoonleft_{j\curvearrowright J}\mathbb{x}^{\prime}):=(x_{j^{\prime}})_{j^{\prime}\in J}\in B^{|J|}
. In other words,
(x_{j}\downharpoonleft_{j\curvearrowright J}\mathbb{x}^{\prime})
is the completion of the
J\backslash\{j\}
-indexed vector
\mathbb{x}^{\prime}
to a
J
-indexed vector by inserting
x_{j}
as the
j
-th coordinate.
When
J=\{0,\dots,k+s-1\}
, we write
(x_{j}\downharpoonleft_{j}\mathbb{x}^{\prime}):=(x_{j}\downharpoonleft_{j\curvearrowright J}\mathbb{x}^{\prime})
for short.

For any
0\leq i\leq k+s-1
, denote

\Omega\times_{i}\Omega:=\{(x,\mathbb{x}^{+},\mathbb{x}^{-})\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})\times(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k+s-1}\times(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k+s-1}\colon(x\downharpoonleft_{j}\mathbb{x}^{+}),(x\downharpoonleft_{j}\mathbb{x}^{-})\in\Omega\}.

It is not hard to see that we may rewrite (
6.1
) as

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}_{(x_{i},\mathbb{x}^{+},\mathbb{x}^{-})\in\Omega\times_{i}\Omega}f_{i}(x_{i})\prod_{0\leq j\leq k+s-1,j\neq i}f_{j}(x^{+}_{j})\overline{f}_{j}(x^{-}_{j}),\end{split}

where
\mathbb{x}^{+}=(x^{+}_{j})_{0\leq j\leq k+s-1,j\neq i}
and
\mathbb{x}^{-}=(x^{-}_{j})_{0\leq j\leq k+s-1,j\neq i}
.

Let
I
be a generating set containing
i
. Define

\Omega_{I}\times_{i}\Omega_{I}:=\{(x,\mathbb{x}^{+},\mathbb{x}^{-})\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})\times(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}\times(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}\colon(x\downharpoonleft_{i\curvearrowright I}\mathbb{x}^{+}),(x\downharpoonleft_{i\curvearrowright I}\mathbb{x}^{-})\in\Omega_{I}\}.

Example 6.2
.

Assume that
I=\{0,\dots,k\}
is a generating set of
X
. Then
\Omega\times_{0}\Omega
is the set of
(x_{0},\dots,x_{2k+2s-2})\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{2k+2s-1}
such that
(x_{0},x_{1},\dots,x_{k+s-1}),(x_{0},x_{k+s},\dots,x_{2k+2s-2})\in\Omega
, and
\Omega_{I}\times_{0}\Omega_{I}
is the set of
(x_{0},\dots,x_{2k})\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{2k+1}
such that
(x_{0},x_{1},\dots,x_{k})
and
(x_{0},x_{k+1},\dots,

x_{2k})
belong to
\Omega_{I}
. For more general sets
\Omega\times_{i}\Omega
and
\Omega_{I}\times_{i}\Omega_{I}
, we have a similar structure except that the notations are more complicated.

Recall from Lemma
3.4
that
\Omega=\mathbb{L}_{I}(\Omega_{I})
for any generating set
I
of
X
. We wish to write the set
\Omega\times_{i}\Omega
in a similar way.
For all
j\in\{0,\dots,k+s-1\}
, let
L^{+}_{i\curvearrowright I,j}\colon(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{2k+1}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
be the linear map given by

L^{+}_{i\curvearrowright I,j}(x,\mathbb{x}^{+},\mathbb{x}^{-}):=L_{I,j}(x\downharpoonleft_{i\curvearrowright I}\mathbb{x}^{+})\text{ for all }(x,\mathbb{x}^{+},\mathbb{x}^{-})\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})\times(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}\times(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k},

and let
L^{-}_{i\curvearrowright I,j}\colon(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{2k+1}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
be the linear map given by

L^{-}_{i\curvearrowright I,j}(x,\mathbb{x}^{+},\mathbb{x}^{-}):=L_{I,j}(x\downharpoonleft_{i\curvearrowright I}\mathbb{x}^{-})\text{ for all }(x,\mathbb{x}^{+},\mathbb{x}^{-})\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})\times(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}\times(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}.

Let

\mathbb{L}_{i\curvearrowright I}^{\pm}\colon(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{2k+1}\to(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{2k+2s-1}
be the linear map given by

\mathbb{L}^{\pm}_{i\curvearrowright I}(\mathbb{x}):=(L^{+}_{i\curvearrowright I,i}(\mathbb{x}),(L^{+}_{i\curvearrowright I,j}(\mathbb{x}))_{j\in\{0,\dots,k+s-1\}\backslash\{i\}},(L^{-}_{i\curvearrowright I,j}(\mathbb{x}))_{j\in\{0,\dots,k+s-1\}\backslash\{i\}})

for all
\mathbb{x}\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{2k+1}
(note that
L^{+}_{i\curvearrowright I,i}(x,\mathbb{x}^{+},\mathbb{x}^{-})=L^{-}_{i\curvearrowright I,i}(x,\mathbb{x}^{+},\mathbb{x}^{-})=x
for all
i\in I
).

For all
i\in I
and
i^{\prime}\in I\backslash\{i\}
,
write

b^{\pm}_{i\curvearrowright I,i}:=(b_{I,i,i},\mathbb{b}^{\prime}_{I,i},\mathbb{b}^{\prime}_{I,i}),b^{+}_{i\curvearrowright I,i^{\prime}}:=(b_{I,i^{\prime},i},\mathbb{b}^{\prime}_{I,i^{\prime}},0,\dots,0)\text{ and }b^{-}_{i\curvearrowright I,i^{\prime}}:=(b_{I,i^{\prime},i},0,\dots,0,\mathbb{b}^{\prime}_{I,i^{\prime}}),

where

\mathbb{b}^{\prime}_{I,i^{\prime\prime}}:=(b_{I,i^{\prime\prime},0},\dots,b_{I,i^{\prime\prime},i-1},b_{I,i^{\prime\prime},i+1},\dots,b_{I,i^{\prime\prime},k+s-1})

for all
i^{\prime\prime}\in I

and
0 appears
k+s-1
times in each expression.
It is not hard to see that

L_{i\curvearrowright I,j}^{+}(x_{i},(x_{i^{\prime}}^{+})_{i^{\prime}\in I\backslash\{i\}},(x_{i^{\prime}}^{-})_{i^{\prime}\in I\backslash\{i\}}):=b^{\pm}_{i\curvearrowright I,i,j}x_{i}+\sum_{i^{\prime}\in I\backslash\{i\}}b^{+}_{i\curvearrowright I,i^{\prime},j}x_{i^{\prime}}^{+}

and

L_{i\curvearrowright I,j}^{-}(x_{i},(x_{i^{\prime}}^{+})_{i^{\prime}\in I\backslash\{i\}},(x_{i^{\prime}}^{-})_{i^{\prime}\in I\backslash\{i\}}):=b^{\pm}_{i\curvearrowright I,i,j}x_{i}+\sum_{i^{\prime}\in I\backslash\{i\}}b^{-}_{i\curvearrowright I,i^{\prime},j}x_{i^{\prime}}^{-}

for all

i\in I
and
0\leq j\leq k+s-1
,
where
b^{\pm}_{i\curvearrowright I,i,j}
is the
j
-th coordinate of
b^{\pm}_{i\curvearrowright I,i}
.
The normalization properties of
b_{I,i,j}
and
L_{I,j}
imply the following
normalization properties
of
L^{\pm}_{i\curvearrowright I,j}
:

\sum_{i^{\prime}\in I}b^{\pm}_{i\curvearrowright I,i^{\prime},j}=1\text{ for all $i\in I,j\in J$ and }L^{\pm}_{i\curvearrowright I,j}(x,\dots,x)=x\text{ for all $i\in I,j\in J$}.

We summarize some properties of the sets
\Omega\times_{i}\Omega
and
\Omega_{I}\times_{i}\Omega_{I}
for later uses.

Lemma 6.3
.

Let
I\subseteq\{0,\dots,k+s-1\}
be a generating set of
X
and
i\in I
.

(1)

We have that
\Omega\times_{i}\Omega=\mathbb{L}_{i\curvearrowright I}^{\pm}(\Omega_{I}\times_{i}\Omega_{I})
.

(2)

If
d\geq 2k^{2}+6k+3,
then
\Omega_{I}\times_{i}\Omega_{I}
is a nice and consistent
M
-set of total co-dimension
k^{2}+3k+1
, and we have that
|\Omega_{I}\times_{i}\Omega_{I}|=p^{d(2k+1)-(k^{2}+3k+1)}(1+O_{k}(p^{-1/2}))
.

(3)

If
d\geq\max\{4k^{2}+12k+7,4k+s+13\}
, then for all
C^{\prime}>0
, there exists
K=O_{C^{\prime},d}(1)
such that
\Omega_{I}\times_{i}\Omega_{I}
admits a partially periodic
(t,Kt^{-K})
-Leibman dichotomy up to step
s
and complexity at most
C^{\prime}
for all
0<t<1/2
.

Proof.

We first prove Part (i). Let
(x,\mathbb{x}^{+},\mathbb{x}^{-})\in\Omega\times_{i}\Omega
.
Since
\Omega=\mathbb{L}_{I}(\Omega_{I})
by Lemma
3.4
(i), we have that
(x\downharpoonleft_{i\curvearrowright I}\mathbb{x}^{+})=\mathbb{L}_{I}(\mathbb{y}^{+}),(x\downharpoonleft_{i\curvearrowright I}\mathbb{x}^{-})=\mathbb{L}_{I}(\mathbb{y}^{-})
for some
\mathbb{y}^{+}=(y_{j}^{+})_{j\in I},\mathbb{y}^{-}=(y_{j}^{-})_{j\in I}\in\Omega_{I}
. Since
i\in I
, we have that
y_{i}^{+}=L_{I,i}(\mathbb{y}^{+})=x=L_{I,i}(\mathbb{y}^{-})=y_{i}^{-}
. Therefore, we have that
(y_{i}^{+},(y_{j}^{+})_{j\in I\backslash\{i\}},(y_{j}^{-})_{j\in I\backslash\{i\}})\in\Omega_{I}\times_{i}\Omega_{I}
. So

(x,\mathbb{x}^{+},\mathbb{x}^{-})=\mathbb{L}_{i\curvearrowright I}^{\pm}(y_{i}^{+},(y_{j}^{+})_{j\in I\backslash\{i\}},(y_{j}^{-})_{j\in I\backslash\{i\}})\in\mathbb{L}_{i\curvearrowright I}^{\pm}(\Omega_{I}\times_{i}\Omega_{I}).

Conversely, let
(x,\mathbb{x}^{+},\mathbb{x}^{-})=\mathbb{L}_{i\curvearrowright I}^{\pm}(y_{i},\mathbb{y}^{+},\mathbb{y}^{-})
for some

(y_{i},\mathbb{y}^{+},\mathbb{y}^{-}):=(y_{i},(y_{j}^{+})_{j\in I\backslash\{i\}},(y_{j}^{-})_{j\in I\backslash\{i\}})\in\Omega_{I}\times_{i}\Omega_{I}.

By definition, we have that
(y_{i}\downharpoonleft_{i\curvearrowright I}\mathbb{y}^{+})
and
(y_{i}\downharpoonleft_{i\curvearrowright I}\mathbb{y}^{-})
belong to
\Omega_{I}
, where we denote
y_{i}^{+}:=y_{i}^{-}:=y_{i}
. Then

\begin{split}&\quad(x\downharpoonleft_{i\curvearrowright J}\mathbb{x}^{+})=(L^{+}_{i\curvearrowright I,i}(y_{i},\mathbb{y}^{+},\mathbb{y}^{-})\downharpoonleft_{i\curvearrowright J}(L^{+}_{i\curvearrowright I,j}(y_{i},\mathbb{y}^{+},\mathbb{y}^{-}))_{j\in J\backslash\{i\}})\\
&=(L^{+}_{i\curvearrowright I,j}(y_{i},\mathbb{y}^{+},\mathbb{y}^{-}))_{j\in J}=(L_{I,j}(y_{i}\downharpoonleft_{i\curvearrowright I}\mathbb{y}^{+}))_{j\in J}=\mathbb{L}_{I}(y_{i}\downharpoonleft_{i\curvearrowright I}\mathbb{y}^{+})\in\mathbb{L}_{I}(\Omega_{I})=\Omega,\end{split}

where the last equality follows from Lemma
3.4
(i).
Similarly,
(x\downharpoonleft_{i\curvearrowright J}\mathbb{x}^{-})\in\Omega
. By definition, this implies that

(x,\mathbb{x}^{+},\mathbb{x}^{-})\in\Omega\times_{i}\Omega
. This complete the proof of Part (i).

We now prove Part (ii).
Note that
\Omega_{I}\times_{i}\Omega_{I}
is the set
\Omega_{4}
in Example B.4 of
[
20
]
(up to a relabelling of the variables).
So
\Omega_{I}
is a nice and consistent
M
-set of total co-dimension
k^{2}+3k+1
.
Since
d\geq 2k^{2}+6k+3,
by Theorem
B.12
,
|\Omega_{I}\times_{i}\Omega_{I}|=p^{d(2k+1)-(k^{2}+3k+1)}(1+O_{k}(p^{-1/2}))
.

Part (iii) follows from Theorem
B.15
and Part (ii).
∎

We now lift the above mentioned notations to the
\mathbb{Z}
-setting.
Recall that

\tilde{b}_{I,i,j}:=\tau(b_{I,i,j})\in\mathbb{Z}
.
For all
i,i^{\prime}\in I
and
j\in J
,
write
\tilde{b}^{\pm}_{i\curvearrowright I,i^{\prime},j}:=\tau(b^{\pm}_{i\curvearrowright I,i^{\prime},j})\in\mathbb{Z}
and
\tilde{b}^{\pm}_{i\curvearrowright I,i^{\prime}}:=\tau(b^{\pm}_{i\curvearrowright I,i^{\prime}})\in\mathbb{Z}^{2k+2s-1}
.
For
i\in I
and
0\leq j\leq k+s-1
,
let
\tilde{L}^{\pm}_{i\curvearrowright I,j}\colon(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{2k+1}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
be the linear linear transformation given by

\tilde{L}_{i\curvearrowright I,j}^{+}(x_{i},(x_{i^{\prime}}^{+})_{i^{\prime}\in I\backslash\{i\}},(x_{i^{\prime}}^{-})_{i^{\prime}\in I\backslash\{i\}}):=\tilde{b}^{\pm}_{i\curvearrowright I,i,j}x_{i}+\sum_{i^{\prime}\in I\backslash\{i\}}\tilde{b}^{+}_{i\curvearrowright I,i^{\prime},j}x_{i^{\prime}}^{+}

and

\tilde{L}_{i\curvearrowright I,j}^{-}(x_{i},(x_{i^{\prime}}^{+})_{i^{\prime}\in I\backslash\{i\}},(x_{i^{\prime}}^{-})_{i^{\prime}\in I\backslash\{i\}}):=\tilde{b}^{\pm}_{i\curvearrowright I,i,j}x_{i}+\sum_{i^{\prime}\in I\backslash\{i\}}\tilde{b}^{-}_{i\curvearrowright I,i^{\prime},j}x_{i^{\prime}}^{-}.

Let
\tilde{\mathbb{L}}^{\pm}_{i\curvearrowright I}\colon(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{2k+1}\to(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{2k+2s-1}
be the linear map given by

\tilde{\mathbb{L}}^{\pm}_{i\curvearrowright I}(\mathbb{x}):=(\tilde{L}^{+}_{i\curvearrowright I,i}(\mathbb{x}),(\tilde{L}^{+}_{i\curvearrowright I,j}(\mathbb{x}))_{j\in\{0,\dots,k+s-1\}\backslash\{i\}},(\tilde{L}^{-}_{i\curvearrowright I,j}(\mathbb{x}))_{j\in\{0,\dots,k+s-1\}\backslash\{i\}})

for all
\mathbb{x}\in(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{2k+1}
.
It is not hard to see that
L^{\pm}_{i\curvearrowright I,j}=\iota\circ\tilde{L}^{\pm}_{i\curvearrowright I,j}\circ\tau
. In other words,
\frac{1}{p}\tilde{L}^{\pm}_{i\curvearrowright I,j}
is the regular lifting of
L^{\pm}_{i\curvearrowright I,j}
.
Let
C_{0}
be the complexity of
X
.
Note that if
p\gg_{C_{0},k,s}1
, then the normalization property of
L^{\pm}_{i\curvearrowright I,j}
imply the following
normalization property
of
\tilde{L}^{\pm}_{i\curvearrowright I,j}
:

\sum_{i^{\prime}\in I}\tilde{b}^{\pm}_{i\curvearrowright I,i^{\prime},j}=1\text{ for all $i\in I,j\in J$ and }\tilde{L}^{\pm}_{i\curvearrowright I,j}(x,\dots,x)=x\text{ for all $i\in I,j\in J$}.

Recall that
V_{X}
is a rational subspace of
\mathbb{R}^{k+s}
defined in Section
6.1
.
For any
0\leq i\leq k+s-1
, denote

V_{X}\times_{i}V_{X}:=\{(x,\mathbb{x}^{+},\mathbb{x}^{-})\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{k+s-1}\times\mathbb{R}^{k+s-1}\colon(x\downharpoonleft_{i}\mathbb{x}^{+}),(x\downharpoonleft_{i}\mathbb{x}^{-})\in V_{X}\}.

Lemma 6.4
.

Assume that the complexity of
X
is at most
C_{0}
and
p\gg_{C_{0},k,s}1
. Let
0\leq i\leq k+s-1
.

(1)

The set
V_{X}\times_{i}V_{X}
is a rational subspace of
\mathbb{R}^{2k+2s-1}
.

(2)

We have that
V_{X}\times_{i}V_{X}=\tilde{\mathbb{L}}^{\pm}_{i\curvearrowright I}((\mathbb{R}^{d})^{2k+1})=\text{span}_{\mathbb{R}}\{\tilde{b}^{\pm}_{i\curvearrowright I,i^{\prime}},i^{\prime}\in I\}
for any generating set
I
of
X
containing
i
.

(3)

For all
j\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
, we have that
(V_{X}\times_{i}V_{X})^{[j]}=V_{X}^{[j]}\times_{i}V_{X}^{[j]}
.

(4)

The vectors
(1,\dots,1)
belongs to
V_{X}\times_{i}V_{X}
. Therefore, we have the
flag property

(V_{X}\times_{i}V_{X})^{[1]}\leq(V_{X}\times_{i}V_{X})^{[2]}\leq\dots.

(5)

The complexities of all of
(V_{X}\times_{i}V_{X})^{[j]},1\leq j\leq s-1
are at most
O_{C_{0},k,s}(1)
.

Proof.

Parts (i), (iii) and (v) are straightforward. Part (iv) follows from the normalization property
\sum_{i^{\prime}\in I}\tilde{b}^{\pm}_{i\curvearrowright I,i^{\prime},j}=1
for all
i\in I,j\in J
.
So it remains to prove Part (ii). The fact that
\tilde{\mathbb{L}}^{\pm}_{i\curvearrowright I}((\mathbb{R}^{d})^{2k+1})=\text{span}_{\mathbb{R}}\{\tilde{b}^{\pm}_{i\curvearrowright I,i^{\prime}},i^{\prime}\in I\}
follows from the the knowledge of linear algebra and the definition of
\tilde{L}_{i\curvearrowright I,j}^{\pm}
. So it remains to show that
V_{X}\times_{i}V_{X}=\text{span}_{\mathbb{R}}\{\tilde{b}^{\pm}_{i\curvearrowright I,i^{\prime}},i^{\prime}\in I\}
.

By definition, for all
i^{\prime}\in I\backslash\{i\}
,
we have that

\tilde{b}^{\pm}_{i\curvearrowright I,i}:=(\tilde{b}_{I,i,i},\tilde{\mathbb{b}}^{\prime}_{I,i},\tilde{\mathbb{b}}^{\prime}_{I,i}),\tilde{b}^{+}_{i\curvearrowright I,i^{\prime}}:=(\tilde{b}_{I,i^{\prime},i},\tilde{\mathbb{b}}^{\prime}_{I,i^{\prime}},0,\dots,0)\text{ and }\tilde{b}^{-}_{i\curvearrowright I,i^{\prime}}:=(\tilde{b}_{I,i^{\prime},i},0,\dots,0,\tilde{\mathbb{b}}^{\prime}_{I,i^{\prime}}),

where

\tilde{\mathbb{b}}^{\prime}_{I,i^{\prime\prime}}:=(\tilde{b}_{I,i^{\prime\prime},0},\dots,\tilde{b}_{I,i^{\prime\prime},i-1},\tilde{b}_{I,i^{\prime\prime},i+1},\dots,\tilde{b}_{I,i^{\prime\prime},k+s-1})

for all
i^{\prime\prime}\in I

and
0 appears
k+s-1
times in each expression. By Lemma
6.1
(ii), we have that
(\tilde{b}_{I,i^{\prime\prime},i}\downharpoonleft_{i}\tilde{\mathbb{b}}^{\prime}_{I,i^{\prime\prime}})=\tilde{b}_{I,i^{\prime\prime}}\in V_{X}
for all
i^{\prime\prime}\in I
. For all
i^{\prime}\in I\backslash\{i\}
, since
\tilde{b}_{I,i^{\prime},i}=0
, we have that

(\tilde{b}_{I,i^{\prime},i}\downharpoonleft_{i}0,\dots,0)=\mathbb{0}\in V_{X}
. From this we can conclude that
\tilde{b}^{\pm}_{i\curvearrowright I,i^{\prime}}\in V_{X}\times_{i}V_{X}
for all
i^{\prime}\in I
. So
\text{span}_{\mathbb{R}}\{\tilde{b}^{\pm}_{i\curvearrowright I,i^{\prime}},i^{\prime}\in I\}\subseteq V_{X}\times_{i}V_{X}
.

Conversely for any
(x,\mathbb{x}^{+},\mathbb{x}^{-})\in V_{X}\times_{i}V_{X}
, since
(x\downharpoonleft_{j}\mathbb{x}^{+}),(x\downharpoonleft_{j}\mathbb{x}^{-})\in V_{x}
, by Lemma
6.1
(ii), we may write

(x\downharpoonleft_{j}\mathbb{x}^{+})=\sum_{i^{\prime}\in I}c^{+}_{i^{\prime}}\tilde{b}_{I,i^{\prime}}\text{ and }(x\downharpoonleft_{j}\mathbb{x}^{-})=\sum_{i^{\prime}\in I}c^{-}_{i^{\prime}}\tilde{b}_{I,i^{\prime}}

for some
c^{\pm}_{i^{\prime}}\in\mathbb{R}
. Since
\tilde{b}_{I,i^{\prime},i}=\delta_{i,i^{\prime}}
for all
i^{\prime}\in I
, projecting to the
i
-th coordinate, we have that
x=c_{i}^{+}=c_{i}^{-}
. Therefore, it is not hard to see that

(x,\mathbb{x}^{+},\mathbb{x}^{-})=x\tilde{b}^{\pm}_{i\curvearrowright I,i}+\sum_{i^{\prime}\in I\backslash\{i\}}\Bigl(c_{i^{\prime}}^{+}\tilde{b}^{+}_{i\curvearrowright I,i^{\prime}}+c_{i^{\prime}}^{-}\tilde{b}^{-}_{i\curvearrowright I,i^{\prime}}\Bigr)\in\text{span}_{\mathbb{R}}\{\tilde{b}^{\pm}_{i\curvearrowright I,i^{\prime}},i^{\prime}\in I\}.

So
V_{X}\times_{i}V_{X}\subseteq\text{span}_{\mathbb{R}}\{\tilde{b}^{\pm}_{i\curvearrowright I,i^{\prime}},i^{\prime}\in I\}
and thus
V_{X}\times_{i}V_{X}=\text{span}_{\mathbb{R}}\{\tilde{b}^{\pm}_{i\curvearrowright I,i^{\prime}},i^{\prime}\in I\}
.
∎

6.2.
The joint equidistribution theorem

We now provide an equidistribution property pertaining to both (
4.1
) and (
6.1
).
We begin with recalling the concept of Leibman group (first studied in
[
15
]
) which plays an important role in
[
9
]
.

Let
t\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
and
G
be a nilpotent group with filtration
(G_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
and
V
be a rational subspace of
\mathbb{R}^{t}
containing
(1,\dots,1)
.
For
g\in G
and
w=(w_{0},\dots,w_{t-1})\in\mathbb{R}^{t}
, denote

g^{w}:=(\exp(w_{0}\log(g)),\dots,\exp(w_{t-1}\log(g)))\in G^{t}.

Let
G^{V}
be the subgroup of
G^{t}
generated by the elements of the form
g^{w}
, where
g
is an iterated commutator of
g_{1},\dots,g_{t}
for some
t\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
g_{j}\in G_{i_{j}}
for some
i_{j}\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
for all
1\leq j\leq t
, and
w=w_{1}\ast\ldots\ast w_{t}
for some
w_{j}\in V^{[i_{j}]}
for all
1\leq j\leq t
, where
\ast
is the coordinate-wise product.
Similar to the argument in
[
9
]
, one can show that
G^{V}
is a normal and rational subgroup of
G^{t}
, and thus
\Gamma^{V}:=\Gamma^{t}\cap G^{V}
is a discrete cocompact subgroup of
G^{V}
.

For
i\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
, let
G^{V}_{(i)}
be the subgroup of
G^{V}
generated by the elements of the form
g^{w}
, where
g
is an iterated commutator of
g_{1},\dots,g_{t}
for some
t\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
i_{1}+\dots+i_{t}\geq i
,
g_{j}\in G_{i_{j}}
for some
i_{j}\geq i
for all
1\leq j\leq t
, and
w=w_{1}\ast\ldots\ast w_{t}
for some
w_{j}\in V^{[i_{j}]}
for all
1\leq j\leq t
.
Set
G_{(0)}^{V}:=G^{V}
.
Since
(1,\dots,1)\in V
,
similar to the proof of Lemma 3.5 of
[
9
]
, one may use the formula (
A.2
) to show that
(G_{(i)}^{V})_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
is a filtration of
G^{V}
.

We are now ready to state our main equidistribution result.

Theorem 6.5

(Joint equidistribution theorem)
.

Let
C_{0},C,\delta>0
. Assume that
X
has complexity at most
C_{0}
and

d\geq\max\{4k^{2}+12k+7,4k+s+13,3k+2s,s^{2}-s+3\}.
11
11

11

Note that this lower bound of
d
is controlled by
d_{0}(X)
defined in (
1.1
).
Let
(G/\Gamma)_{\mathbb{N}}
be an
\mathbb{N}
-filtered nilmanifold of step
s-1
and complexity at most
C
.
There exists
N_{0}:=N_{0}(C_{0},C,\delta,d)>0
such that if
p\gg_{C_{0},C,\delta,d}1
, then for all
N\geq N_{0}
and

(N,V(M-r))
-irrational
g\in\text{poly}_{p}(V(M-r)\to G_{\mathbb{N}}|\Gamma)
,

(1)

the sequence

(g(x_{0})\Gamma,\dots,g(x_{k+s-1})\Gamma)_{(x_{0},\dots,x_{k+s-1})\in\Omega}

is
2\delta
-equidistributed on
G^{V_{X}}/\Gamma^{V_{X}}
;

(2)

the sequence

(g(x_{0})\Gamma,\dots,g(x_{2k+2s-2})\Gamma)_{(x_{0},\dots,x_{2k+2s-2})\in\Omega\times_{j}\Omega}

is
\delta
-equidistributed on
G^{V_{X}\times_{j}V_{X}}/\Gamma^{V_{X}\times_{j}V_{X}}
for all
0\leq j\leq k+s-1
.

The rest of this section is devoted to the proof of Theorem
6.5
.
For convenience, when proving Part (ii) of Theorem
6.5
, we only consider the case
j=0
since the other cases are similar.
By Lemma
2.1
(ii), there exists a generating set
I
of
X
containing 0. We may assume without loss of generality that
I=\{0,\dots,k\}
.

We may write
g=\tilde{g}\circ\tau
for some
\tilde{g}\in\text{poly}_{p}(\iota^{-1}(V(M-r))\to G_{\mathbb{N}}|\Gamma)
, and expand
\tilde{g}
by its type-I Taylor expansion

(6.2)

\tilde{g}(n)=\prod_{j\in\mathbb{N}^{d},0\leq|j|\leq s-1}\tilde{g}_{j}^{\binom{n}{j}}

for some
\tilde{g}_{j}\in G_{j}
. It is convenience to rewrite
\tilde{\mathbb{L}}^{\pm}_{0\curvearrowright I}
as

\mathbb{L}^{\pm}_{0\curvearrowright I}(\mathbb{x}):=(L^{\prime}_{0}(\mathbb{x}),\dots,L^{\prime}_{2k+2s-2}(\mathbb{x}))

for all
\mathbb{x}\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{2k+1}
, and to
rewrite
\tilde{\mathbb{L}}^{\pm}_{0\curvearrowright I}
as

\tilde{\mathbb{L}}^{\pm}_{0\curvearrowright I}(\mathbb{x}):=(\tilde{L}^{\prime}_{0}(\mathbb{x}),\dots,\tilde{L}^{\prime}_{2k+2s-2}(\mathbb{x}))

for all
\mathbb{x}\in(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{2k+1}
.
Then by (
6.2
),

(6.3)

\tilde{g}^{\otimes(2k+2s-1)}\circ\tilde{\mathbb{L}}^{\pm}_{0\curvearrowright I}(\mathbb{x})=\prod_{j\in\mathbb{N}^{d},0\leq|j|\leq s-1}\Bigl(\tilde{g}_{j}^{\binom{\tilde{L}^{\prime}_{0}(\mathbb{x})}{j}},\dots,\tilde{g}_{j}^{\binom{\tilde{L}^{\prime}_{2k+2s-2}(\mathbb{x})}{j}}\Bigr)

for all
\mathbb{x}\in(\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{2k+1}
. We begin with some properties for the polynomial sequence described in (
6.3
).

Lemma 6.6
.

We have that

\tilde{g}^{\otimes(2k+2s-1)}\circ\tilde{\mathbb{L}}^{\pm}_{0\curvearrowright I}\in\text{poly}_{p}(\iota^{-1}(\Omega_{I}\times_{0}\Omega_{I})\to G^{2k+2s-1}_{\mathbb{N}}|\Gamma^{2k+2s-1}).

Moreover, we have that

\tilde{g}^{\otimes(2k+2s-1)}\circ\tilde{\mathbb{L}}^{\pm}_{0\curvearrowright I}(\tau(\mathbb{x}))\Gamma^{2k+2s-1}=g^{\otimes(2k+2s-1)}\circ\mathbb{L}^{\pm}_{0\curvearrowright I}(\mathbb{x})\Gamma^{2k+2s-1}

for all
\mathbb{x}\in\Omega_{I}\times_{0}\Omega_{I}
.

Proof.

Using the flag property of
V_{X}\times_{0}V_{X}
and expanding the linear transformations
\tilde{L}^{\prime}_{j}
, it is not hard to see that the map
\mathbb{x}\mapsto\Bigl(\tilde{g}_{j}^{\binom{\tilde{L}^{\prime}_{0}(\mathbb{x})}{j}},\dots,\tilde{g}_{j}^{\binom{\tilde{L}^{\prime}_{2k+2s-2}(\mathbb{x})}{j}}\Bigr)
belongs to
\text{poly}((\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{2k+1}\to G_{\mathbb{N}}^{V_{X}\times_{0}V_{X}})
for all
j\in\mathbb{N}^{d}
. So by Corollary B.4 of
[
12
]
and (
6.3
),
\tilde{g}^{\otimes(2k+2s-1)}\circ\tilde{\mathbb{L}}^{\pm}_{0\curvearrowright I}
also belongs to
\text{poly}((\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{2k+1}\to G_{\mathbb{N}}^{V_{X}\times_{0}V_{X}})
.

Now fix
0\leq i\leq 2k+2s-1
,
\mathbb{x}\in\iota^{-1}(\Omega_{I}\times_{0}\Omega_{I})
and
\mathbb{y}\in(\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{2k+1}
.
Since
\Omega\times_{0}\Omega\subseteq V(M-r)^{2k+2s-1}
, it follows from Lemma
6.3
(i) that
L^{\prime}_{i}(\Omega_{I}\times_{0}\Omega_{I})\subseteq V(M-r)
.
Since
\tilde{L}^{\prime}_{i}\circ\tau\equiv\tau\circ L^{\prime}_{i}\mod p(\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{2k+1}
, we have that

\tilde{L}^{\prime}_{i}(\mathbb{x})\in\tilde{L}^{\prime}_{i}(\iota^{-1}(\Omega_{I}\times_{0}\Omega_{I}))\subseteq\iota^{-1}\circ L^{\prime}_{i}(\Omega_{I}\times_{0}\Omega_{I})\subseteq\iota^{-1}(V(M-r)).

Since

\tilde{L}^{\prime}_{i}(\mathbb{y})\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
and
\tilde{g}\in\text{poly}_{p}(\iota^{-1}(V(M-r))\to G_{\mathbb{N}}|\Gamma)
, we have that

\tilde{g}(\tilde{L}^{\prime}_{i}(\mathbb{x}+p\mathbb{y}))^{-1}\tilde{g}(\tilde{L}^{\prime}_{i}(\mathbb{x}))=\tilde{g}(\tilde{L}^{\prime}_{i}(\mathbb{x})+p\tilde{L}^{\prime}_{i}(\mathbb{y}))^{-1}\tilde{g}(\tilde{L}^{\prime}_{i}(\mathbb{x}))\in\Gamma.

Therefore,

\tilde{g}^{\otimes(2k+2s-1)}(\tilde{\mathbb{L}}^{\pm}_{0\curvearrowright I}(\mathbb{x}+p\mathbb{y}))^{-1}\tilde{g}^{\otimes(2k+2s-1)}(\tilde{\mathbb{L}}^{\pm}_{0\curvearrowright I}(\mathbb{x}))\in\Gamma^{2k+2s-1}.

This implies that
\tilde{g}^{\otimes(2k+2s-1)}\circ\tilde{\mathbb{L}}^{\pm}_{0\curvearrowright I}
belongs to
\text{poly}_{p}(\iota^{-1}(\Omega_{I}\times_{0}\Omega_{I})\to G^{2k+2s-1}_{\mathbb{N}}|\Gamma^{2k+2s-1})
. Since we have shown that
\tilde{g}^{\otimes(2k+2s-1)}\circ\tilde{\mathbb{L}}^{\pm}_{0\curvearrowright I}\in\text{poly}((\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{2k+1}\to G_{\mathbb{N}}^{V_{X}\times_{0}V_{X}})
, this implies that
\tilde{g}^{\otimes(2k+2s-1)}\circ\tilde{\mathbb{L}}^{\pm}_{0\curvearrowright I}
belongs to

\text{poly}_{p}(\iota^{-1}(\Omega_{I}\times_{0}\Omega_{I})\to G_{\mathbb{N}}^{V_{X}\times_{0}V_{X}}|\Gamma^{V_{X}\times_{0}V_{X}})\subseteq\text{poly}_{p}(\iota^{-1}(\Omega_{I}\times_{0}\Omega_{I})\to G^{2k+2s-1}_{\mathbb{N}}|\Gamma^{2k+2s-1}).

Now for any
0\leq i\leq 2k+2s-1
and
\mathbb{x}\in\Omega_{I}\times_{0}\Omega_{I}
, since

\tilde{L}^{\prime}_{i}\circ\tau(\mathbb{x})\equiv\tau\circ L^{\prime}_{i}(\mathbb{x})\mod p(\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{2k+1}
,
\tilde{g}\in\text{poly}_{p}(\iota^{-1}(V(M-r))\to G_{\mathbb{N}}|\Gamma)
and
\tau\circ L^{\prime}_{i}(\mathbb{x})\in\tau(L^{\prime}_{i}(\Omega_{I}\times_{0}\Omega_{I}))\subseteq\tau(V(M-r))
(as we have shown earlier in the proof), we have that

\tilde{g}\circ\tilde{L}^{\prime}_{i}\circ\tau(\mathbb{x})\Gamma=\tilde{g}\circ\tau\circ\tilde{L}_{i}(\mathbb{x})\Gamma=g\circ\tilde{L}_{i}(\mathbb{x})\Gamma.

This implies that

\tilde{g}^{\otimes(2k+2s-1)}\circ\tilde{\mathbb{L}}^{\pm}_{0\curvearrowright I}(\tau(\mathbb{x}))\Gamma^{2k+2s-1}=g^{\otimes(2k+2s-1)}\circ\mathbb{L}^{\pm}_{0\curvearrowright I}(\mathbb{x})\Gamma^{2k+2s-1}

for all
\mathbb{x}\in\Omega_{I}\times_{0}\Omega_{I}
. ∎

Assume that the sequence

(g(x_{0})\Gamma,\dots,g(x_{2k+2s-2})\Gamma)_{(x_{0},\dots,x_{2k+2s-2})\in\Omega\times_{0}\Omega}

is not
\delta
-equidistributed on
G^{V_{X}\times_{0}V_{X}}/\Gamma^{V_{X}\times_{0}V_{X}}
. Then by
Lemma
6.3
(i), the sequence

(g^{\otimes(2k+2s-1)}\circ\mathbb{L}^{\pm}_{0\curvearrowright I}(\mathbb{x})\Gamma^{2k+2s-1})_{\mathbb{x}\in\Omega_{I}\times_{0}\Omega_{I}}

is not
\delta
-equidistributed on
G^{V_{X}\times_{0}V_{X}}/\Gamma^{V_{X}\times_{0}V_{X}}
. By Lemma
6.6
,
the sequence

(\tilde{g}^{\otimes(2k+2s-1)}\circ\tilde{\mathbb{L}}^{\pm}_{0\curvearrowright I}(\mathbb{x})\Gamma^{2k+2s-1})_{\mathbb{x}\in\tau(\Omega_{I}\times_{0}\Omega_{I})}

is not
\delta
-equidistributed on
G^{V_{X}\times_{0}V_{X}}/\Gamma^{V_{X}\times_{0}V_{X}}
, or equivalently, the sequence

(\tilde{g}^{\otimes(2k+2s-1)}\circ\tilde{\mathbb{L}}^{\pm}_{0\curvearrowright I}\circ\tau(\mathbb{x})\Gamma^{2k+2s-1})_{\mathbb{x}\in\Omega_{I}\times_{0}\Omega_{I}}

is not
\delta
-equidistributed on
G^{V_{X}\times_{0}V_{X}}/\Gamma^{V_{X}\times_{0}V_{X}}
. Similar to Lemma 3.7 of
[
9
]
, our strategy is to obtain a contradiction by showing that
g
is not sufficiently irrational via the construction of some suitable type-III horizontal character. However, our case is more complicated, and so we need to use some tools developed in the previous part of the series
[
20
]
.

By Lemma
6.6
, we have that

\tilde{g}^{\otimes(2k+2s-1)}\circ\tilde{\mathbb{L}}^{\pm}_{0\curvearrowright I}\in\text{poly}_{p}(\iota^{-1}(\Omega_{I}\times_{0}\Omega_{I})\to G^{2k+2s-1}_{\mathbb{N}}|\Gamma^{2k+2s-1})
and thus by definition
\tilde{g}^{\otimes(2k+2s-1)}\circ\tilde{\mathbb{L}}^{\pm}_{0\curvearrowright I}\circ\tau\in\text{poly}_{p}(\Omega_{I}\times_{0}\Omega_{I}\to G^{2k+2s-1}_{\mathbb{N}}|\Gamma^{2k+2s-1})
. By Lemma
6.1
(iv) and Lemma
6.4
(v),
the complexities of
G^{V_{X}}/\Gamma^{V_{X}}
and
G^{V_{X}\times_{0}V_{X}}/\Gamma^{V_{X}\times_{0}V_{X}}
are bounded by some
C^{\prime}=O_{C_{0},C,k,s}(1)
. Finally, since
d\geq\max\{4k^{2}+12k+7,4k+s+13\}
, it follows from Lemma
6.3
(iii) that

\Omega_{I}\times_{0}\Omega_{I}
admits a partially periodic
(\delta,O_{C_{0},C,\delta,d}(1))
-Leibman dichotomy up to step
s
and complexity at most
C^{\prime}
for all
0<\delta<1/2
.
So there exists a nontrivial type-I horizontal character
\eta\colon G^{V_{X}\times_{0}V_{X}}\to\mathbb{R}
of complexity at most
O_{C_{0},C,\delta,d}(1)
such that

\eta\circ\tilde{g}^{\otimes(2k+2s-1)}\circ\tilde{\mathbb{L}}^{\pm}_{0\curvearrowright I}\circ\tau\mod\mathbb{Z}

is a constant on
\Omega_{I}\times_{0}\Omega_{I}
, or equivalently,

\eta\circ\tilde{g}^{\otimes(2k+2s-1)}\circ\tilde{\mathbb{L}}^{\pm}_{0\curvearrowright I}\mod\mathbb{Z}

is a constant on
\tau(\Omega_{I}\times_{0}\Omega_{I})
. Since
\tilde{g}^{\otimes(2k+2s-1)}\circ\tilde{\mathbb{L}}^{\pm}_{0\curvearrowright I}\in\text{poly}_{p}(\tau(\Omega_{I}\times_{0}\Omega_{I})\to G^{2k+2s-1}_{\mathbb{N}}|\Gamma^{2k+2s-1})
, this implies that
\eta\circ\tilde{g}^{\otimes(2k+2s-1)}\circ\tilde{\mathbb{L}}^{\pm}_{0\curvearrowright I}\mod\mathbb{Z}

is a constant on
\iota^{-1}(\Omega_{I}\times_{0}\Omega_{I})
.

Denote

P:=\eta\circ\tilde{g}^{\otimes(2k+2s-1)}\circ\tilde{\mathbb{L}}^{\pm}_{0\curvearrowright I}.

By (
6.3
),

P(\mathbb{x})=\eta\circ\tilde{g}^{\otimes(2k+2s-1)}\circ\tilde{\mathbb{L}}^{\pm}_{0\curvearrowright I}(\mathbb{x})=\prod_{j\in\mathbb{N}^{d},0\leq|j|\leq s-1}\eta\Bigl(\tilde{g}_{j}^{\binom{\tilde{L}^{\prime}_{0}(\mathbb{x})}{j}},\dots,\tilde{g}_{j}^{\binom{\tilde{L}^{\prime}_{2k+2s-2}(\mathbb{x})}{j}}\Bigr)

for all
\mathbb{x}\in(\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{2k+1}
.
Let
1\leq i\leq s-1
be the largest integer such that
\eta|_{G_{(i)}^{V_{X}\times_{0}V_{X}}}
is nontrivial. Then we may rewrite
P
as

(6.4)

P(\mathbb{x})=\prod_{j\in\mathbb{N}^{d},0\leq|j|\leq i}\eta\Bigl(\tilde{g}_{j}^{\binom{\tilde{L}^{\prime}_{0}(\mathbb{x})}{j}},\dots,\tilde{g}_{j}^{\binom{\tilde{L}^{\prime}_{2k+2s-2}(\mathbb{x})}{j}}\Bigr).

In order make use of the fact that
P\mod\mathbb{Z}
is a constant on
\iota^{-1}(\Omega_{I}\times_{0}\Omega_{I})
, we compute the partial derivatives of
P
.
For any

\mathbb{a}=(a_{0},\dots,a_{2k})\in\mathbb{N}^{2k+1}
with
a_{0}+\dots+a_{2k}=i
, let
W(\mathbb{a})
denote the set of all

(\mathbb{x},(h_{t,t^{\prime}})_{0\leq t\leq 2k,1\leq t^{\prime}\leq a_{t}})\in(\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{2k+i+1},\mathbb{x}=(x_{0},\dots,x_{2k})\in(\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{2k+1},h_{t,t^{\prime}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
such that for all
\epsilon_{t,t^{\prime}}\in\{0,1\}
,

0\leq t\leq 2k
,
1\leq t^{\prime}\leq a_{t}
, we have

\Bigl(x_{0}+\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{a_{0}}\epsilon_{0,t^{\prime}}h_{0,t^{\prime}},\dots,x_{2k}+\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{a_{2k}}\epsilon_{2k,t^{\prime}}h_{2k,t^{\prime}}\Bigr)\in\iota^{-1}(\Omega_{I}\times_{0}\Omega_{I}).

We first show that the “projection” of the set
W(\mathbb{a})
to the
h_{t,t^{\prime}}
coordinates has a nice algebraic description. With a slight abuse of notation, we use
A
to denote the matrix associated to both
M-r
and its regular lifting.

Lemma 6.7
.

If
d\geq 3k+2s
, then
for any
h_{t,t^{\prime}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d},0\leq t\leq 2k,1\leq t^{\prime}\leq a_{t}
which are
p
-linearly independent (recall Section
1.3
for the definition) and pairwise
(M,p)
-orthogonal (meaning that
p|(h_{t,t^{\prime}}A)\cdot h_{\tilde{t},\tilde{t}^{\prime}}
for
(t,t^{\prime})\neq(\tilde{t},\tilde{t}^{\prime})
), there exists
\mathbb{x}\in(\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{2k+1}
such that
(\mathbb{x},(h_{t,t^{\prime}})_{0\leq t\leq 2k,1\leq t^{\prime}\leq a_{t}})
belongs to
W(\mathbb{a})
.

Proof.

It is more convenient to prove Lemma
6.7
under the
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
-setting. Indeed,
it suffices to show that for any
h_{t,t^{\prime}}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d},0\leq t\leq 2k,1\leq t^{\prime}\leq a_{t}
which are linearly independent and pairwise
M
-orthogonal (meaning that
(h_{t,t^{\prime}}A)\cdot h_{\tilde{t},\tilde{t}^{\prime}}=0
for
(t,t^{\prime})\neq(\tilde{t},\tilde{t}^{\prime})
), there exists
\mathbb{y}\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k+1}
such that
(\mathbb{y},\mathbb{h})
belongs to
\iota(W(\mathbb{a}))
, where
\mathbb{h}:=(h_{t,t^{\prime}})_{0\leq t\leq 2k,1\leq t^{\prime}\leq a_{t}}
. Write
\mathbb{y}=(y_{0},\dots,y_{k})
for some
y_{0},\dots,y_{k}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
.
Note that
(\mathbb{y},\mathbb{h})
belongs to
\iota(W(\mathbb{a}))
if and only if
for all
\epsilon_{t,t^{\prime}}\in\{0,1\}
,

0\leq t\leq 2k
,
1\leq t^{\prime}\leq a_{t}
, writing

\mathbb{w}:=(w_{0},\dots,w_{2k}):=\Bigl(y_{0}+\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{a_{0}}\epsilon_{0,t^{\prime}}h_{0,t^{\prime}},\dots,y_{2k}+\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{a_{2k}}\epsilon_{2k,t^{\prime}}h_{2k,t^{\prime}}\Bigr)

we have that
\mathbb{w}\in\Omega_{I}\times_{0}\Omega_{I}
. Recall that
\Omega_{I}\times_{0}\Omega_{I}
is the set
\Omega_{4}
in Example B.4 of
[
20
]
,
M
is homogeneous and
X=\{v_{0},\dots,v_{k+s-1}\}
. So
(\mathbb{y},\mathbb{h})\in\iota(W(\mathbb{a}))
if and only if for all the above mentioned
\mathbb{w}
, we have that

•

M(w_{j})=r
for all
0\leq j\leq 2k
;

•

M(w_{0}-w_{j})=M(w_{0}-w_{k+j})=M(v_{0}-v_{j})
for all
1\leq j\leq k
;

•

M(w_{j}-w_{j^{\prime}})=M(w_{k+j}-w_{k+j^{\prime}})=M(v_{j}-v_{j^{\prime}})
for all
1\leq j,j^{\prime}\leq k
.

This is equivalent of saying that

(w_{j}A)\cdot w_{j^{\prime}}=c_{j,j^{\prime}}

for all
(j,j^{\prime})\in\{0,\dots,k\}^{2}\cup\{0,k+1,\dots,2k\}^{2}
,
where
c_{j,j^{\prime}}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}
are some constants depending only on
v_{0},\dots,v_{k}
with
c_{j,j^{\prime}}=c_{j^{\prime},j}
and
c_{j,j}=r
(since
M
is homogeneous).
By Lemma
B.1
, this implies that

(h_{t,v}A)\cdot h_{t^{\prime},v^{\prime}}=0
for all
0\leq t,t^{\prime}\leq k
,
1\leq v\leq a_{t}
,
1\leq v^{\prime}\leq a_{t^{\prime}}
with
(t,v)\neq(t^{\prime},v^{\prime})
.
It is then not hard to compute that
(\mathbb{y},\mathbb{h})
belongs to
\iota(W(\mathbb{a}))
if and only if

•

(y_{t}A)\cdot y_{t^{\prime}}=c_{t,t^{\prime}}

for all
(t,t^{\prime})\in\{0,\dots,k\}^{2}\cup\{0,k+1,\dots,2k\}^{2}
;

•

(h_{t,v}A)\cdot h_{t^{\prime},v^{\prime}}=0
for all
(t,t^{\prime})\in\{0,\dots,k\}^{2}\cup\{0,k+1,\dots,2k\}^{2}
,
1\leq v\leq a_{t}
,
1\leq v^{\prime}\leq a_{t^{\prime}}
with
(t,v)\neq(t^{\prime},v^{\prime})
;

•

(y_{t}A)\cdot h_{t^{\prime},v^{\prime}}=0
for all
(t,t^{\prime})\in\{0,\dots,k\}^{2}\cup\{0,k+1,\dots,2k\}^{2}
,
1\leq v^{\prime}\leq a_{t^{\prime}}
with
t\neq t^{\prime}
;

•

2(y_{t}A)\cdot h_{t,v}+(h_{t,v}A)\cdot h_{t,v}=0
for all
0\leq t\leq 2k
,
1\leq v\leq a_{t}
.

Let
0\leq j\leq 2k

and suppose that we have constructed
y_{0},\dots,y_{j-1}
such that all the above mentioned conditions which involve
y_{0},\dots,y_{j-1}
hold, and that
y_{j^{\prime}},h_{t,t^{\prime}},0\leq j^{\prime}\leq j-1,0\leq t\leq 2k,1\leq t^{\prime}\leq a_{t}
are linearly independent (in the special case
j=0
, there is nothing to construct). We now construct
y_{j}
.
Let
W_{j}
be the set of
y\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
such that

•

(y_{j}A)\cdot y_{j^{\prime}}=c_{j,j^{\prime}}

for all
0\leq j^{\prime}\leq j-1
with
(j,j^{\prime})\in\{0,\dots,k\}^{2}\cup\{0,k+1,\dots,2k\}^{2}
;

•

(y_{j}A)\cdot h_{j^{\prime},v^{\prime}}=0
for all
j^{\prime}\neq j
with
(j,j^{\prime})\in\{0,\dots,k\}^{2}\cup\{0,k+1,\dots,2k\}^{2}
and
1\leq v^{\prime}\leq a_{j^{\prime}}
;

•

2(y_{j}A)\cdot h_{j,v}+(h_{j,v}A)\cdot h_{j,v}=0
for all
1\leq v\leq a_{j}
.

To complete the induction, we need to find some
y_{j}\in V(M-r)\cap W_{j}
such that
y_{j^{\prime}},h_{t,t^{\prime}},0\leq j^{\prime}\leq j,0\leq t\leq 2k,1\leq t^{\prime}\leq a_{t}
are linearly independent.

Since
y_{j^{\prime}},h_{t,t^{\prime}},0\leq j^{\prime}\leq j-1,0\leq t\leq 2k,1\leq t^{\prime}\leq a_{t}
are linearly independent and
A
is invertible,
W_{j}
is an affine subspace of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
of co-dimension at most
k+\sum_{t=0}^{2k}a_{t}\leq k+s-1
. By Proposition
B.2
,
\text{rank}(M|_{W_{j}})\geq d-2(k+s-1)
. Since
d\geq 2k+2s+1
, by Lemma
B.5
,
|V(M-r)\cap W_{j}|\geq p^{d-k-s}/2
if
p\gg_{d}1
.
On the other hand, the number of
y_{j}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
such that
y_{j^{\prime}},h_{t,t^{\prime}},0\leq j^{\prime}\leq j,0\leq t\leq 2k,1\leq t^{\prime}\leq a_{t}
are linearly dependent is at most
p^{2k+s-1}
. Since
d-k-s\geq 2k+s
, there exists at least one
y_{j}
in
V(M-r)\cap W_{j}
such that
y_{j^{\prime}},h_{t,t^{\prime}},0\leq j^{\prime}\leq j,0\leq t\leq 2k,1\leq t^{\prime}\leq a_{t}
are linearly independent. This completes the construction of
y_{j}
.
The claim follows by constructing
y_{0},\dots,y_{2k}
inductively as described above.
∎

Fix any
(\mathbb{y},(h_{t,t^{\prime}})_{0\leq t\leq 2k,1\leq t^{\prime}\leq a_{t}})\in W(\mathbb{a})
.
Let
\tilde{h}_{t,t^{\prime}}\in(\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{2k+1}
be the vector whose
td+1
to
(t+1)d
coordinates equal to
h_{t,t^{\prime}}
, and whose all other coefficients are zero.
Since
P
is a constant
\mod\mathbb{Z}
on
\iota^{-1}(\Omega_{I}\times_{0}\Omega_{I})
, we have that

(6.5)

\prod_{0\leq t\leq 2k,1\leq t^{\prime}\leq a_{t}}\Delta_{\tilde{h}_{t,t^{\prime}}}P(\mathbb{x})\in\mathbb{Z}\text{ for all }(\mathbb{x},(h_{t,t^{\prime}})_{0\leq t\leq k,1\leq t^{\prime}\leq a_{t}})\in\iota^{-1}(\Omega_{I}\times_{0}\Omega_{I}).

We need to introduce some notations. Write

\tilde{L}^{\prime}_{j}(x_{0},\dots,x_{2k}):=\sum_{i=0}^{2k}u_{i,j}x_{i}

for some
u_{i,j}\in\mathbb{Z}
of complexity at most
O_{C_{0},k,s}(1)
for all
0\leq j\leq 2k+2s-2
. Let
u_{i}:=(u_{i,0},\dots,u_{i,2k+2s-2})\in\mathbb{Z}^{2k+2s-1}
, and let
\mathbb{u}:=(u_{0},\dots,u_{2k})
. For any
\mathbb{a}=(a_{0},\dots,a_{2k})\in\mathbb{N}^{2k+1}
, denote
\mathbb{u}^{\mathbb{a}}:=u_{0}^{\ast a_{0}}\ast\ldots\ast u_{2k}^{\ast a_{2k}}
, where
u_{i}^{\ast a_{i}}:=u_{i}\ast\ldots\ast u_{i}
with
u_{i}
appearing
a_{i}
times.

After some computations, it follows from (
6.4
) that

\begin{split}\prod_{0\leq t\leq k,1\leq t^{\prime}\leq a_{t}}\Delta_{\tilde{h}_{t,t^{\prime}}}P(\mathbb{x})=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}^{d},|j|=i}\eta(\tilde{g}_{j}^{\mathbb{u}^{\mathbb{a}}})\sum_{i_{0},\dots,i_{2k}\in\mathbb{N}^{d},|i_{t}|=a_{t},i_{0}+\dots+i_{2k}=j}\Bigl(\sum_{R\in\mathcal{G}(i_{0},\dots,i_{2k})}\prod_{t=0}^{2k}\prod_{t^{\prime}=1}^{a_{t}}h_{t,t^{\prime},R(t,t^{\prime})}\Bigr),\end{split}

where
h_{t,t^{\prime}}=(h_{t,t^{\prime},1},\dots,h_{t,t^{\prime},d})
,
i_{t}=(i_{t,1},\dots,i_{t,d})
,
\mathcal{G}(i_{0},\dots,i_{k})
is the collection of all functions
R\colon\{(t,t^{\prime})\colon 0\leq t\leq 2k,1\leq t^{\prime}\leq a_{t}\}\to\{1,\dots,d\}
such that for all
0\leq t\leq 2k
and
1\leq t^{\prime\prime}\leq d
, the number of
R(t,t^{\prime}),1\leq t^{\prime}\leq a_{t}
which equals to
t^{\prime\prime}
is
i_{t,t^{\prime\prime}}
. Let
\phi
be a bijection between
\{1,\dots,i\}
and
\{(t,t^{\prime})\colon 0\leq t\leq 2k,1\leq t^{\prime}\leq a_{t}\}
. Then

\begin{split}\prod_{0\leq t\leq 2k,1\leq t^{\prime}\leq a_{t}}\Delta_{\tilde{h}_{t,t^{\prime}}}P(\mathbb{x})=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}^{d},|j|=i}\eta(\tilde{g}_{j}^{\mathbb{u}^{\mathbb{a}}})\sum_{i_{0},\dots,i_{2k}\in\mathbb{N}^{d},|i_{t}|=a_{t},i_{0}+\dots+i_{2k}=j}\Bigl(\sum_{R\in\mathcal{G}(i_{0},\dots,i_{2k})}\prod_{t=1}^{i}h_{\phi(t),R(\phi(t))}\Bigr),\end{split}

Let
\mathcal{F}(j)
be the collection of all maps
r\colon\{1,\dots,i\}\to\{1,\dots,d\}
such that
|r^{-1}(t^{\prime\prime})|=j_{t^{\prime\prime}}
for all
1\leq t^{\prime\prime}\leq d
.
Note that for all
r\in\mathcal{F}(j)
, there exist a unique choice of
(i_{0},\dots,i_{2k})\in(\mathbb{N}^{d})^{2k+1}

with
|i_{t}|=a_{t},i_{0}+\dots+i_{2k}=j
,
and
R\in\mathcal{G}(i_{0},\dots,i_{2k})
such that
r=R\circ\phi
. Moreover, this induces a bijection between
\mathcal{F}(j)
and
\cup_{i_{0},\dots,i_{2k}\in\mathbb{N}^{d},|i_{t}|=a_{t},i_{0}+\dots+i_{2k}=j}\mathcal{G}(i_{0},\dots,i_{2k})
. In other words,

(6.6)

\begin{split}\prod_{0\leq t\leq 2k,1\leq t^{\prime}\leq a_{t}}\Delta_{\tilde{h}_{t,t^{\prime}}}P(\mathbb{x})=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}^{d},|j|=i}\eta(\tilde{g}_{j}^{\mathbb{u}^{\mathbb{a}}})\cdot\Bigl(\sum_{r\in\mathcal{F}(j)}\prod_{t=1}^{i}h_{\phi(t),r(\phi(t))}\Bigr).\end{split}

By (
6.5
), (
6.6
) and Lemma
6.7
,

\prod_{0\leq t\leq 2k,1\leq t^{\prime}\leq a_{t}}\Delta_{\tilde{h}_{t,t^{\prime}}}P(\mathbb{x})
is independent of
\mathbb{x}
, and belongs to
\mathbb{Z}

whenever
h_{t,t^{\prime}},0\leq t\leq 2k,1\leq t^{\prime}\leq a_{t}
are
p
-linearly independent and pairwise
(M,p)
-orthogonal.
By a change of indices,
for all
h_{1},\dots,h_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
which are
p
-linearly independent and pairwise
(M,p)
-orthogonal, writing
h_{t}=(m_{t,1},\dots,m_{t_{d}})
,
we have that

(6.7)

\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}^{d},|j|=i}\eta(\tilde{g}_{j}^{\mathbb{u}^{\mathbb{a}}})\cdot\Bigl(\sum_{r\in\mathcal{F}(j)}\prod_{t=1}^{i}m_{t,r(t)}\Bigr)\in\mathbb{Z}.

Lemma 6.8
.

There exist
\mathbb{a}=(a_{0},\dots,a_{2k})\in\mathbb{N}^{2k+1}
with
a_{0}+\dots+a_{2k}=i
such that the map
\xi_{\mathbb{a}}(h):=\eta(h^{\mathbb{u}^{\mathbb{a}}}),h\in G
is a nontrivial
i
-th type-III horizontal character of
G/\Gamma
of complexity at most
O_{C_{0},C,\delta,d}(1)
.

Proof.

The proof is similar to the argument on pages 29–30 of
[
9
]
.
Since
\eta|_{G_{(i)}^{V_{X}\times_{0}V_{X}}}
is nontrivial, and the vectors
\mathbb{u}^{\mathbb{a}},\mathbb{a}=(a_{0},\dots,a_{2k})\in\mathbb{N}^{2k+1},a_{0}+\dots+a_{2k}=i
span
(V_{X}\times_{0}V_{X})^{[i]}
,
there exist
\mathbb{a}=(a_{0},\dots,a_{2k})\in\mathbb{N}^{2k+1}
with
a_{0}+\dots+a_{2k}=i
such that
\xi_{\mathbb{a}}
is nontrivial. Fix such an
\mathbb{a}
.

For all
\gamma\in\Gamma_{i}
, since
\gamma^{\mathbb{u}^{\mathbb{a}}}\in\Gamma_{(i)}^{V_{X}\times_{0}V_{X}}
, we have that
\xi_{\mathbb{a}}(\gamma)\in\mathbb{Z}
.

Moreover, by the flag property of
V_{X}\times_{0}V_{X}
,
\mathbb{u}^{\mathbb{a}}\in(V_{X}\times_{0}V_{X})^{[i]}\subseteq(V_{X}\times_{0}V_{X})^{[i+1]}
. So for all
h_{i+1}\in G_{i+1}
, we have
h_{i+1}^{\mathbb{u}^{\mathbb{a}}}\in G_{(i+1)}^{V_{X}\times_{0}V_{X}}
. By the maximality of
i
, we have that
\eta(h_{i+1}^{\mathbb{u}^{\mathbb{a}}})\in\mathbb{Z}
and so
\xi_{\mathbb{a}}
annihilates
G_{i+1}
.

On the other hand, for any
0\leq j\leq i
, there exist
0\leq a^{\prime}_{0}\leq a_{0},\dots,0\leq a^{\prime}_{2k}\leq a_{2k}
such that
a^{\prime}_{0}+\dots+a^{\prime}_{2k}=j
. Write
\mathbb{a}^{\prime}:=(a^{\prime}_{0},\dots,a^{\prime}_{2k})
. Then
we may write

\mathbb{u}^{\mathbb{a}}=w\ast w^{\prime},

where

w:=\mathbb{u}^{\mathbb{a}^{\prime}}
and
w^{\prime}:=\mathbb{u}^{\mathbb{a}-\mathbb{a}^{\prime}}.

It is clear that
w\in(V_{X}\times_{0}V_{X})^{[j]}
and
w^{\prime}\in(V_{X}\times_{0}V_{X})^{[i-j]}
.
If
h\in G_{j}
and
h^{\prime}\in G_{i-j}
, then

[h^{w},{h^{\prime}}^{w^{\prime}}]\equiv[h,h^{\prime}]^{ww^{\prime}}\mod G^{V_{X}\times_{0}V_{X}}_{(i+1)}

by the Baker-Campbell-Hausdorff formula. Since
\eta
is trivial on
G^{V_{X}\times_{0}V_{X}}_{(i+1)}
and
[G^{V_{X}\times_{0}V_{X}},

G^{V_{X}\times_{0}V_{X}}]
, we have that

\xi_{\mathbb{a}}([h,h^{\prime}])\equiv\eta([h,h^{\prime}]^{ww^{\prime}})\equiv\eta([h^{w},h^{w^{\prime}}])=0\mod\mathbb{Z}.

So
\xi_{\mathbb{a}}
vanishes on
[G_{j},G_{i-j}]
for all
0\leq j\leq i
.
In conclusion, we have that
\xi_{\mathbb{a}}
is an
i
-th type-III horizontal character. It is clear to see that the complexity of
\xi_{\mathbb{a}}
is
O_{C_{0},C,\delta,d}(1)
.
∎

Fix such an
\mathbb{a}
and
\xi:=\xi_{\mathbb{a}}
given by Lemma
6.8
, and denote

Q(n,h_{1},\dots,h_{i}):=\xi\circ\Delta_{h_{i}}\dots\Delta_{h_{1}}\tilde{g}(n).

Since
\xi
is a
i
-th type-III horizontal character, similar to (
5.5
),
one can compute that

(6.8)

Q(n,h_{1},\dots,h_{i})=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}^{d},|j|=i}\xi(\tilde{g}_{j})\sum_{r\in\mathcal{F}(j)}\prod_{t=1}^{i}m_{t,r(t)},

where
h_{t}=(m_{t,1},\dots,m_{t,d})
, and recall that
\mathcal{F}(j)
is the collection of all maps
r\colon\{1,\dots,i\}\to\{1,\dots,d\}
such that
|r^{-1}(t^{\prime\prime})|=j_{t^{\prime\prime}}
for all
1\leq t^{\prime\prime}\leq d
.
So
Q

is a polynomial in
\text{poly}((\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{i+1}\to\mathbb{R})
which is independent of
n
.
Let
\tilde{M}_{r}\colon\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to\mathbb{Z}/p
be the regular lifting of
M-r
.
Since
\tilde{g}\in\text{poly}_{p}(\iota^{-1}(V(M-r))\to G_{\mathbb{N}}|\Gamma)=\text{poly}_{p}(V_{p}(\tilde{M}_{r})\to G_{\mathbb{N}}|\Gamma)
, by Lemma
A.16
,
p^{s-1}\eta(g_{i})\in\mathbb{Z}
for all
i\in\mathbb{N}^{d},0<|i|\leq s-1
. So
Q\in\text{poly}((\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{i+1}\to\mathbb{Z}/p^{s-1})
.

Combining (
6.8
) with (
6.7
), we have that

(6.9)

Q(n,h_{1},\dots,h_{i})\in\mathbb{Z}\text{ for all $(n,h_{1},\dots,h_{i})\in W_{i}$,}

where
W_{i}
is the set of

(n,h_{1},\dots,h_{i})\in\Box_{p,i}(V_{p}(\tilde{M}_{r}))
with
h_{1},\dots,h_{i}
being
p
-linearly independent. Here we used the fact that
for all
h_{1},\dots,h_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
which are
p
-linearly independent and pairwise
(M,p)
-orthogonal, there exists
n\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
with
(n,h_{1},\dots,h_{i})\in\Box_{p,i}(V_{p}(\tilde{M}_{r}))
(which follows from the
\mathbb{Z}^{d}
-version of Lemmas
B.6
and
B.8
since
d\geq 2s+1
).
In other words,

W_{i}\subseteq V_{p}(Q)\cap\Box_{p,i}(V_{p}(\tilde{M}_{r}))
(note that
W_{i}+p(\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{i+1}=W_{i}
).

Next we use the irreducibility property of
\Box_{p,i}(V_{p}(\tilde{M}_{r}))
to remove the
p
-linearly independence restriction in (
6.9
).
By Lemma
B.7
, since
d\geq s^{2}-s+3
,

|\Box_{p,i}(V_{p}(\tilde{M}_{r}))\cap[p]^{d(i+1)}|=|\Box_{i}(V(M-r))|=p^{d(i+1)-(\frac{i(i+1)}{2}+1)}(1+O_{s}(p^{-1/2})).

On the other hand, by Lemma
B.3
,

|[p]^{d(i+1)}\backslash W_{i}|\leq ip^{d+(d+1)(i-1)}=ip^{di+(i-1)}.

Since
d\geq s^{2}-s+3
and
p\gg_{d,k,s}1
, this implies that

|(\Box_{p,i}(V_{p}(\tilde{M}_{r}))\cap[p]^{d(i+1)})\backslash W_{i}|<|\Box_{p,i}(V_{p}(\tilde{M}_{r}))\cap[p]^{d(i+1)}|/2.

Since
W_{i}\subseteq V_{p}(Q)\cap\Box_{p,i}(V_{p}(\tilde{M}_{r}))
, we have that

(6.10)

|V_{p}(Q)\cap\Box_{p,i}(V_{p}(\tilde{M}_{r}))\cap[p]^{d(i+1)}|>|\Box_{p,i}(V_{p}(\tilde{M}_{r}))\cap[p]^{d(i+1)}|/2.

By Example B.4 of
[
20
]
,
\Box_{i}(V(M-r))
is a nice and consistent
M
-set of total co-dimension
\binom{i+1}{2}+1
. So by Theorem
B.14
,
since
d\geq s^{2}-s+3
, we have that

\\
iota^{-1}(\Box_{i}(V(M-r)))=\Box_{p,i}(V_{p}(\tilde{M}_{p}))
is strongly
(1/2,p)
-irreducible up to degree
s-1
(see Appendix
B.6
for definitions). Since
Q\in\text{poly}((\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{i+1}\to\mathbb{Z}/p^{s-1})
, (
6.10
) implies that

\Box_{p,i}(V_{p}(\tilde{M}_{r}))\subseteq V_{p}(Q)
.
Therefore,

Q(n,h_{1},\dots,h_{i})\in\mathbb{Z}
for all

(n,h_{1},\dots,h_{i})\in\Box_{p,i}(V_{p}(\tilde{M}_{r}))
.

By the definition of
Q
, this means that

\tilde{g}
is not
(O_{C_{0},C,\delta,d}(1),\iota^{-1}(V(M-r)),p)
-irrational and thus
g
is not
(O_{C_{0},C,\delta,d}(1),V(M-r))
-irrational. We arrive at contradiction if we pick some
N_{0}\gg_{C_{0},C,\delta,d}1
. This completes the proof of Part (ii) of Theorem
6.5
.

So now it suffices to prove Part (i) of Theorem
6.5
. We refer the readers to Appendices
B.3
and
B.4
for the terminologies to be used in the discussion below. By Lemma
6.3
(ii),
\Omega_{I}\times_{0}\Omega_{I}
is a nice and consistent
M
-set of total co-dimension at most
k^{2}+3k+1
.
So we may write
\Omega_{I}\times_{0}\Omega_{I}=V(\mathcal{J})
for some consistent
(M,2k+1)
-family
\mathcal{J}\subseteq\mathbb{F}_{p}[x_{0},\dots,x_{2k}]
of total dimension at most
k^{2}+3k+1
. Let
(\mathcal{J}^{\prime},\mathcal{J}^{\prime\prime})
be an
\{x_{0},\dots,x_{k}\}
-decomposition of
\mathcal{J}
.
Note that the set of
(x_{0},\dots,x_{k})\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{2k+1}
such that
(x_{0},\dots,x_{2k})\in V(\mathcal{J}^{\prime})
for all
x_{k+1},\dots,x_{2k}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
is equal to
\Omega_{I}
.

We remark that
G^{V_{X}}
is the projection of
G^{V_{X}\times_{0}V_{X}}
onto the first
(k+s)d
coordinates.
Let
F\in\text{Lip}(G^{V_{X}}/\Gamma^{V_{X}}\to\mathbb{C})
with Lipschitz norm bounded by 1. Define
\tilde{F}\colon G^{V_{X}\times_{0}V_{X}}/\Gamma^{V_{X}\times_{0}V_{X}}\to\mathbb{C}
,
\tilde{F}(x_{0},\dots,x_{2k+2s-2}):=F(x_{0},\dots,x_{k+s-1})
.
Since Part (ii) holds, by Theorem
B.12
, Lemma
3.4
, and the observation that
L^{\prime}_{j}(\mathbb{x},\mathbb{x}^{\prime})=L_{I,j}(\mathbb{x})
for all
(\mathbb{x},\mathbb{x}^{\prime})\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k+1}\times(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}
and
0\leq j\leq k+s-1
, we have that

\begin{split}&\quad\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{(x_{0},\dots,x_{k+s-1})\in\Omega}F((g(x_{0}),\dots,g(x_{k+s-1}))\Gamma^{k+s})-\int_{G^{V_{X}}/\Gamma^{V_{X}}}F\,dm\Bigr|\\
&=\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{x}\in\Omega_{I}}F((g(L_{I,0}(\mathbb{x})),\dots,g(L_{I,k+s-1}(\mathbb{x})))\Gamma^{k+s})-\int_{G^{V_{X}}/\Gamma^{V_{X}}}F\,dm\Bigr|\\
&=\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{x}\in\Omega_{I}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{x}^{\prime}\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}\colon(\mathbb{x},\mathbb{x}^{\prime})\in V(\mathcal{J}^{\prime\prime})}F((g(L_{I,0}(\mathbb{x})),\dots,g(L_{I,k+s-1}(\mathbb{x})))\Gamma^{k+s})-\int_{G^{V_{X}\times_{0}V_{X}}/\Gamma^{V_{X}\times_{0}V_{X}}}\tilde{F}\,dm\Bigr|\\
&=\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{(\mathbb{x},\mathbb{x}^{\prime})\in\Omega_{I}\times_{0}\Omega_{I}}F((g(L_{I,0}(\mathbb{x})),\dots,g(L_{I,k+s-1}(\mathbb{x})))\Gamma^{k+s})-\int_{G^{V_{X}\times_{0}V_{X}}/\Gamma^{V_{X}\times_{0}V_{X}}}\tilde{F}\,dm\Bigr|+O_{k,s}(p^{-1/2})\\
&=\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{y}\in\Omega_{I}\times_{0}\Omega_{I}}\tilde{F}((g(L^{\prime}_{0}(\mathbb{y})),\dots,g(L^{\prime}_{2k+2s-2}(\mathbb{y})))\Gamma^{k+s})-\int_{G^{V_{X}\times_{0}V_{X}}/\Gamma^{V_{X}\times_{0}V_{X}}}\tilde{F}\,dm\Bigr|+O_{k,s}(p^{-1/2})\\
&\leq\delta\|\tilde{F}\|_{\text{Lip}(G^{V_{X}\times_{0}V_{X}}/\Gamma^{V_{X}\times_{0}V_{X}})}+O_{k,s}(p^{-1/2})=\delta\|F\|_{\text{Lip}(G^{V_{X}}/\Gamma^{V_{X}})}+O_{k,s}(p^{-1/2})\leq\delta+O_{k,s}(p^{-1/2}),\end{split}

where
m
is the Haar measure of
G^{V_{X}\times_{0}V_{X}}/\Gamma^{V_{X}\times_{0}V_{X}}.

This completes the proof of Theorem
6.5

by taking
p\gg_{C_{0},C,\delta,d}1
.

7.
Proof of Theorem
3.1

We are now ready to complete the proof of Theorem
3.1
. The outline of the proof is similar to Section 6 of
[
9
]
.
By Lemma
3.4
(ii) and (iii),
it suffices to show that if

d\geq d_{0}(X),

and
p\gg_{C_{0},d,\epsilon}1
, then

(7.1)

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}_{(x_{0},\dots,x_{k+s-1})\in\Omega}\mathbb{1}_{E}(x_{0})\mathbb{1}_{E}(x_{1})\dots\mathbb{1}_{E}(x_{k+s-1})\gg_{C_{0},d,\epsilon}1\end{split}

for all
E\subseteq V(M-r)
with
|E|>\epsilon p^{d-1}
. Let
\mathcal{F}\colon\mathbb{R}_{+}\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
be a growth function and
\epsilon^{\prime}>0
to be chosen later, which depend only on
C_{0},d,\epsilon
.
By Theorem
5.11
, if
p\gg_{d,\epsilon^{\prime},\mathcal{F}}1
and
d\geq(2s+12)(15s+423)
, then there exists
C:=C(d,\epsilon^{\prime},\mathcal{F})>0
such that we may write

(7.2)

\begin{split}\mathbb{1}_{E}=f_{\text{str}}+f_{\text{uni}}+f_{\text{err}}\end{split}

such that

•

f_{\text{str}}
and
f_{\text{str}}+f_{\text{err}}
take values in
[0,1]
;

•

we may write
f_{\text{str}}
as

f_{\text{str}}(n)=F(g(n)\Gamma)\text{ for all }n\in V(M-r)

for some
\mathbb{N}
-filtered nilmanifold
G/\Gamma
of degree at most
s-1
and complexity at most
C
, some
(\mathcal{F}(C),V(M-r))
-irrational
g\in\text{poly}_{p}(V(M)\to G_{\mathbb{N}}|\Gamma)
, and some
F\in\text{Lip}(G/\Gamma\to\mathbb{C})
of Lipschitz norm at most
C
;

•

\|f_{\text{uni}}\|_{U^{s}(V(M-r))}\leq 1/\mathcal{F}(C)
;

•

\|f_{\text{err}}\|_{L^{2}(V(M-r))}\leq\epsilon^{\prime}
.

We remark that
C
will be dependent only on
C_{0},d,\epsilon
since we will choose
\mathcal{F}
and
\epsilon^{\prime}
to be dependent only on
C_{0},d,\epsilon
.

We need to first construct a good weight function for our average:

Proposition 7.1
.

If the growth function
\mathcal{F}
grows sufficiently fast depending on
C_{0},d,\epsilon^{\prime}
and
p\gg_{C_{0},C,d,\epsilon^{\prime},\mathcal{F}}1
, then there exists a function
\omega\colon\Omega\to\mathbb{R}
such that the following holds:

(1)

0\leq\omega(\mathbb{x})\leq O_{C_{0},C,d,\epsilon^{\prime}}(1)
for all
\mathbb{x}\in\Omega
;

(2)

\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{x}\in\Omega}\omega(\mathbb{x})=1
;

(3)

|\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{x}\in\Omega\times_{i}\Omega}(\omega\times_{i}\omega)(\mathbb{x})-1|\leq O_{C_{0},d}(\epsilon^{\prime})
for all
0\leq i\leq k+s-1
, where
\omega\times_{i}\omega\colon\Omega\times_{i}\Omega\to\mathbb{C}
is the function given by

\omega\times_{i}\omega(x,\mathbb{x}^{+},\mathbb{x}^{-}):=\omega(x\downharpoonleft_{i}\mathbb{x}^{+})\overline{\omega}(x\downharpoonleft_{i}\mathbb{x}^{+})

for all
(x,\mathbb{x}^{+},\mathbb{x}^{-})\in\Omega\times_{i}\Omega
;
12
12

12

It is crutial that the implicit constants in Condition (iii) is independent of
C
.

(4)

|f_{\text{str}}(x_{i})-f_{\text{str}}(x_{i^{\prime}})|\leq\epsilon^{\prime}
for all
0\leq i,i^{\prime}\leq k+s-1
if
\omega(x_{0},\dots,x_{k+s-1})>0
.

Proof.

The idea of the proof comes from Proposition 6.2 of
[
9
]
. However, we need to tailor the approach of
[
9
]
in many aspects for our purposes.
Let
\epsilon^{\prime\prime},R,\rho_{0},\rho,\delta>0
to be chosen later. We will chose
\epsilon^{\prime\prime},R,\rho_{0},\rho,\delta
in a way such that the later parameter in the list below

1,\epsilon^{\prime\prime},R^{-1},\rho_{0},\rho,\delta

is sufficiently smaller than the former one, depending only on
C_{0},C,d,\epsilon^{\prime}
.

For the moment write the quantity
\delta:=\delta_{C_{0},d,\epsilon^{\prime}}(C)
to be chosen laster as a function of
C
.
Since
g
is
(\mathcal{F}(C),V(M-r))
-irrational, by Theorem
6.5
,
if
\mathcal{F}
is a growth function such that

\mathcal{F}(x)>N_{0}(\max\{C_{0},x\},\delta_{C_{0},d,\epsilon^{\prime}}(x),d,k,s)
(where
N_{0}
is defined in Theorem
6.5
) for all
x>0
and
p\gg_{C_{0},C,\delta,d,\mathcal{F}}1
, then

•

the sequence

(g(x_{0})\Gamma,\dots,g(x_{k+s-1})\Gamma)_{(x_{0},\dots,x_{k+s-1})\in\Omega}

is
2\delta
-equidistributed on
G^{V_{X}}/\Gamma^{V_{X}}
;

•

the sequence

(g(x_{0})\Gamma,\dots,g(x_{2k+2s-1})\Gamma)_{(x_{0},\dots,x_{2k+2s-1})\in\Omega\times_{i}\Omega}

is
\delta
-equidistributed on
G^{V_{X}\times_{i}V_{X}}/\Gamma^{V_{X}\times_{i}V_{X}}
for all
0\leq i\leq k+s-1
.

Let
\{X_{1},\dots,X_{\dim(G)}\}
be the Mal’cev basis of
G/\Gamma
.
For
\rho>0
, let
B_{\rho}
denote the set of elements in
G
of the form

\exp\Bigl(\sum_{j=1}^{\dim(G)}t_{j}X_{j}\Bigr)

for some
|t_{j}|\leq\rho^{s-i}
whenever
1\leq i\leq s-1
and
j\leq\dim(G)-\dim(G_{i})
.
For all
\epsilon^{\prime\prime},\rho>0
, let
\phi_{\rho,\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}\colon G\to\mathbb{R}
be a non-negative function which is supported on
B_{\rho}
and equals to 1 on
B_{(1-\epsilon^{\prime\prime})\rho}
. We may further require that the Lipschitz norm of
\phi_{\rho,\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}
is
O_{C,\epsilon^{\prime\prime},\rho}(1)
and that
\phi_{\rho^{\prime},\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}\leq\phi_{\rho,\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}
pointwise for all
0<\rho^{\prime}<\rho\leq 1
and
\epsilon^{\prime\prime}>0
. Let
\Phi_{\rho,\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}\colon(G/\Gamma)^{2}\to\mathbb{R}
be the function given by

\Phi_{\rho,\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}(x,x^{\prime}):=\sum_{h\in G\colon hx=x^{\prime}}\phi_{\rho,\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}(h).

The function
\Phi_{\rho,\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}
is supported near the diagonal of
(G/\Gamma)^{2}
and
\Phi_{\rho,\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}(x,x^{\prime})\neq 0
only if
x^{\prime}\in B_{\rho}x
. Moreover, if
x^{\prime}\in B_{(1-\epsilon^{\prime\prime})\rho}x
, then
\Phi_{\rho,\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}(x,x^{\prime})=1
.
By Lemma 6.3 of
[
9
]
, if
\rho_{0}
is chosen sufficiently small depending on
C,\epsilon^{\prime\prime},R
, then we have the approximate shift-invariance

(7.3)

\begin{split}\Phi_{(1-3\epsilon^{\prime\prime})\rho,\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}(x,x^{\prime})\leq\Phi_{\rho,\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}(hx,hx^{\prime})\leq\Phi_{(1+3\epsilon^{\prime\prime})\rho,\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}(x,x^{\prime})\end{split}

for all
0<\rho<\rho_{0}
,
x,x^{\prime}\in G/\Gamma
and
h\in G
such that
d_{G}(h,id_{G})\leq R
.

Let

W_{\rho,\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}:=\mathbb{E}_{(x_{0},\dots,x_{k+s-1})\in\Omega}\prod_{0\leq i<i^{\prime}\leq k+s-1}\Phi_{\rho,\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}(g(x_{i})\Gamma,g(x_{i^{\prime}})\Gamma)

and

\begin{split}\omega(x_{0},\dots,x_{k+s-1}):=\omega_{\rho,\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}(x_{0},\dots,x_{k+s-1}):=\frac{1}{W_{\rho,\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}}\prod_{0\leq i<i^{\prime}\leq k+s-1}\Phi_{\rho,\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}(g(x_{i})\Gamma,g(x_{i^{\prime}})\Gamma)\end{split}

for some
\rho
and
\epsilon^{\prime\prime}
to be chosen later. By definition, it is clear that
\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{x}\in\Omega}\omega(\mathbb{x})=1
.
So Condition (ii) holds.

If
\omega(x_{0},\dots,x_{k+s-1})>0
, then for all
0\leq i<i^{\prime}\leq k+s-1
,

|f_{\text{str}}(x_{i})-f_{\text{str}}(x_{i^{\prime}})|\leq\|F\|_{\text{Lip}(G/\Gamma)}\cdot d_{G/\Gamma}(g(x_{i})\Gamma,g(x_{i^{\prime}})\Gamma)\leq C\rho_{0}<\epsilon^{\prime}.

So Condition (iv) is satisfied if we choose
\rho_{0}\leq\epsilon^{\prime}C^{-1}
.

Since
V_{X}
is of complexity at most
O_{C_{0},d}(1)
, by Lemma
6.1
(iv), we have that
G^{V_{X}}/\Gamma^{V_{X}}
is of complexity at most
O_{C_{0},C,d}(1)
.
Let
\tilde{\Phi}_{\rho,\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}\colon(G/\Gamma)^{k+s}\to\mathbb{R}
be the function given by
