\begin{split}\tilde{\Phi}_{\rho,\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}(u_{0},\dots,u_{k+s-1}):=\prod_{0\leq i<i^{\prime}\leq k+s-1}\Phi_{\rho,\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}(u_{i},u_{i^{\prime}})\end{split}

Clearly
\|\tilde{\Phi}_{\rho,\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}\|_{\text{Lip}((G/\Gamma)^{k+s})}=O_{C_{0},C,d,\epsilon^{\prime\prime},\rho}(1)
and

\|\tilde{\Phi}_{\rho,\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}\|_{\text{Lip}(G^{V_{X}}/\Gamma^{V_{X}})}=O_{C_{0},C,d,\epsilon^{\prime\prime},\rho}(1).

Define

S_{\rho,\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}:=\int_{G^{V_{X}}/\Gamma^{V_{X}}}\tilde{\Phi}_{\rho,\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}\,dm_{G^{V_{X}}/\Gamma^{V_{X}}},

where
m_{G^{V_{X}}/\Gamma^{V_{X}}}
is the Haar measure of
G^{V_{X}}/\Gamma^{V_{X}}
.
Then

(7.4)

\begin{split}|W_{\rho,\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}-S_{\rho,\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}|\leq 2\delta\|\tilde{\Phi}_{\rho,\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}\|_{\text{Lip}(G^{V_{X}}/\Gamma^{V_{X}})}=O_{C_{0},C,d,\epsilon^{\prime\prime},\rho}(\delta).\end{split}

Since
\tilde{\Phi}_{\rho,\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}
equals to 1 on a ball of radius
\rho^{O_{C_{0},C,d}(1)}
centered at the identity and is bounded by 1 throughout. We have that

(7.5)

\begin{split}O_{C_{0},C,d}(\rho^{O_{C_{0},C,d}(1)})\leq S_{\rho,\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}\leq 1.\end{split}

Moreover, by the property of
\phi_{\rho,\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}
, we have that
S_{\rho^{\prime},\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}\leq S_{\rho,\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}
for all
0<\rho^{\prime}<\rho<\rho_{0}
.

Claim.
If
\rho_{0}
and
\epsilon^{\prime\prime}
sufficiently small depending only on
C_{0},C,d,\epsilon^{\prime}
, then there exists

\rho_{0}>\rho\gg_{C_{0},C,d,\epsilon^{\prime},\epsilon^{\prime\prime},\rho_{0}}1\gg_{C_{0},C,d,\epsilon^{\prime}}1
such that

(7.6)

\begin{split}(1-\epsilon^{\prime})S_{\rho,\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}\leq S_{(1-3\epsilon^{\prime\prime})\rho,\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}\leq S_{(1+3\epsilon^{\prime\prime})\rho,\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}\leq(1+\epsilon^{\prime})S_{\rho,\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}.\end{split}

To see this,
we say that
0<\rho<\rho_{0}
is
good
if

S_{\rho,\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}<(1+\epsilon^{\prime})S_{(1-3\epsilon^{\prime\prime})\rho,\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}.

Denote
\rho_{n}:=(1-3\epsilon^{\prime\prime})^{n}\rho_{0}
. Suppose that for some
S\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
, there exist
n_{0}:=0<n_{1}<n_{2}<\dots<n_{S}
with
1\leq n_{i}-n_{i-1}\leq 2
such that
\rho_{n_{i}}
is bad for all
1\leq i\leq S
.
Then

S_{\rho_{n_{i}},\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}-S_{\rho_{n_{i+1}},\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}\geq S_{\rho_{n_{i}},\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}-S_{(1-3\epsilon^{\prime\prime})\rho_{n_{i}},\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}\geq\epsilon^{\prime}S_{(1-3\epsilon^{\prime\prime})\rho_{n_{i}},\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}\geq\epsilon^{\prime}S_{\rho_{n_{i+1}},\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}

for all
1\leq i\leq S-1
.
Summing over
i
, it follows from (
7.5
) that

\begin{split}&\quad 1\geq S_{\rho_{n_{1}},\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}\geq\sum_{i=2}^{S-1}\epsilon^{\prime}S_{\rho_{n_{i}},\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}\geq\sum_{i=2}^{S-1}\epsilon^{\prime}S_{(1-3\epsilon^{\prime\prime})^{2i+2}\rho_{0},\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}\\
&=O_{C_{0},C,d,\epsilon^{\prime}}\Bigl(\rho_{0}^{O_{C_{0},C,d}(1)}\frac{(1-3\epsilon^{\prime\prime})^{4}(1-(1-3\epsilon^{\prime\prime})^{2S-2})}{1-(1-3\epsilon^{\prime\prime})^{2}}\Bigr).\end{split}

If we choose
\rho_{0}
and
\epsilon^{\prime\prime}
sufficiently small depending only on
C_{0},C,d,\epsilon^{\prime}
, and then choose
S
to be sufficiently large depending only on
\rho_{0},C_{0},C,d,\epsilon^{\prime},\epsilon^{\prime\prime}
, we arrive at a contradiction.
In other words, there exist
S=O_{\rho_{0},C_{0},C,d,\epsilon^{\prime},\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}(1)
and some
0<n\leq 2S
such that both
\rho_{n}
and
\rho_{n+1}
are good.

Let
\rho:=\rho_{n+1}
. Then
\rho\gg_{\rho_{0},C_{0},C,d,\epsilon^{\prime},\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}1
.
Since
\rho
and
(1-3\epsilon^{\prime\prime})^{-1}\rho
are good, we have that

(1-\epsilon^{\prime})S_{\rho,\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}<(1+\epsilon^{\prime})S_{(1-3\epsilon^{\prime\prime})\rho,\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}-\epsilon^{\prime}S_{\rho,\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}\leq S_{(1-3\epsilon^{\prime\prime})\rho,\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}

and

S_{(1+3\epsilon^{\prime\prime})\rho,\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}<S_{(1-3\epsilon^{\prime\prime})^{-1}\rho,\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}<(1+\epsilon^{\prime})S_{\rho,\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}.

This completes the proof of the claim.

We now fix such
\rho
given by the claim. We remark that
1\ll_{C_{0},C,d,\epsilon^{\prime}}\rho\leq O_{C_{0},C,d,\epsilon^{\prime}}(1)
since eventually we will pick
\rho_{0}
to be dependent only on
C_{0},C,d,\epsilon^{\prime}
.
It then follows from (
7.4
) and (
7.5
) that

W_{\rho,\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}\geq O_{C_{0},C,d}(\rho^{O_{C_{0},C,d}(1)})+O_{C_{0},C,d,\epsilon^{\prime\prime},\rho}(\delta)\gg_{C_{0},C,d,\epsilon^{\prime}}1

if
\delta
is sufficiently small depending on
C_{0},C,d,\epsilon^{\prime\prime},\rho
. So Condition (i) holds.

It remains to check Condition (iii). For convenience we only consider the case
i=0
as the other cases are similar. By Lemma
2.1
, we may assume without loss of generality that
I=\{0,\dots,k\}
is a generating set of
X
.
Let
\tilde{\Phi}_{\rho,\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}\times_{0}\tilde{\Phi}_{\rho,\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}\colon(G/\Gamma)^{2k+2s-1}\to\mathbb{R}
be the function given by

\begin{split}\tilde{\Phi}_{\rho,\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}\times_{0}\tilde{\Phi}_{\rho,\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}(u_{0},\dots,u_{2k+2s-1}):=\tilde{\Phi}_{\rho,\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}(u_{0},\dots,u_{k+s-1})\tilde{\Phi}_{\rho,\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}(u_{k+s},\dots,u_{2k+2s-1})\end{split}

for all
u_{0},\dots,u_{2k+2s-1}\in G/\Gamma
.
Clearly

\|\tilde{\Phi}_{\rho,\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}\times_{0}\tilde{\Phi}_{\rho,\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}\|_{\text{Lip}(G^{V_{X}\times_{0}V_{X}}/\Gamma^{V_{X}\times_{0}V_{X}})}=O_{C_{0},C,d,\epsilon^{\prime\prime},\rho}(1).

Define

S^{\prime}_{\rho,\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}:=\int_{G^{V_{X}\times_{0}V_{X}}/{\Gamma}^{V_{X}\times_{0}V_{X}}}\tilde{\Phi}_{\rho,\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}\times_{0}\tilde{\Phi}_{\rho,\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}\,dm_{G^{V_{X}\times_{0}V_{X}}/{\Gamma}^{V_{X}\times_{0}V_{X}}},

where
m_{G^{V_{X}\times_{0}V_{X}}/{\Gamma}^{V_{X}\times_{0}V_{X}}}
is the Haar measure of
G^{V_{X}\times_{0}V_{X}}/{\Gamma}^{V_{X}\times_{0}V_{X}}
. By (
7.4
),

(7.7)

\begin{split}&\quad\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{x}\in\Omega\times_{i}\Omega}(\omega\times_{i}\omega)(\mathbb{x})=\frac{1}{W_{\rho,\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{x}\in\Omega\times_{i}\Omega}\tilde{\Phi}_{\rho,\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}\times_{0}\tilde{\Phi}_{\rho,\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}(\mathbb{x})\\
&=\frac{1}{(S_{\rho,\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}+O_{C_{0},C,d,\epsilon^{\prime\prime},\rho}(\delta))^{2}}(S^{\prime}_{\rho,\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}+\delta\|\tilde{\Phi}_{\rho,\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}\times_{0}\tilde{\Phi}_{\rho,\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}\|_{\text{Lip}(G^{V_{X}\times_{0}V_{X}}/\Gamma^{V_{X}\times_{0}V_{X}})})\\
&=(S^{\prime}_{\rho,\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}+O_{C_{0},C,d,\epsilon^{\prime\prime},\rho}(\delta))(S_{\rho,\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}+O_{C_{0},C,d,\epsilon^{\prime\prime},\rho}(\delta))^{-2}.\end{split}

For convenience we write
A=o_{c\to\infty;\mathcal{C}}(B)
if for any
\epsilon>0
, there exists
K>0
depending only on
\epsilon
and the parameters in
\mathcal{C}
such that
|A|\leq\epsilon|B|
for all
c\geq K
.
If we can show that

(7.8)

\begin{split}(1+O(\epsilon^{\prime}))(1+o_{R\to\infty;C_{0},C,d}(1))S_{\rho,\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}^{2}=S^{\prime}_{\rho,\epsilon^{\prime\prime}},\end{split}

then by choosing first
R
being sufficiently small depending on
C_{0},C,d,\epsilon,\epsilon^{\prime\prime}
, then
\rho
being sufficiently small depending on
C_{0},C,d,\epsilon,\epsilon^{\prime\prime},R
, and then
\delta
being sufficiently small depending on
C_{0},C,d,\epsilon,\epsilon^{\prime\prime},R,\rho
, and finally
p
being sufficiently large depending on
C_{0},C,d,

\epsilon,\epsilon^{\prime\prime},\mathcal{F},R,\rho,\delta
,
we deduce from (
7.5
), (
7.7
) and (
7.8
) that Condition (iii) holds.

So it suffices to prove (
7.8
). The argument is similar to Claim 6.4 of
[
9
]
. For
i\in\mathbb{N}
,
Denote
H_{i}:=\{(g_{0},\dots,g_{2k+2s-1})\in G_{(i)}^{V_{X}}\times G_{(i)}^{V_{X}}\colon g_{0}=g_{k+s}\}

and let
H:=H_{1}
. Then
(H_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
is an
\mathbb{N}
-filtration of
H
.
It is not hard to see that
there exists a natural
\mathbb{N}
-filtered isomorphism
\psi\colon G^{V_{X}\times_{0}V_{X}}/\Gamma^{V_{X}\times_{0}V_{X}}\to H/((\Gamma^{V_{X}}\times\Gamma^{V_{X}})\cap H)
induced by

\psi(g_{0},\dots,g_{2k+2s-2}):=(g_{0},\dots,g_{k+s-1},g_{0},g_{k+s},\dots,g_{2k+2s-2})

for all
(g_{0},\dots,g_{2k+2s-2})\in G^{V_{X}\times_{0}V_{X}}
.
For the rest of the proof, for convenience all the integrals are assumed to be taken with respect to the Haar measure of space.
Then we may rewrite
S^{\prime}_{\rho,\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}
as

S^{\prime}_{\rho,\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}=\int_{(\mathbb{x},\mathbb{x}^{\prime})\in(G^{V_{X}}\times G^{V_{X}})/(\Gamma^{V_{X}}\times\Gamma^{V_{X}}),x_{0}=x^{\prime}_{0}}\tilde{\Phi}_{\rho,\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}(\mathbb{x})\tilde{\Phi}_{\rho,\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}(\mathbb{x}^{\prime}),

where we write
\mathbb{x}=(x_{0},\dots,x_{k+s-1})
and
\mathbb{x}^{\prime}=(x^{\prime}_{0},\dots,x^{\prime}_{k+s-1})
.
For any
h
in
B_{R}:=\{h\in G\colon d_{G}(h,id_{G})\leq R\}
, by (
7.3
), we have that

\int_{(\mathbb{x},\mathbb{x}^{\prime})\in(G^{V_{X}}\times G^{V_{X}})/(\Gamma^{V_{X}}\times\Gamma^{V_{X}}),x_{0}=hx^{\prime}_{0}}\tilde{\Phi}_{(1+3\epsilon^{\prime\prime})\rho,\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}(\mathbb{x})\tilde{\Phi}_{(1+3\epsilon^{\prime\prime})\rho,\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}(\mathbb{x}^{\prime})\geq S^{\prime}_{\rho^{\prime},\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}.

Integrating both side over
B_{R}
, we deduce that

\int_{(\mathbb{x},\mathbb{x}^{\prime})\in(G^{V_{X}}\times G^{V_{X}})/(\Gamma^{V_{X}}\times\Gamma^{V_{X}})}\lambda(\mathbb{x},\mathbb{x}^{\prime})\tilde{\Phi}_{(1+3\epsilon^{\prime\prime})\rho,\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}(\mathbb{x})\tilde{\Phi}_{(1+3\epsilon^{\prime\prime})\rho,\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}(\mathbb{x}^{\prime})\geq m_{G}(B_{R})S^{\prime}_{\rho^{\prime},\epsilon^{\prime\prime}},

where
\lambda(\mathbb{x},\mathbb{x}^{\prime})
is the number of
h\in B_{R}
such that
x_{0}\Gamma=hx^{\prime}_{0}\Gamma
and
m_{G}
is the Haar measure of
G
. In other words,
\lambda(\mathbb{x},\mathbb{x}^{\prime})
equals to the cardinality of the set
\Gamma\cap x_{0}^{-1}B_{R}x^{\prime}_{0}
. Choose any representatives of
x_{0}
and
x_{0}^{\prime}
in some fundamental domain of complexity
O_{C_{0},C,d}(1)
, and use a volume-packing argument and some simple geometry, one can show that

\lambda(\mathbb{x},\mathbb{x}^{\prime})=m_{G}(B_{R})(1+o_{R\to\infty;C_{0},C,d}(1)).

Comparing with the above we have

(1+o_{R\to\infty;C_{0},C,k,s}(1))S_{(1+3\epsilon^{\prime\prime})\rho,\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}^{2}\geq S^{\prime}_{\rho,\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}.

So by (
7.6
),
we have that

(1+\epsilon^{\prime})^{2}(1+o_{R\to\infty;C_{0},C,d}(1))S_{\rho,\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}^{2}\geq S^{\prime}_{\rho,\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}.

This proves one side of (
7.8
), and the proof for the other side is similar.
We are done.
∎

We now continue to prove Theorem
3.1
.
First note that

(7.9)

\begin{split}&\quad\|f_{\text{str}}\|_{L^{1}(V(M-r))}\geq\|\mathbb{1}_{E}\|_{L^{1}(V(M-r))}-\|f_{\text{uni}}\|_{L^{1}(V(M-r))}-\|f_{\text{err}}\|_{L^{1}(V(M-r))}\\
&\geq\|\mathbb{1}_{E}\|_{L^{1}(V(M-r))}-\|f_{\text{uni}}\|_{U^{s}(V(M-r))}-\|f_{\text{err}}\|_{L^{2}(V(M-r))}\geq\epsilon-\mathcal{F}(C)^{-1}-\epsilon^{\prime}.\end{split}

Let
f^{\prime}:=f_{\text{str}}+f_{\text{err}}
.
By Theorem
4.2
and the triangle inequality, if
d\geq d_{0}(X)
, then

(7.10)

\begin{split}|\mathbb{E}_{(x_{0},\dots,x_{k+s-1})\in\Omega}\mathbb{1}_{E}^{\otimes(k+s)}(x_{0},\dots,x_{k+s-1})-\mathbb{E}_{(x_{0},\dots,x_{k+s-1})\in\Omega}{f^{\prime}}^{\otimes(k+s)}(x_{0},\dots,x_{k+s-1})|\leq 2^{k+s}\mathcal{F}(C)^{-1}\end{split}

provided that
p\gg_{C,d,\mathcal{F}}1
.

By Proposition
7.1
, if
\mathcal{F}
grows sufficiently fast depending only on
C_{0},d,\epsilon^{\prime}
(which will be eventually dependent only on
C_{0},d,\epsilon
since we will choose
\epsilon^{\prime}
to be dependent only on these quantities) and
p\gg_{C_{0},C,d,\epsilon^{\prime},\mathcal{F}}1
, then there exists a function
\omega\colon\Omega\to\mathbb{R}
such that the following holds:

(1)

0\leq\omega(\mathbb{x})\leq O_{C_{0},C,d,\epsilon^{\prime}}(1)
for all
\mathbb{x}\in\Omega
;

(2)

\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{x}\in\Omega}\omega(\mathbb{x})=1
;

(3)

|\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{x}\in\Omega\times_{i}\Omega}(\omega\times_{i}\omega)(\mathbb{x})-1|\leq O_{C_{0},d}(\epsilon^{\prime})
for all
0\leq i\leq k+s-1
;

(4)

|f_{\text{str}}(x_{i})-f_{\text{str}}(x_{i^{\prime}})|\leq\epsilon^{\prime}
for all
0\leq i,i^{\prime}\leq k+s-1
if
\omega(x_{0},\dots,x_{k+s-1})>0
.

Since
0\leq f^{\prime}\leq 1
and
0\leq\omega(\mathbb{x})\leq O_{C_{0},C,d,\epsilon^{\prime}}(1)
for all
\mathbb{x}\in\Omega
,

(7.11)

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{x}\in\Omega}{f^{\prime}}^{\otimes(k+s)}(\mathbb{x})\geq O_{C_{0},C,d,\epsilon^{\prime}}(1)^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{x}\in\Omega}{f^{\prime}}^{\otimes(k+s)}(\mathbb{x})\omega(\mathbb{x}).\end{split}

We need some definitions in order the apply Theorem
B.12
to the sets
\Omega
and
\Omega\times_{i}\Omega
.
For any generating set
I
of
X
,
by Lemma
3.4
(ii),
\Omega_{I}
is a nice and consistent
M
-set of total co-dimension
\frac{(k+2)(k+1)}{2}
.
So we may write
\Omega_{I}=V(\mathcal{J}_{I})
for some consistent
(M,k+1)
-family
\mathcal{J}_{I}\subseteq\mathbb{F}_{p}[(x_{i})_{i\in I}]
of total dimension
\frac{(k+2)(k+1)}{2}
. For any
i\in I
, let
(\mathcal{J}^{\prime}_{I,i},\mathcal{J}^{\prime\prime}_{I,i})
be an
\{x_{i}\}
-decomposition of
\mathcal{J}_{I}
. It is clear that

(7.12)

\begin{split}\text{the set of $x_{i}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}$ such that $(x_{i})_{i\in I}\in V(\mathcal{J}^{\prime}_{I,i})$ for all $(x_{i^{\prime}})_{i^{\prime}\in I\backslash\{i\}}\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}$ is equal to $V(M-r)$.}\end{split}

Similarly, for any generating set
I
of
X
and any
i\in I
,
by Lemma
6.3
(ii),
\Omega_{I}\times_{i}\Omega_{I}
is a nice and consistent
M
-set of total co-dimension
k^{2}+3k+1
.
So we may write
\Omega_{I}\times_{i}\Omega_{I}=V(\tilde{\mathcal{J}}_{I,i})
for some consistent
(M,2k+1)
-family
\tilde{\mathcal{J}}_{I,i}\subseteq\mathbb{F}_{p}[x_{i},(x^{+}_{i^{\prime}})_{i^{\prime}\in I\backslash\{i\}},(x^{-}_{i^{\prime}})_{i^{\prime}\in I\backslash\{i\}}]
of total dimension
k^{2}+3k+1
. Let
(\tilde{\mathcal{J}}^{\prime}_{I,i},\tilde{\mathcal{J}}^{\prime\prime}_{I,i})
be an
\{x_{i}\}
-decomposition of
\tilde{\mathcal{J}}_{I,i}
. It is clear that

(7.13)

\begin{split}\text{the set of $x_{i}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}$ such that $(x_{i},\mathbb{x}^{+},\mathbb{x}^{-})\in V(\tilde{\mathcal{J}}^{\prime}_{I,i})$ for all $\mathbb{x}^{+},\mathbb{x}^{-}\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}$ is equal to $V(M-r)$,}\end{split}

and that for all
x_{i}\in V(M-r)
,
\mathbb{x}^{+},\mathbb{x}^{-}\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}
,

(7.14)

\begin{split}(x,\mathbb{x}^{+},\mathbb{x}^{-})\in V(\tilde{\mathcal{J}}^{\prime\prime}_{I,i})\Leftrightarrow(x\downharpoonleft_{i\curvearrowright I}\mathbb{x}^{+}),(x\downharpoonleft_{i\curvearrowright I}\mathbb{x}^{-})\in V(\mathcal{J}^{\prime\prime}_{I,i}).\end{split}

For any functions
f_{0},\dots,f_{k+s-1}
taking values in
[0,1]
with
f_{i}=f_{\text{err}}
for some
0\leq i\leq k+s-1
, by Theorem
B.12
, Lemmas
3.4
and
6.3
, relations (
7.12
), (
7.13
), (
7.14
), and the Cauchy-Schwartz inequality, we have that (taking
I
to be any generating set containing
i
)

(7.15)

\begin{split}&\quad|\mathbb{E}_{(x_{0},\dots,x_{k+s-1})\in\Omega}f_{0}(x_{0})f_{1}(x_{1})\dots f_{k+s-1}(x_{k+s-1})\omega(x_{0},\dots,x_{k+s-1})|\\
&\leq\mathbb{E}_{(x_{0},\dots,x_{k+s-1})\in\Omega}|f_{\text{err}}(x_{i})|\cdot\omega(x_{0},\dots,x_{k+s-1})\\
&=\mathbb{E}_{(x_{i^{\prime}})_{i^{\prime}\in I}\in\Omega_{I}}|f_{\text{err}}(x_{i})|\cdot\omega(\mathbb{L}_{I}((x_{i^{\prime}})_{i^{\prime}\in I}))\\
&=\mathbb{E}_{x_{i}\in V(M-r)}|f_{\text{err}}(x_{i})|\cdot\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{x}^{\prime}\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}\colon(x_{i}\downharpoonleft_{i\curvearrowright I}\mathbb{x}^{\prime})\in V(\mathcal{J}^{\prime\prime}_{I,i})}\omega(\mathbb{L}_{I}(x_{i}\downharpoonleft_{i\curvearrowright I}\mathbb{x}^{\prime}))+O_{C_{0},C,d,\epsilon^{\prime}}(p^{-1/2})\\
&\leq(|\mathbb{E}_{x_{i}\in V(M-r)}|f_{\text{err}}(x_{i})|^{2})^{1/2}\cdot(|\mathbb{E}_{x_{i}\in V(M-r)}|\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{x}^{\prime}\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}\colon(x_{i}\downharpoonleft_{i\curvearrowright I}\mathbb{x}^{\prime})\in V(\mathcal{J}^{\prime\prime}_{I,i})}\omega(\mathbb{L}_{I}(x_{i}\downharpoonleft_{i\curvearrowright I}\mathbb{x}^{\prime}))|^{2})^{1/2}+\epsilon^{\prime}\\
&\leq\epsilon^{\prime}(\mathbb{E}_{x_{i}\in V(M-r)}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{x}^{\prime},\mathbb{x}^{\prime\prime}\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}\colon(x_{i}\downharpoonleft_{i\curvearrowright I}\mathbb{x}^{\prime}),(x_{i}\downharpoonleft_{i\curvearrowright I}\mathbb{x}^{\prime\prime})\in V(\mathcal{J}^{\prime\prime}_{I,i})}\omega(\mathbb{L}_{I}(x_{i}\downharpoonleft_{i\curvearrowright I}\mathbb{x}^{\prime}))\omega(\mathbb{L}_{I}(x_{i}\downharpoonleft_{i\curvearrowright I}\mathbb{x}^{\prime\prime})))^{1/2}+\epsilon^{\prime}\\
&=\epsilon^{\prime}(\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{x}\in\Omega_{I}\times_{i}\Omega_{I}}(\omega\times_{i}\omega)(\mathbb{L}^{\pm}_{i\curvearrowright I}(\mathbb{x}))+O_{C_{0},C,d,\epsilon^{\prime}}(p^{-1/2}))^{1/2}+\epsilon^{\prime}\\
&=\epsilon^{\prime}(\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{x}\in\Omega\times_{i}\Omega}(\omega\times_{i}\omega)(\mathbb{x})+O_{C_{0},C,d,\epsilon^{\prime}}(p^{-1/2}))^{1/2}+\epsilon^{\prime}\\
&\leq\epsilon^{\prime}(1+O_{C_{0},d}(\epsilon^{\prime})+O_{C_{0},C,d,\epsilon^{\prime}}(p^{-1/2}))^{1/2}+\epsilon^{\prime}\leq 3\epsilon^{\prime}\end{split}

provided that
\epsilon^{\prime}
is sufficiently small depending on
C_{0},d
and that
p\gg_{C_{0},C,d,\epsilon^{\prime}}1
.

Let
I
be any generating set of
X
containing 0. From now on we assume without loss of generality that
I=\{0,\dots,k\}
.
Since
\omega(\mathbb{x})>0
implies that
|f_{\text{str}}(x_{i})-f_{\text{str}}(x_{i^{\prime}})|\leq\epsilon^{\prime}
for all
0\leq i,i^{\prime}\leq k+s-1
, it follows from Theorem
B.12
, Lemma
3.4
and (
7.12
) that

(7.16)

\begin{split}&\quad|\mathbb{E}_{(x_{0},\dots,x_{k+s-1})\in\Omega}f_{\text{str}}(x_{0})f_{\text{str}}(x_{1})\dots f_{\text{str}}(x_{k+s-1})\omega(x_{0},\dots,x_{k+s-1})|\\
&\geq\mathbb{E}_{(x_{0},\dots,x_{k+s-1})\in\Omega}(f_{\text{str}}(x_{0})^{k+s}-(k+s)\epsilon^{\prime})\omega(x_{0},\dots,x_{k+s-1})\\
&=\mathbb{E}_{(x_{0},\dots,x_{k})\in\Omega_{I}}f_{\text{str}}(x_{0})^{k+s}\omega(\mathbb{L}_{I}(x_{0},\dots,x_{k}))-(k+s)\epsilon^{\prime}\\
&=\mathbb{E}_{x_{0}\in V(M-r)}f_{\text{str}}(x_{0})^{k+s}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{x}^{\prime}\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}\colon(x_{0},\mathbb{x}^{\prime})\in V(\mathcal{J}^{\prime\prime}_{I,0})}\omega(\mathbb{L}_{I}(x_{0},\mathbb{x}^{\prime}))-(k+s)\epsilon^{\prime}+O_{C_{0},C,d,\epsilon^{\prime}}(p^{-1/2}).\end{split}

By the construction of
\omega
, Theorem
B.12
and Lemma
3.4
, we have

(7.17)

\begin{split}&\quad\mathbb{E}_{x_{0}\in V(M-r)}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{x}^{\prime}\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}\colon(x_{0},\mathbb{x}^{\prime})\in V(\mathcal{J}^{\prime\prime}_{I,0})}\omega(\mathbb{L}_{I}(x_{0},\mathbb{x}^{\prime}))=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{x}\in\Omega_{I}}\omega(\mathbb{L}_{I}(\mathbb{x}))+O_{C_{0},C,d,\epsilon^{\prime}}(p^{-1/2})\\
&=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{x}\in\Omega}\omega(\mathbb{x})+O_{C_{0},C,d,\epsilon^{\prime}}(p^{-1/2})=1+O_{C_{0},C,d,\epsilon^{\prime}}(p^{-1/2}).\end{split}

So by the construction of
\omega
, Theorem
B.12
, Lemmas
3.4
and
6.3
, and relations (
7.12
), (
7.13
), (
7.14
), (
7.17
), we have that

\begin{split}&\quad\mathbb{E}_{x_{0}\in V(M-r)}|\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{x}^{\prime}\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}\colon(x_{0},\mathbb{x}^{\prime})\in V(\mathcal{J}^{\prime\prime}_{I,0})}\omega(\mathbb{L}_{I}(x_{0},\mathbb{x}^{\prime}))-1|^{2}\\
&=\mathbb{E}_{x_{0}\in V(M-r)}|\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{x}^{\prime}\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}\colon(x_{0},\mathbb{x}^{\prime})\in V(\mathcal{J}^{\prime\prime}_{I,0})}\omega(\mathbb{L}_{I}(x_{0},\mathbb{x}^{\prime}))|^{2}+1-2\mathbb{E}_{x_{0}\in V(M-r)}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{x}^{\prime}\colon(x_{0},\mathbb{x}^{\prime})\in V(\mathcal{J}^{\prime\prime}_{I,0})}\omega(\mathbb{L}_{I}(x_{0},\mathbb{x}^{\prime}))\\
&=\mathbb{E}_{x_{0}\in V(M-r)}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{x}^{\prime},\mathbb{x}^{\prime\prime}\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}\colon(x_{0},\mathbb{x}^{\prime})(x_{0},\mathbb{x}^{\prime\prime})\in V(\mathcal{J}^{\prime\prime}_{I,0})}\omega(\mathbb{L}_{I}(x_{0},\mathbb{x}^{\prime}))\omega(\mathbb{L}_{I}(x_{0},\mathbb{x}^{\prime\prime}))-1+O_{C_{0},C,d,\epsilon^{\prime}}(p^{-1/2})\\
&=\mathbb{E}_{(x_{0},\mathbb{x}^{\prime},\mathbb{x}^{\prime\prime})\in\Omega_{I}\times_{0}\Omega_{I}}(\omega\times_{0}\omega)(\mathbb{L}^{\pm}_{0\curvearrowright I}(x_{0},\mathbb{x}^{\prime},\mathbb{x}^{\prime\prime}))-1+O_{C_{0},C,d,\epsilon^{\prime}}(p^{-1/2})\\
&=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{x}\in\Omega\times_{0}\Omega}(\omega\times_{0}\omega)(\mathbb{x})-1+O_{C_{0},C,d,\epsilon^{\prime}}(p^{-1/2})\\
&=O_{C_{0},d}(\epsilon^{\prime})+O_{C_{0},C,d,\epsilon^{\prime}}(p^{-1/2})=O_{C_{0},d}(\epsilon^{\prime}).\end{split}

So by the Pigeonhole Principle, there exists a subset
U
of
V(M-r)
with
|U|=O_{C_{0},d}({\epsilon^{\prime}}^{1/3}|V(M-r)|)
such that for all
x_{0}\in V(M-r)\backslash U
,

(7.18)

\begin{split}|\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{x}^{\prime}\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}\colon(x_{0},\mathbb{x}^{\prime})\in V(\mathcal{J}^{\prime\prime}_{I,0})}\omega(\mathbb{L}_{I}(x_{0},\mathbb{x}^{\prime}))-1|=O_{C_{0},d}({\epsilon^{\prime}}^{1/3}).\end{split}

It then follows from (
7.9
), (
7.16
), (
7.18
) and the Cauchy-Schwartz inequality that

(7.19)

\begin{split}&\quad|\mathbb{E}_{(x_{0},\dots,x_{k+s-1})\in\Omega}f_{\text{str}}(x_{0})f_{\text{str}}(x_{1})\dots f_{\text{str}}(x_{k+s-1})\omega(x_{0},\dots,x_{k+s-1})|\\
&\geq\mathbb{E}_{x_{0}\in V(M-r)}\mathbb{1}_{V(M-r)\backslash U}(x_{0})f_{\text{str}}(x_{0})^{k+s}(1+O_{C_{0},d}({\epsilon^{\prime}}^{1/3}))+O_{C_{0},d}({\epsilon^{\prime}})\\
&=\mathbb{E}_{x_{0}\in V(M-r)}f_{\text{str}}(x_{0})^{k+s}+O_{C_{0},d}({\epsilon^{\prime}}^{1/3})\\
&\geq(\mathbb{E}_{x_{0}\in V(M-r)}f_{\text{str}}(x_{0}))^{k+s}+O_{C_{0},d}({\epsilon^{\prime}}^{1/3})\\
&\geq(\epsilon-\mathcal{F}(C)^{-1}-\epsilon^{\prime})^{k+s}+O_{C_{0},d}({\epsilon^{\prime}}^{1/3})\end{split}

Combining (
7.10
), (
7.11
), (
7.15
) and (
7.19
), we have that

\begin{split}&\quad\mathbb{E}_{(x_{0},\dots,x_{k+s-1})\in\Omega}\mathbb{1}_{E}(x_{0})\mathbb{1}_{E}(x_{1})\dots\mathbb{1}_{E}(x_{k+s-1})\\
&\geq O_{C_{0},C,d,\epsilon^{\prime}}(1)^{-1}\Bigl((\epsilon-\mathcal{F}(C)^{-1}-\epsilon^{\prime})^{k+s}+O_{C_{0},d}({\epsilon^{\prime}}^{1/3})\Bigr)-2^{k+s}\mathcal{F}(C)^{-1}.\end{split}

If we first chose
\epsilon^{\prime}
to be sufficiently small depending on
C_{0},d,\epsilon
, then
\mathcal{F}
grow sufficiently fast depending on
C_{0},d,\epsilon
, and
then
p
sufficiently large depending on
C_{0},C,d,\epsilon,\mathcal{F}
, we will derive a lower bound

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}_{(x_{0},\dots,x_{k+s-1})\in\Omega}\mathbb{1}_{E}(x_{0})\mathbb{1}_{E}(x_{1})\dots\mathbb{1}_{E}(x_{k+s-1})>\delta(C_{0},C,d,\epsilon)>0.\end{split}

This proves (
7.12
) since
C
is dependent on
d,\epsilon^{\prime},\mathcal{F}
and thus on
C_{0},d,\epsilon
. This finishes the proof of Theorem
3.1
.

Appendix A
Background materials for polynomials and nilmanifolds

In this appendix, we recall the definitions in
[
20
]
on polynomials and nilmanifolds.

A.1.
Polynomials

Definition A.1

(Polynomials in finite field)
.

Let
\text{poly}(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}^{d^{\prime}}_{p})
be the collection of all functions
f\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
of the form

(A.1)

f(n_{1},\dots,n_{d})=\sum_{0\leq a_{1},\dots,a_{d}\leq p-1,a_{i}\in\mathbb{N}}C_{a_{1},\dots,a_{d}}n^{a_{1}}_{1}\dots n^{a_{d-1}}_{d}

for some
C_{a_{1},\dots,a_{d}}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d^{\prime}}
.
Let
f\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}^{d^{\prime}}_{p})
be a function which is not constant zero.
The
degree of
f
(denoted as
\deg(f)
) is the largest
r\in\mathbb{N}
such that
C_{a_{1},\dots,a_{d}}\neq\mathbb{0}
for some
a_{1}+\dots+a_{d}=r
. We say that
f
is
homogeneous of degree
r
if
C_{a_{1},\dots,a_{d}}\neq\mathbb{0}
implies that
a_{1}+\dots+a_{d}=r
. We say that
f
is a
linear transformation
if
f
is homogeneous of degree 1.

Convention A.2
.

For convenience, the
degree
of the constant zero function is allowed to take any integer value. For example, 0 can be regarded as a homogeneous polynomial of degree 10, as a linear transformation, or as a polynomial of degree -1. Here we also adopt the convention the only polynomial of negative degree is 0.

Definition A.3

(Partially
p
-periodic polynomials)
.

For any subset
R
of
\mathbb{R}
,
let
\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to R)
be the collection of all polynomials in
\mathbb{Z}^{d}
taking values in
R
. For any prime
p
, subset
\Omega
of
\mathbb{Z}^{d}
, and sets
R^{\prime}\subseteq R\subseteq\mathbb{R}
, let
\text{poly}(\Omega\to R|R^{\prime})
denote the set of all
f\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to R)
such that
f(n)\in R
for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
and that
f(n)\in R^{\prime}
for all
n\in\Omega+p\mathbb{Z}^{d}
.
Let
\text{poly}_{p}(\Omega\to R|R^{\prime})
denote the set of all
f\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to R)
such that
f(n)\in R
for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
and that
f(n+pm)-f(n)\in R^{\prime}
for all
n\in\Omega+p\mathbb{Z}^{d}
and
m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
.

A polynomial
f
in
\text{poly}(\Omega\to\mathbb{R}|\mathbb{Z})
is called a
partially
p
-periodic polynomial on
\Omega
.

A.2.
Nilmanifolds, filtrations and polynomial sequences

Let
G
be a group and
g,h\in G
. Denote
[g,h]:=g^{-1}h^{-1}gh
. For subgroups
H,H^{\prime}
of
G
, let
[H,H^{\prime}]
denote the group generated by
[h,h^{\prime}]
for all
h\in H
and
h^{\prime}\in H^{\prime}
.

Definition A.4

(Filtered group)
.

Let
G
be a group. An
\mathbb{N}
-filtration
on
G
is a collection
G_{\mathbb{N}}=(G_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
of subgroups of
G
indexed by
\mathbb{N}
with
G_{0}=G
such that the following holds:

(1)

for all
i,j\in\mathbb{N}
with
i\leq j
, we have that
G_{i}\supseteq G_{j}
;

(2)

for all
i,j\in\mathbb{N}
, we have
[G_{i},G_{j}]\subseteq G_{i+j}
.

For
s\in\mathbb{N}
, we say that
G
is an
(
\mathbb{N}
-filtered) nilpotent group
of
degree
at most
s
(or of
degree

\leq s
) with respect to some
\mathbb{N}
-filtration
(G_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
if
G_{i}
is trivial whenever
i>s
.
13
13

13

Unlike the convention in literature, for our convenience, we do not require
G_{0}=G
to be the same as
G_{1}
.

Definition A.5

(Nilmanifold)
.

Let
\Gamma
be a discrete and cocompact subgroup of a connected, simply-connected nilpotent Lie group
G
with filtration
G_{\mathbb{N}}=(G_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
such that
\Gamma_{i}:=\Gamma\cap G_{i}
is a cocompact subgroup of
G_{i}
14
14

14

In some papers, such
\Gamma_{i}
is called a
rational
subgroup of
G
.
for all
i\in\mathbb{N}
.
Then we say that
G/\Gamma
is an
(
\mathbb{N}
-filtered) nilmanifold
, and we use
(G/\Gamma)_{\mathbb{N}}
to denote the collection
(G_{i}/\Gamma_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
(which is called the
\mathbb{N}
-filtration
of
G/\Gamma
). We say that
G/\Gamma
has degree
\leq s
with respect to
(G/\Gamma)_{\mathbb{N}}
if
G
has degree
\leq s
with respect to
G_{\mathbb{N}}
.

Definition A.6

(Sub-nilmanifold)
.

Let
G/\Gamma
be an
\mathbb{N}
-filtered nilmanifold of degree
\leq s
with filtration
G_{\mathbb{N}}
and
H
be a rational subgroup of
G
. Then
H/(H\cap\Gamma)
is also an
\mathbb{N}
-filtered nilmanifold of degree
\leq s
with the filtration
H_{\mathbb{N}}
given by
H_{i}:=G_{i}\cap H
for all
i\in\mathbb{N}
(see Example 6.14 of
[
12
]
). We say that
H/(H\cap\Gamma)
is a
sub-nilmanifold
of
G/\Gamma
,
H_{\mathbb{N}}
(or
(H/(H\cap\Gamma))_{\mathbb{N}}
) is the filtration
induced by

G_{\mathbb{N}}
(or
(G/\Gamma)_{\mathbb{N}}
).

Definition A.7

(Quotient nilmanifold)
.

Let
G/\Gamma
be an
\mathbb{N}
-filtered nilmanifold of degree
\leq s
with filtration
G_{\mathbb{N}}
and
H
be a normal subgroup of
G
. Then
(G/H)/(\Gamma/(\Gamma\cap H))
is also an
\mathbb{N}
-filtered nilmanifold of most
\leq s
with the filtration
(G/H)_{\mathbb{N}}
given by
(G/H)_{i}:=G_{i}/(H\cap G_{i})
for all
i\in\mathbb{N}
. We say that
(G/H)/(\Gamma/(\Gamma\cap H))
is the
quotient nilmanifold
of
G/\Gamma
by
H
and that
(G/H)_{\mathbb{N}}
is the filtration
induced by

G_{\mathbb{N}}
.

Definition A.8

(Product nilmanifold)
.

Let
G/\Gamma
and
G^{\prime}/\Gamma^{\prime}
be
\mathbb{N}
-filtered nilmanifolds of degree
\leq s
with filtration
G_{\mathbb{N}}
and
G^{\prime}_{\mathbb{N}}
. Then
G\times G^{\prime}/\Gamma\times\Gamma^{\prime}
is also an
\mathbb{N}
-filtered nilmanifold of most
\subseteq J
with the filtration
(G\times G^{\prime}/\Gamma\times\Gamma^{\prime})_{\mathbb{N}}
given by
(G\times G^{\prime}/\Gamma\times\Gamma^{\prime})_{i}:=G_{i}\times G^{\prime}_{i}/\Gamma_{i}\times\Gamma^{\prime}_{i}
for all
i\in\mathbb{N}
. We say that
G\times G^{\prime}/\Gamma\times\Gamma^{\prime}
is the
product nilmanifold
of
G/\Gamma
and
G^{\prime}/\Gamma^{\prime}
and that
(G\times G^{\prime}/\Gamma\times\Gamma^{\prime})_{\mathbb{N}}
is the filtration
induced by

G_{\mathbb{N}}
and
G^{\prime}_{\mathbb{N}}
.

Every nilmanifold has an explicit algebraic description by using the Mal’cev basis:

Definition A.9

(Mal’cev basis)
.

Let
s\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
G/\Gamma
be a nilmanifold of step at most
s
with the
\mathbb{N}
-filtration
(G_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
. Let
\dim(G)=m
and
\dim(G_{i})=m_{i}
for all
0\leq i\leq s
. A basis
\mathcal{X}:=\{X_{1},\dots,X_{m}\}
for the Lie algebra
\log G
of
G
(over
\mathbb{R}
) is a
Mal’cev basis
for
G/\Gamma
adapted to the filtration
G_{\mathbb{N}}
if

•

for all
0\leq j\leq m-1
,
\log H_{j}:=\text{Span}_{\mathbb{R}}\{\xi_{j+1},\dots,\xi_{m}\}
is a Lie algebra ideal of
\log G
and so
H_{j}:=\exp(\log H_{j})
is a normal Lie subgroup of
G;

•

G_{i}=H_{m-m_{i}}
for all
0\leq i\leq s
;

•

the map
\psi^{-1}\colon\mathbb{R}^{m}\to G
given by

\psi^{-1}(t_{1},\dots,t_{m})=\exp(t_{1}X_{1})\dots\exp(t_{m}X_{m})

is a bijection;

•

\Gamma=\psi^{-1}(\mathbb{Z}^{m})
.

We call
\psi
the
Mal’cev coordinate map
with respect to the Mal’cev basis
\mathcal{X}
.
If
g=\psi^{-1}(t_{1},\dots,t_{m})
, we say that
(t_{1},\dots,t_{m})
are the
Mal’cev coordinates
of
g
with respect to
\mathcal{X}
.

We say that the Mal’cev basis
\mathcal{X}
is
C
-rational
(or of
complexity
at most
C
) if all the structure constants
c_{i,j,k}
in the relations

[X_{i},X_{j}]=\sum_{k}c_{i,j,k}X_{k}

are rational with complexity at most
C
.

It is known that for every filtration
G_{\bullet}
which is rational for
\Gamma
, there exists a Mal’cev basis adapted to it. See for example the discussion on pages 11–12 of
[
10
]
.

We use the following quantities to describe the complexities of the objected defined above.

Definition A.10

(Notions of complexities for nilmanifolds)
.

Let
G/\Gamma
be a nilmanifold with filtration
G_{I}
and a Mal’cev basis
\mathcal{X}=\{X_{1},\dots,X_{D}\}
adapted to it.
We say that
G/\Gamma
is of
complexity
at most
C
if the Mal’cev basis
\mathcal{X}
is
C
-rational and
\dim(G)\leq C
.

An element
g\in G
is of
complexity
at most
C
(with respect to the Mal’cev coordinate map
\psi\colon G/\Gamma\to\mathbb{R}^{m}
) if
\psi(g)\in[-C,C]^{m}
.

Let
G^{\prime}/\Gamma^{\prime}
be a nilmanifold endowed with the Mal’cev basis
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}=\{X^{\prime}_{1},\dots,X^{\prime}_{D^{\prime}}\}
respectively. Let
\phi\colon G/\Gamma\to G^{\prime}/\Gamma^{\prime}
be a filtered homomorphism, we say that
\phi
is of
complexity
at most
C
if the map
X_{i}\to\sum_{j}a_{i,j}X^{\prime}_{j}
induced by
\phi
is such that all
a_{i,j}
are of complexity at most
C
.

Let
G^{\prime}\subseteq G
be a closed connected subgroup. We say that
G^{\prime}
is
C
-rational
(or of
complexity
at most
C
) relative to
\mathcal{X}
if the Lie algebra
\log G
has a basis consisting of linear combinations
\sum_{i}a_{i}X_{i}
such that
a_{i}
are rational numbers of complexity at most
C
.

Convention A.11
.

Throughout this paper, all nilmanifolds are assumed to have a fixed filtration, Mal’cev basis and a smooth Riemannian metric induced by the Mal’cev basis. Therefore, we will simply say that a nilmanifold, Lipschitz function, sub-nilmanifold etc. is of complexity
C
without mentioning the reference filtration and Mal’cev basis.

Definition A.12

(Polynomial sequences)
.

Let
d,k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
and
G
be a connected simply-connected nilpotent Lie group. Let
(G_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
be an
\mathbb{N}
-filtration of
G
. A map
g\colon\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to G
is a
(
\mathbb{N}
-filtered)
d
-integral polynomial sequence
if

\Delta_{h_{m}}\dots\Delta_{h_{1}}g(n)\in G_{m}

for all
m\in\mathbb{N}
and
n,h_{1},\dots,h_{m}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
.
15
15

15

Recall that
\Delta_{h}g(n):=g(n+h)g(n)^{-1}

for all
n,h\in H
.

The set of all
\mathbb{N}
-filtered polynomial sequences is denoted by
\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to G_{\mathbb{N}})
.

By Corollary B.4 of
[
12
]
,
\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{k}\to G_{\mathbb{N}})
is a group with respect to the pointwise multiplicative operation.
We refer the readers to Appendix B of
[
12
]
for more properties for polynomial sequences.

Definition A.13

(Partially periodic polynomial sequences)
.

Let
k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
G/\Gamma
be an
\mathbb{N}
-filtered nilmanifold,
p
be a prime, and
\Omega
be a subset of
\mathbb{Z}^{k}
.
Let
\text{poly}(\Omega\to G_{\mathbb{N}}|\Gamma)
denote the set of all
g\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{k}\to G_{\mathbb{N}})
such that
g(n)\in\Gamma
for all
n\in\Omega
, and
\text{poly}_{p}(\Omega\to G_{\mathbb{N}}|\Gamma)
denote the set of all
g\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{k}\to G_{\mathbb{N}})
such that
g(n+pm)^{-1}g(n)\in\Gamma
for all
n\in\Omega
and
m\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}
.

Let

\text{poly}(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{k}\to G_{\mathbb{N}})
denote the set of all functions of the form
f\circ\tau
for some
f\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{k}\to G_{\mathbb{N}})
.
For a subset
\Omega
of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{k}
, let

\text{poly}_{p}(\Omega\to G_{\mathbb{N}}|\Gamma)
denote the set of all functions of the form
f\circ\tau
for some
f\in\text{poly}_{p}(\iota^{-1}(\Omega)\to G_{\mathbb{N}}|\Gamma)
. We define
\text{poly}(\Omega\to G_{\mathbb{N}}|\Gamma)
similarly.

For
\Omega\subseteq\mathbb{Z}^{k}

or
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{k}
, we call functions in
\text{poly}_{p}(\Omega\to G_{\mathbb{N}}|\Gamma)

partially
p
-periodic polynomial sequences on
\Omega
.

Recall from
[
20
]
that
\text{poly}_{p}(\Omega\to G_{\mathbb{N}}|\Gamma)
is not closed under multiplication. However, we have

Lemma A.14

(Lemma 3.17 of
[
20
]
)
.

Let
k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
G/\Gamma
be an
\mathbb{N}
-filtered nilmanifold,
p
be a prime, and
\Omega
be a subset of
\mathbb{Z}^{k}
. For all
f\in\text{poly}_{p}(\Omega\to G_{\mathbb{N}}|\Gamma)
and
g\in\text{poly}(\Omega\to G_{\mathbb{N}}|\Gamma)
, we have that
fg\in\text{poly}_{p}(\Omega\to G_{\mathbb{N}}|\Gamma)
.

A.3.
The Baker-Campbell-Hausdorff formula

The material of this section comes from Appendix C of
[
9
]
. We write it down for completeness.

Let
G
be a group,
t\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
and
g_{1},\dots,g_{t}\in G
. The
iterated commutator
of
g_{1}
is defined to be
g_{1}
itself. Iteratively, we define an
iterated commutator
of
g_{1},\dots,g_{t}
to be an element of the form
[w,w^{\prime}]
, where
w
is an iterated commutator of
g_{i_{1}},\dots,g_{i_{r}}
,
w^{\prime}
is an iterated commutator of
g_{i^{\prime}_{1}},\dots,g_{i^{\prime}_{r^{\prime}}}
for some
1\leq r,r^{\prime}\leq t-1
with
r+r^{\prime}=t
and
\{i_{1},\dots,i_{r}\}\cup\{i^{\prime}_{1},\dots,i^{\prime}_{r^{\prime}}\}=\{1,\dots,t\}
.

Similarly, let
X_{1},\dots,X_{t}
be elements of a Lie algebra. The
iterated Lie bracket
of
X_{1}
is defined to be
X_{1}
itself. Iteratively, we define an
iterated Lie bracket
of
X_{1},\dots,X_{t}
to be an element of the form
[w,w^{\prime}]
, where
w
is an iterated Lie bracket of
X_{i_{1}},\dots,X_{i_{r}}
,
w^{\prime}
is an iterated Lie bracket of
X_{i^{\prime}_{1}},\dots,X_{i^{\prime}_{r^{\prime}}}
for some
1\leq r,r^{\prime}\leq t-1
with
r+r^{\prime}=t
and
\{i_{1},\dots,i_{r}\}\cup\{i^{\prime}_{1},\dots,i^{\prime}_{r^{\prime}}\}=\{1,\dots,t\}
.

Let
G
be a connected and simply connected nilpotent Lie group. The
Baker-Campbell-Hausdorff formula
asserts that for all
X_{1},X_{2}\in\log G
, we have

\exp(X_{1})\exp(X_{2})=\exp\Bigl(X_{1}+X_{2}+\frac{1}{2}[X_{1},X_{2}]+\prod_{\alpha}c_{\alpha}X_{\alpha}\Bigr),

where
\alpha
is a finite set of labels,
c_{\alpha}
are real constants, and
X_{\alpha}
are iterated Lie brackets with
k_{1,\alpha}
copies of
X_{1}
and
k_{2,\alpha}
copies of
X_{2}
for some
k_{1,\alpha},k_{2,\alpha}\geq 1
and
k_{1,\alpha}+k_{2,\alpha}\geq 3
. One may use this formula to show that for all
g_{1},g_{2}\in G
and
x\in\mathbb{R}
, we have that

(g_{1}g_{2})^{x}=g_{1}^{x}g_{2}^{x}\prod_{\alpha}g_{\alpha}^{Q_{\alpha}(x)},

where
\alpha
is a finite set of labels,
g_{\alpha}
are iterated commutators with
k_{1,\alpha}
copies of
g_{1}
and
k_{2,\alpha}
copies of
X_{2}
for some
k_{1,\alpha},k_{2,\alpha}\geq 1
, and
Q_{\alpha}\colon\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
are polynomials of degrees at most
k_{1,\alpha}+k_{2,\alpha}
without constant terms.

Similarly, one can show that for any
g_{1},g_{2}\in G
and
x_{1},x_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
, we have that

(A.2)

[g_{1}^{x_{1}},g_{2}^{x_{2}}]=[g_{1},g_{2}]^{x_{1}x_{2}}\prod_{\alpha}g_{\alpha}^{P_{\alpha}(x_{1},x_{2})},

where
\alpha
is a finite set of labels,
g_{\alpha}
are iterated commutators with
k_{1,\alpha}
copies of
g_{1}
and
g_{2,\alpha}
copies of
X_{2}
for some
k_{1,\alpha},k_{2,\alpha}\geq 1
,
k_{1,\alpha}+k_{2,\alpha}\geq 3
, and
P_{\alpha}\colon\mathbb{R}^{2}\to\mathbb{R}
are polynomials of degrees at most
k_{1,\alpha}
in
x_{1}
and at most
k_{2,\alpha}
in
x_{2}
which vanishes when
x_{1}x_{2}=0
.

A.4.
Type-I horizontal torus and character

Definition A.15

(Type-I horizontal torus and character)
.

Let
G/\Gamma
be a nilmanifold endowed with a Mal’cev basis
\mathcal{X}
. The
type-I horizontal torus
of
G/\Gamma
is
G/[G,G]\Gamma
.
A
type-I horizontal character
is a continuous homomorphism
\eta\colon G\to\mathbb{R}
such that
\eta(\Gamma)\subseteq\mathbb{Z}
.
When written in the coordinates relative to
\mathcal{X}
, we may write
\eta(g)=k\cdot\psi(g)
for some unique
k=(k_{1},\dots,k_{m})\in\mathbb{Z}^{m}
, where
\psi\colon G\to\mathbb{R}^{m}
is the coordinate map with respect to the Mal’cev basis
\mathcal{X}
. We call the quantity
\|\eta\|:=|k|=|k_{1}|+\dots+|k_{m}|
the
complexity
of
\eta
(with respect to
\mathcal{X}
).

It is not hard to see that any type-I horizontal character mod
\mathbb{Z}
vanishes on
[G,G]\Gamma
and thus descent to a continuous homomorphism between the type-I horizontal torus
G/[G,G]\Gamma
and
\mathbb{R}/\mathbb{Z}
. Moreover,
\eta\mod\mathbb{Z}
is a well defined map from
G/\Gamma
to
\mathbb{R}/\mathbb{Z}
.

It follows from Lemma 3.21 of
[
20
]
that
a function
g\colon\mathbb{Z}^{k}\to G
belongs to
\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{k}\to G_{\mathbb{N}})
if and only if

(A.3)

g(n)=\prod_{i\in\mathbb{N}^{k},|i|\leq s}g_{i}^{\binom{n}{i}}

for some
g_{i}\in G_{|i|}
(with any fixed order in
\mathbb{N}^{k}
), where
s
is the degree of
G
.
We call
g_{i}
the
(
i
-th) type-I Taylor coefficient
of
g
(which is uniquely determined by
g
if
g\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{k}\to G_{\mathbb{N}})
thanks to Lemma 3.21 of
[
20
]
), and (
A.3
) the
type-I Taylor expansion
of
g
.

Lemma A.16

(Lemma 3.28 of
[
20
]
)
.

Let
d,s\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
p\gg_{d,s}1
be a prime,
\Omega
be a non-empty
p
-periodic subset of
\mathbb{Z}^{d}
, and
\eta
be a type-I horizontal character on some
\mathbb{N}
-filtered degree
\leq s
nilmanifold
G/\Gamma
.
Then for all
g\in\text{poly}_{p}(\Omega\to G_{\mathbb{N}}|\Gamma)
,
p^{s}\eta\circ g(n)
takes values in
\mathbb{Z}+C
for some
C\in\mathbb{R}
.

Appendix B
Results for
M
-sets from previous papers

In this appendix, we collect some results proved in previous parts of the series
[
20
,
22
]
which are used in this paper. We refer the readers to Section
1.3
for the notation used in this section.

B.1.
The rank of quadratic forms

Lemma B.1

(Lemma 4.2 of
[
20
]
)
.

Let
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a quadratic form associated with the matrix
A
, and
x,y,z\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
. Suppose that
M(x)=M(x+y)=M(x+z)=0
. Then
M(x+y+z)=0
if and only if
(yA)\cdot z=0
.

Let
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a quadratic form associated with the matrix
A
and
V
be a subspace of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
. Let
V^{\perp_{M}}
denote the set of
\{n\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\colon(mA)\cdot n=0\text{ for all }m\in V\}
.
A subspace
V
of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
is
M
-isotropic
if
V\cap V^{\perp_{M}}\neq\{\mathbb{0}\}
.
We say that a tuple
(h_{1},\dots,h_{k})
of vectors in
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
is
M
-isotropic
if the span of
h_{1},\dots,h_{k}
is an
M
-isotropic subspace.
We say that a subspace or tuple of vectors is
M
-non-isotropic
if it is not
M
-isotropic.

Proposition B.2

(Proposition 4.8 of
[
20
]
)
.

Let
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a quadratic form and
V
be a subspace of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
of co-dimension
r
, and
c\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
.

(1)

We have
\dim(V\cap V^{\perp_{M}})\leq\min\{d-\text{rank}(M)+r,d-r\}
.

(2)

The rank of
M|_{V+c}
equals to
d-r-\dim(V\cap V^{\perp_{M}})
(i.e.
\dim(V)-\dim(V\cap V^{\perp_{M}})
).

(3)

The rank of
M|_{V+c}
is at most
d-r
and at least
\text{rank}(M)-2r
.

(4)

M|_{V+c}
is non-degenerate (i.e.
\text{rank}(M|_{V+c})=d-r
) if and only if
V
is not an
M
-isotropic subspace.

Lemma B.3

(Lemma 4.11 of
[
20
]
)
.

Let
d,k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
p
be a prime, and
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a non-degenerate quadratic form.

(1)

The number of tuples
(h_{1},\dots,h_{k})\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}
such that
h_{1},\dots,h_{k}
are linearly dependent is at most
kp^{(d+1)(k-1)}
.

(2)

The number of
M
-isotropic tuples
(h_{1},\dots,h_{k})\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}
is at most
O_{d,k}(p^{kd-1})
.

B.2.
Some basic counting properties

Lemma B.4

(Lemma 4.10 of
[
20
]
)
.

Let
P\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p})
be of degree at most
r
.
Then
|V(P)|\leq O_{d,r}(p^{d-1})
unless
P\equiv 0
.

Lemma B.5

(Corollary 4.14 of
[
20
]
)
.

Let
d\in\mathbb{N}_{+},r\in\mathbb{N}
and
p
be a prime number. Let
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a quadratic form and
V+c
be an affine subspace of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
of co-dimension
r
. If either
s:=\text{rank}(M|_{V+c})\geq 3
or
\text{rank}(M)-2r\geq 3
, then

|V(M)\cap(V+c)|=p^{d-r-1}(1+O(p^{-\frac{1}{2}})).

Lemma B.6

(Corollary 4.16 of
[
20
]
)
.

Let
d,r\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
and
p
be a prime number. Let
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a non-degenerate quadratic form and
h_{1},\dots,h_{r}
be linearly independent vectors.
If
d-2r\geq 3
, then

|V(M)^{h_{1},\dots,h_{r}}|=p^{d-r-1}(1+O(p^{-\frac{1}{2}})).

Lemma B.7

(Corollary C.7 of
[
20
]
)
.

Let
d\in\mathbb{N}_{+},r,s\in\mathbb{N}
and
p
be a prime. Let
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a quadratic form and
V+c
be an affine subspace of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
of co-dimension
r
. If either
\text{rank}(M|_{V+c})
or
\text{rank}(M)-2r
is at least
s^{2}+s+3
, then

|\Box_{s}(V(M)\cap(V+c))|=p^{(s+1)(d-r)-(\frac{s(s+1)}{2}+1)}(1+O_{s}(p^{-1/2})).

Lemma B.8

(Lemma 4.18 of
[
20
]
)
.

Let
s\in\mathbb{N}
,
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a quadratic form associated with the matrix
A
, and
V+c
be an affine subspace of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
of dimension
r
. For
n,h_{1},\dots,h_{s}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
, we have that
(n,h_{1},\dots,h_{s})\in\Box_{s}(V(M)\cap(V+c))
if and only if

•

n\in V+c
,
h_{1},\dots,h_{s}\in V
;

•

n\in V(M)^{h_{1},\dots,h_{s}}
;

•

(h_{i}A)\cdot h_{j}=0
for all
1\leq i,j\leq s,i\neq j
.

In particular, let
\phi\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{r}\to V
be any bijective linear transformation,
M^{\prime}(m):=M(\phi(m)+c)
. We have that
(n,h_{1},\dots,h_{s})\in\Box_{s}(V(M)\cap(V+c))
if and only if

(n,h_{1},\dots,h_{s})=(\phi(n^{\prime})+c,\phi(h^{\prime}_{1}),\dots,\phi(h^{\prime}_{s}))

for some
(n^{\prime},h^{\prime}_{1},\dots,h^{\prime}_{s})\in\Box_{s}(V(M^{\prime}))
.

B.3.
M
-families and
M
-sets

We say that a linear transformation
L\colon(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}\to(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k^{\prime}}
is
d
-integral
if there exist
a_{i,j}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}
for
1\leq i\leq k
and
1\leq j\leq k^{\prime}
such that

L(n_{1},\dots,n_{k})=\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{k}a_{i,1}n_{i},\dots,\sum_{i=1}^{k}a_{i,k^{\prime}}n_{i}\Bigr)

for all
n_{1},\dots,n_{k}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
.
Let
L\colon(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
be a
d
-integral linear transformation given by

L(n_{1},\dots,n_{k})=\sum_{i=1}^{k}a_{i}n_{i}
for some
a_{i}\in\mathbb{F}_{p},1\leq i\leq k
. We say that
L
is the
d
-integral linear transformation
induced
by
(a_{1},\dots,a_{k})\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{k}
.

Similarly, we say that a linear transformation
L\colon(\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{k}\to(\frac{1}{p}\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{k^{\prime}}
is
d
-integral
if there exist
a_{i,j}\in\mathbb{Z}/p
for
1\leq i\leq k
and
1\leq j\leq k^{\prime}
such that

L(n_{1},\dots,n_{k})=\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{k}a_{i,1}n_{i},\dots,\sum_{i=1}^{k}a_{i,k^{\prime}}n_{i}\Bigr)

for all
n_{1},\dots,n_{k}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
.

Let
d\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
p
be a prime,
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a quadratic form with
A
being the associated matrix. We say that a function
F\colon(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
is an
(M,k)
-integral quadratic function

if

(B.1)

F(n_{1},\dots,n_{k})=\sum_{1\leq i\leq j\leq k}b_{i,j}(n_{i}A)\cdot n_{j}+\sum_{1\leq i\leq k}v_{i}\cdot n_{i}+u

for some
b_{i,j},u\in\mathbb{F}_{p}
and
v_{i}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
.
We say that an
(M,k)
-integral quadratic function
F
is
pure

if
F
can be written in the form of (
B.1
) with
v_{1}=\dots=v_{k}=\mathbb{0}
.
We say that an
(M,k)
-integral quadratic function
F\colon(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
is
nice

if

F(n_{1},\dots,n_{k})=\sum_{1\leq i\leq k^{\prime}}b_{i}(n_{k^{\prime}}A)\cdot n_{i}+u

for some
0\leq k^{\prime}\leq k
,
b_{i},u\in\mathbb{F}_{p}
.

For
F
given in (
B.1
), denote

v_{M}(F):=(b_{k,k},b_{k,k-1},\dots,b_{k,1},v_{k},b_{k-1,k-1},\dots,b_{k-1,1},v_{k-1},\dots,b_{1,1},v_{1},u)\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{\binom{k+1}{2}+kd+1},

and

v^{\prime}_{M}(F):=(b_{k,k},b_{k,k-1},\dots,b_{k,1},v_{k},b_{k-1,k-1},\dots,b_{k-1,1},v_{k-1},\dots,b_{1,1},v_{1})\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{\binom{k+1}{2}+kd}.

Informally, we say that
b_{i,j}
is the
n_{i}n_{j}
-coefficient,
v_{i}
is the
n_{i}
-coefficient, and
u
is the constant term coefficient for these vectors.

An
(M,k)
-family
is a collections of
(M,k)
-integral quadratic functions.
Let
\mathcal{J}=\{F_{1},\dots
,
F_{r}\}
be an
(M,k)
-family.

•

We say that
\mathcal{J}
is
pure
if all of
F_{1},\dots,F_{r}
are pure.

•

We say that
\mathcal{J}
is
consistent
if
(0,\dots,0,1)
does not belong to the span of
v_{M}(F_{1}),

\dots,

v_{M}(F_{r})
, or equivalently, there is no linear combination of
F_{1},\dots,F_{r}
which is a constant nonzero function, or equivalently, for all
c_{1},\dots,c_{r}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}
, we have

c_{1}v^{\prime}_{M}(F_{1})+\dots+c_{r}v^{\prime}_{M}(F_{r})=\mathbb{0}\Rightarrow c_{1}v_{M}(F_{1})+\dots+c_{r}v_{M}(F_{r})=\mathbb{0}.

•

We say that
\mathcal{J}
is
independent
if
v^{\prime}_{M}(F_{1}),\dots,v^{\prime}_{M}(F_{r})
are linearly independent, or equivalently, there is no nontrivial linear combination of
F_{1},\dots,F_{r}
which is a constant function, or equivalently, for all
c_{1},\dots,c_{r}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}
, we have

c_{1}v^{\prime}_{M}(F_{1})+\dots+c_{r}v^{\prime}_{M}(F_{r})=\mathbb{0}\Rightarrow c_{1}=\dots=c_{r}=0.

•

We say that
\mathcal{J}
is
nice
if there exist some bijective
d
-integral linear transformation
L\colon(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}\to(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}
and some
v\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}
such that
F_{i}(L(\cdot)+v)
is nice for all
1\leq i\leq r
.

The dimension of the span of
v^{\prime}_{M}(F_{1}),\dots,v^{\prime}_{M}(F_{r})
is called the
dimension
of an
(M,k)
-family
\{F_{1},\dots,F_{r}\}
.

When there is no confusion, we call an
(M,k)
-family to be an
M
-family
for short.

We say that a subset
\Omega
of
(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}
is an
M
-set
if there exists an
(M,k)
-family
\{F_{i}\colon(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\colon 1\leq i\leq r\}

such that
\Omega=\cap_{i=1}^{r}V(F_{i})
.
We call either
\{F_{1},\dots,F_{r}\}
or the ordered set
(F_{1},\dots,F_{r})
an
M
-representation
of
\Omega
.

Let
\mathbb{P}\in\{\text{pure, nice, independent, consistent}\}
.
We say that
\Omega
is
\mathbb{P}
if one can choose the
M
-family
\{F_{1},\dots,F_{r}\}
to be
\mathbb{P}
.
We say that the
M
-representation
(F_{1},\dots,F_{r})
is
\mathbb{P}
if
\{F_{i}\colon 1\leq i\leq r\}
is
\mathbb{P}
.
We say that
r
is the
dimension
of the
M
-representation
(F_{1},\dots,F_{r})
.
The
total co-dimension
of a consistent
M
-set
\Omega
, denoted by
r_{M}(\Omega)
, is the minimum of the dimension of the independent
M
-representations of
\Omega
. It was shown in Proposition C.8 of
[
20
]
that
r_{M}(\Omega)
is independent of the choice of the independent
M
-representation if
d
and
p
are sufficiently large.

We say that an independent
M
-representation
(F_{1},\dots,F_{r})
of
\Omega
is
standard
if
the matrix

\begin{bmatrix}v_{M}(F_{1})\\
\dots\\
v_{M}(F_{r})\end{bmatrix}
is in the reduced row echelon form (or equivalently, the matrix

\begin{bmatrix}v^{\prime}_{M}(F_{1})\\
\dots\\
v^{\prime}_{M}(F_{r})\end{bmatrix}
is in the reduced row echelon form).
If
(F_{1},\dots,F_{r})
is a standard
M
-representation of
\Omega
, then we may relabeling
(F_{1},\dots,F_{r})
as

(F_{k,1},\dots,F_{k,r_{k}},F_{k-1,1},\dots,F_{k-1,r_{k-1}},\dots,F_{1,1},\dots,F_{1,r_{1}})

for some
r_{1},\dots,r_{k}\in\mathbb{N}
such that
F_{i,j}
is non-constant with respect to
n_{i}
and is independent of
n_{i+1},\dots,n_{k}
.
We also call
(F_{i,j}\colon 1\leq i\leq k,1\leq j\leq r_{i})
a
standard
M
-representation
of
\Omega
. The vector
(r_{1},\dots,r_{k})
is called the
dimension vector
of this representation.

Convention B.9
.

We allow
(M,k)
-families,
M
-representations and dimension vectors to be empty. In particular,
(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}
is considered as a nice and consistent
M
-set with total co-dimension zero.

Proposition B.10

(Proposition B.3 of
[
20
]
)
.

Let
d,k,r\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
p
be a prime,
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a quadratic form and
\mathcal{J}=\{F_{1},\dots,F_{r}\},F_{i}\colon(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}\to\mathbb{F}_{p},1\leq i\leq r
be an
(M,k)
-family.

(1)

For any subset
I\subseteq\{1,\dots,r\}
,
\{F_{i}\colon i\in I\}
is an
(M,k)
-family.

(2)

Let
I\subseteq\{1,\dots,r\}
be a subset such that all of
F_{i},i\in I
are independent of the last
d
-variables. Then writing
G_{i}(n_{1},\dots,n_{k-1}):=F_{i}(n_{1},\dots,n_{k-1})
, we have that
\{G_{i}\colon i\in I\}
is an
(M,k-1)
-family.

(3)

For any bijective
d
-integral linear transformation
L\colon(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}\to(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}
and any
v\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}
,
\{F_{1}(L(\cdot)+v),\dots,F_{r}(L(\cdot)+v)\},F_{i}\colon(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}\to\mathbb{F}_{p},1\leq i\leq r
is an
(M,k)
-family.

(4)

Let
1\leq k^{\prime}\leq k
and
1\leq r^{\prime}\leq r
be such that
F_{1},\dots,F_{r^{\prime}}
are independent of
n_{k-k^{\prime}+1},\dots,n_{r}
and that every nontrival linear combination of
F_{r^{\prime}+1},\dots,F_{r}
dependents nontrivially on some of
n_{k-k^{\prime}+1},\dots,n_{r}
.
Define
G_{i},H_{i}\colon(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k+k^{\prime}}\to\mathbb{F}_{p},1\leq i\leq r
by

\text{$G_{i}(n_{1},\dots,n_{k+k^{\prime}}):=F_{i}(n_{1},\dots,n_{k})$ and $H_{i}(n_{1},\dots,n_{k+k^{\prime}}):=F_{i}(n_{1},\dots,n_{k-k^{\prime}},n_{k+1},\dots,n_{k+k^{\prime}})$}.

Then
\{F_{1},\dots,F_{r^{\prime}},G_{r^{\prime}+1},\dots,G_{r},H_{r^{\prime}+1},\dots,H_{r}\}
16
16

16

Here we regard
F_{1},\dots,F_{r^{\prime}}
as functions of
n_{1},\dots,n_{k+k^{\prime}}
, which acutally depends only on
n_{1},\dots,n_{k-k^{\prime}}
.
is an
(M,k+k^{\prime})
-family.

Moreover, if
\mathcal{J}
is nice, then so are the sets mentioned in Parts (i)-(iii);
if
\mathcal{J}
is
consistent/independent, then so are the sets mentioned in Parts (i)-(iv);
if
\mathcal{J}
is pure, then so are the sets mentioned in Parts (i), (ii), (iv), and so is the set mentioned in Part (iii) when
v=\mathbb{0}
.

Proposition B.11

(Proposition C.8 of
[
20
]
)
.

Let
d,k,r\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
p
be a prime,
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a quadratic form and
\Omega\subseteq(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}
be a consistent
M
-set. Suppose that
\Omega=\cap_{i=1}^{r}V(F_{i})
for some consistent
(M,k)
-family
\{F_{1},\dots,F_{r}\}
.
Let
1\leq k^{\prime}\leq k
.
Suppose that
\text{rank}(M)\geq 2r+1
and
p\gg_{k,r}1
.

(1)

The dimension of all independent
M
-representations of
\Omega
equals to
r_{M}(\Omega)
.

(2)

We have that
r_{M}(\Omega)\leq r
, and that
r_{M}(\Omega)=r
if
the
(M,k)
-family
\{F_{1},\dots,F_{r}\}
is independent.

(3)

For all
I\subseteq\{1,\dots,r\}
, the set
\Omega^{\prime}:=\cap_{i\in I}V(F_{i})
is a consistent
M
-set such that
r_{M}(\Omega^{\prime})\leq r_{M}(\Omega)
. Moreover, if the
(M,k)
-family
\{F_{1},\dots,F_{r}\}
is independent, then
r_{M}(\Omega^{\prime})=|I|
.

(4)

For any bijective
d
-integral linear transformation
L\colon(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}\to(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}
and
v\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}
, we have that
L(\Omega)+v
is a consistent
M
-set and that
r_{M}(L(\Omega)+v)=r_{M}(\Omega)
.

(5)

Assume that
\Omega
admits a standard
M
-representation with dimension vector
(r_{1},\dots,

r_{k})
, Then for all
1\leq k^{\prime}\leq k
, the set

\{(n_{1},\dots,n_{k+k^{\prime}})\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k+k^{\prime}}\colon(n_{1},\dots,n_{k}),(n_{1},\dots,n_{k-k^{\prime}},n_{k+1},\dots,n_{k+k^{\prime}})\in\Omega\}

admits a standard
M
-representation with dimension vector
(r_{1},\dots,r_{k^{\prime}},r_{k+1},\dots,r_{k^{\prime}})
.

(6)

Assume that
\Omega
admits a standard
M
-representation with dimension vector
(r_{1},\dots,

r_{k})
.
For
I=\{1,\dots,k^{\prime}\}
for some
1\leq k^{\prime}\leq k
, the
I
-projection
\Omega_{I}
of
\Omega

admits a standard
M
-representation with dimension vector
(r_{1},\dots,r_{k^{\prime}})
.

(7)

If
\Omega
is a nice and consistent
M
-set or a pure and consistent
M
-set, then
r_{M}(\Omega)\leq\binom{k+1}{2}
.

B.4.
Fubini’s theorem for
M
-sets

Let
n_{i}=(n_{i,1},\dots,n_{i,d})\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
denote a
d
-dimensional variable for
1\leq i\leq k
. For convenience we denote
\mathbb{F}^{d}_{p}[n_{1},\dots,n_{k}]
to be the polynomial ring
\mathbb{F}_{p}[n_{1,1},\dots,n_{1,d},\dots,n_{k,1},\dots,n_{k,d}]
.
Let
J,J^{\prime},J^{\prime\prime}
be finitely generated ideals of the polynomial ring
\mathbb{F}^{d}_{p}[n_{1},\dots,n_{k}]
and
I\subseteq\{n_{1},\dots,n_{k}\}
.
Suppose that
J=J^{\prime}+J^{\prime\prime}
,
J^{\prime}\cap J^{\prime\prime}=\{0\}
, all the polynomials in
J^{\prime}
are independent of
\{n_{1},\dots,n_{k}\}\backslash I
, and all the non-zero polynomials in
J^{\prime\prime}
depend nontrivially on
\{n_{1},\dots,n_{k}\}\backslash I
. Then we say that
J^{\prime}
is an
I
-projection
of
J
and that
(J^{\prime},J^{\prime\prime})
is an
I
-decomposition
of
J
.
It was proved in Proposition C.1 of
[
20
]
that the
I
-projection of
J
exists and is unique.

Let
\mathcal{J}
,
\mathcal{J}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{J}^{\prime\prime}
be finite subsets of
\mathbb{F}^{d}_{p}[n_{1},\dots,n_{k}]
, and
I\subseteq\{n_{1},\dots,n_{k}\}
.
Let
J,J^{\prime}
, and
J^{\prime\prime}
be the ideals generated by
\mathcal{J}
,
\mathcal{J}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{J}^{\prime\prime}
respectively. If
J^{\prime}
is an
I
-projection of
J
with
(J^{\prime},J^{\prime\prime})
being an
I
-decomposition of
J
, then we say that
\mathcal{J}^{\prime}
is an
I
-projection
of
\mathcal{J}
and that
(\mathcal{J}^{\prime},\mathcal{J}^{\prime\prime})
is an
I
-decomposition
of
\mathcal{J}
.
Note that the
I
-projection
\mathcal{J}^{\prime}
of
\mathcal{J}
is not unique. However, by Proposition C.1 of
[
20
]
, the ideal generated by
\mathcal{J}^{\prime}
and the set of zeros
V(\mathcal{J}^{\prime})
are unique.

If
I
is a subset of
\{1,\dots,k\}
, for convenience we say that
J^{\prime}
is an
I
-projection
of
J
if
J^{\prime}
is an
\{n_{i}\colon i\in I\}
-projection of
J
. Similarly, we say that
(J^{\prime},J^{\prime\prime})
is an
I
-decomposition
of
J
if
(J^{\prime},J^{\prime\prime})
is an
\{n_{i}\colon i\in I\}
-decomposition of
J
. Here
J,J^{\prime},J^{\prime\prime}
are either ideals or finite subsets of the polynomial ring.

We now define the projections of
M
-sets.
Let
\mathcal{J}\subseteq\mathbb{F}_{p}[n_{1},\dots,n_{k}]
be a consistent
(M,k)
-family and
let
\Omega=V(\mathcal{J})\subseteq(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}
.
Let
I\cup J
be a partition of
\{1,\dots,k\}
(where
I
and
J
are non-empty), and
(\mathcal{J}_{I},\mathcal{J}^{\prime}_{I})
be an
I
-decomposition of
\mathcal{J}
.
Let
\Omega_{I}
denote the set of
(n_{i})_{i\in I}\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{|I|}
such that
f(n_{1},\dots,n_{k})=0
for all
f\in\mathcal{J}_{I}
and
(n_{j})_{j\in J}\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{|J|}
.
Note that all
f\in\mathcal{J}_{I}
are independent of
(n_{j})_{j\in J}
, and that
\Omega_{I}
is independent of the choice of the
I
-decomposition.
We say that
\Omega_{I}
is an
I
-projection
of
\Omega
.

For
(n_{i})_{i\in I}\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{|I|}
, let
\Omega_{I}((n_{i})_{i\in I})
denote the set of
(n_{j})_{j\in J}\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{|J|}
such that
f(n_{1},\dots,n_{k})=0
for all
f\in\mathcal{J}^{\prime}_{I}
. By construction, for any
(n_{i})_{i\in I}\in\Omega_{I}
, we have that
(n_{j})_{j\in J}\in\Omega_{I}((n_{i})_{i\in I})
if and only if
(n_{1},\dots,n_{k})\in\Omega
. So for all
(n_{i})_{i\in I}\in\Omega_{I}
,
\Omega_{I}((n_{i})_{i\in I})
is independent of the choice of the
I
-decomposition.

Theorem B.12

(Theorem C.3 of
[
20
]
)
.

Let
d,k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
r_{1},\dots,r_{k}\in\mathbb{N}
and
p
be a prime. Set
r:=r_{1}+\dots+r_{k}
.
Let
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a quadratic form with
\text{rank}(M)\geq 2r+1
, and
\Omega\subseteq(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}
be a consistent
M
-set admitting a standard
M
-representation of
\Omega
with dimension vector
(r_{1},\dots,r_{k})
.

(1)

We have
|\Omega|=p^{dk-r}(1+O_{k,r}(p^{-1/2}))
;

(2)

If
I=\{1,\dots,k^{\prime}\}
for some
1\leq k^{\prime}\leq k-1
, then

\Omega_{I}
is a consistent
M
-set admitting a standard
M
-representation with dimension vector
(r_{1},\dots,r_{k^{\prime}})
. Moreover,
for all but at most
(k-k^{\prime})r^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}p^{dk^{\prime}+r^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}-1-\text{rank}(M)}
many
(n_{i})_{i\in I}\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k^{\prime}}
, we have that
\Omega_{I}((n_{i})_{i\in I})
has a standard
M
-representation with dimension vector
(r_{k^{\prime}+1},\dots,r_{k})
and that
|\Omega_{I}((n_{i})_{i\in I})|=p^{d(k-k^{\prime})-(r_{k^{\prime}+1}+\dots+r_{k})}(1+O_{k,r}(p^{-1/2}))
, where
r^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}:=\max_{k^{\prime}+1\leq i\leq k}r_{i}
.

(3)

If
k\geq 2
, and
I\cup J
is a partition of
\{1,\dots,k\}
(where
I
and
J
are non-empty), then for any function
f\colon\Omega\to\mathbb{C}
with norm bounded by 1, we have that

\mathbb{E}_{(n_{1},\dots,n_{k})\in\Omega}f(n_{1},\dots,n_{k})=\mathbb{E}_{(n_{i})_{i\in I}\in\Omega_{I}}\mathbb{E}_{(n_{j})_{j\in J}\in\Omega_{I}((n_{i})_{i\in I})}f(n_{1},\dots,n_{k})+O_{k,r}(p^{-1/2}).

B.5.
Intrinsic definitions for polynomials

Proposition B.13

(A special case of Proposition 7.12 of
[
20
]
)
.

Let
d,s\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
p\gg_{d}1
be a prime, and
M\colon\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to\mathbb{Z}/p
be a quadratic form associated with the matrix
A
of
p
-rank at least
s^{2}+s+3
.
Denote
M^{\prime}\colon\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to\mathbb{Z}/p
,
M^{\prime}(n):=\frac{1}{p}((nA)\cdot n)
.
Then for any
g\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to\mathbb{Q})
of degree at most
s
taking values in
\mathbb{Q}
, the following are equivalent:

(1)

\Delta_{h_{s}}\dots\Delta_{h_{1}}g(n)\in\frac{1}{q}\mathbb{Z}
for some
q\in\mathbb{N},p\nmid q
for all
(n,h_{1},\dots,h_{s})\in\Box_{p,s}(V_{p}(M))
;

(2)

we have

g=\frac{1}{q}g_{1}+g_{2}

for some
q\in\mathbb{N},p\nmid q
,
some
g_{1}\in\text{poly}(V_{p}(M)\to\mathbb{R}|\mathbb{Z})
of degree at most
s
and some
g_{2}\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to\mathbb{R})
of degree at most
s-1
;

(3)

we have

g=\frac{1}{q}g_{1}+g_{2}

for some
q\in\mathbb{N},p\nmid q
,
some
g_{1}\in\text{poly}(V_{p}(M^{\prime})\to\mathbb{R}|\mathbb{Z})
of degree at most
s
, and some
g_{2}\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to\mathbb{R})
of degree at most
s-1
.

B.6.
An irreducible property for
M
-sets

Let
k\in\mathbb{N}_{+},s\in\mathbb{N},\delta>0
and
p
be a prime.
We say that a
p
-periodic subset
\Omega
of
\mathbb{Z}^{k}
is
strongly
(\delta,p)
-irreducible up to degree
s
if for all
P\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{k}\to\mathbb{Z}/p^{s})
of degree at most
s
, either

|V_{p}(P)\cap\Omega\cap[p]^{d}|\leq\delta|\Omega\cap[p]^{d}|
or
\Omega\subseteq V_{p}(P).

Theorem B.14

(A special case of Theorem 9.14 of
[
20
]
)
.

Let
d,K\in\mathbb{N}_{+},s\in\mathbb{N}
,
p
be a prime, and
\delta>0
. Let
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a non-degenerate quadratic form and
\Omega\subseteq(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{K}
be a nice and consistent
M
-set. If
d\geq\max\{2r_{M}(\Omega)+1,4K-1\}
and
p\gg_{d,s}\delta^{-O_{d,s}(1)}
, then

\iota^{-1}(\Omega)
is strongly
(\delta,p)
-irreducible up to degree
s
.

B.7.
Leibman dichotomy for
M
-sets

Let
d\in\mathbb{N}_{+},s\in\mathbb{N}
,
C,\delta,K>0
,
p
be a prime, and
\Omega
be a non-empty subset of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
. We say that
\Omega
admits a
partially periodic
(\delta,K)
-Leibman dichotomy up to step
s
and complexity
C

if for any
\mathbb{N}
-filtered nilmanifold
G/\Gamma
of degree at most
s
and complexity at most
C
, and any
g(n)\in\text{poly}_{p}(\Omega\to G_{\mathbb{N}}|\Gamma)
, either
(g(n)\Gamma)_{n\in\Omega}
is
\delta
-equidistributed on
G/\Gamma
, or there exists a nontrivial type-I horizontal character
\eta
with
0<\|\eta\|\leq K
such that
\eta\circ g|_{\Omega}\colon\Omega\to\mathbb{R}
is a constant mod
\mathbb{Z}
.

Theorem B.15

(Theorem 10.6 of
[
20
]
)
.

Let
d,k\in\mathbb{N}_{+},s,r\in\mathbb{N}
with
d\geq\max\{4r+1,4k+3,2k+s+11\}
,
C>0
and
p
be a prime.
There exists
K:=O_{C,d}(1)
such that for any non-degenerate quadratic form
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
, any nice and consistent
M
-set
\Omega\subseteq(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}
of total co-dimension
r
, and any
0<\delta<1/2
, if
p\gg_{C,d}\delta^{-O_{C,d}(1)}
,
then
\Omega
admits a partially periodic
(\delta,K\delta^{-K})
-Leibman dichotomy up to step
s
and complexity
C
.
